# Open Threads

## How to read this file

**This file is a rolling window. It holds the global thread list, the whole of the volume currently being written, and nothing older, and everything older is in `state/archive/open-threads-volumes-01-12.md`, verbatim, with every thread number exactly as it was here.** The window moved for Volumes 01 to 06 during the Volume 07 Batch 0004 review, for Volumes 01 to 07 at the Volume 07 close, and for Volumes 01 to 08 at the Volume 08 close, **and after that last cut this file holds the global thread list and nothing else.** As of the review of Batch 0004 this file was **661 KB**; it was about 98 KB through Volume 07 and **is now about 20 KB, and it stays about that size for as long as the window is maintained.** The rule that makes the move safe is the same one the other three ledgers use and it is worth restating: **threads are never renumbered, nothing is deleted, only moved, and a volume is never archived while a close is reading it.**

**Read this guide, then the section for the volume you are writing. For the Volume 09 first prompt the useful reading is this guide and the seventeen threads under *Threads the Volume 08 close hands to Volume 09*, which are in `state/archive/open-threads-volumes-01-12.md` and which are the successor's whole instruction, with `outline/volumes/volume-08-close.md` §7 behind them when a thread demands the reasoning.** **The live window is empty and opens on Volume 09; every section above that, covering the initial threads, the outline-phase answers, and Volumes 01 to 07, is in `state/archive/open-threads-volumes-01-07.md` under the same numbers and is reachable by grep, not by reading.**

**The window was moved for Volumes 01 to 07 at the Volume 07 close and for Volumes 01 to 08 at the Volume 08 close, in each case after the close had audited the whole volume, and every section through the Volume 08 close is in the archive under its own numbers. A Volume 09 writer needs this guide and the Volume 08 close's seventeen threads and nothing older, and needs `state/archive/open-threads-volumes-01-12.md` whenever a thread demands its own chapter.**

**The same warning as in `state/continuity.md` applies. Do not trust a line-number pointer into a chapter file. Refer to a passage by its chapter and its opening words.**

## Initial story threads

1. **The cracked toll bell:** Why does the Bellweather anchor respond to Aren, and what does its first crossing cost him? The local mystery pays off in Volume 1 — a damaged anchor that still remembers refusing a severance writ, a permanent roadmark, the remembered pitch of one verse, nine lost days, and three days of fever. The bell's provenance and its two-pence purchase stay live for the whole series, because at Chapter 900 it is silent under his hand.
2. **The living Bellweather path:** Bellweather-Living has its own people, debt system, and refusal to be restored by the main world. Established in Volume 1 through the guild, the toll houses, and the Hushwater ferry cooperative, and carried forward as a recurring relationship rather than a one-volume exhibit.
3. **The official Sundering account:** Who authorized the cull, what did Aren actually know, and why was his disgrace manufactured? Edric's name is put on paper in Volume 1's midpoint, by the toll houses rather than by the guild. The full Council context, the post-Sundering inquiry, and the cost of Aren's own silence are spread through Volumes 2, 6, 11, and 18.
4. **The Gatehouse's consent rule:** The archive was built to prevent forced historical selection, but the Council altered it. The First Witness is revealed in Volumes 11–15; Volume 01 may only show its *effect* — a private-keeper clause being applied to Aren in a provincial letter — and must not name the mechanism.
5. **Ilyra Kest's crossing:** Aren’s mother used the Gatehouse to carry him from a sealed path. Her route is answered by the final volume; her exact survival is intentionally unknown and is untouched in Volume 01 except as the verse whose pitch he loses.
6. **Aren and Mara:** Their relationship begins as evidence custody versus intrusion. Volume 1 stages it as a fight over a filing system, with her winning the terms of his only evidence. It develops through disagreement and changed decisions, never instant attraction or dependence.
7. **Aren's command flaw:** Volume 1 stages his first failure of the pattern inside the path (Chapters 6 and 41–47): a solo Bind that puts the force into the pier and hurts a man, because he is the best reader of the load path in the room. Volume 1's smaller version is volunteering for a cost nobody asked him to pay, in Chapter 7.
8. **Roads without Aren:** Established in Volume 1 by the guild bench, the path's stewards, and the refusal to be grateful. The limited channel that opens at the volume's close is run by a Merefen steward and a Bellweather-Living steward with one independent witness each; Aren witnesses and translates. Volume 18 can therefore remove him without removing the network.
9. **The named dead:** Ferris Oat, Hanna Vey, Corin Slate, Bess Alder, Anwen Rusk, and Bryn Rusk exist as records, not as figures. They are the reason the case has a moral weight the Crown cannot dismiss. Jos Rusk, who survives and claims for them, is a different person and must stay distinct from Pei Tarrow; both first appear in Batch 0002, never together.
10. **Hushwater's silence:** The drowned hamlet's name in older records hints that the Bellweather-Living path was known to the archive system before the cull. Volume 01 gives the hint its physical form — Hushwater Reach, a living hamlet on a channel that is drowned in the main world, with a crossing book in a living hand — and explains nothing. The cooperative counts hands and hearths, not souls, so its nearest estimate is about **sixty** and nobody can be more definite; the erased village of the same name is **eighty**, and the two figures must never be printed together or compared on the page. This stays a hint until Volume 09.

## Answers to the questions the outline phase had to settle

- **What exactly is Aren doing, and for whom, in the first three chapters before any crossing occurs?** He is holding a road at flood stage for a town that cannot pay him properly, in the cheapest labor available, and he is doing it because it is the only problem he is allowed to have. The first three chapters are one working day argued over by four parties with money at stake, his own bond, and the bell he should have sold.
- **What makes him ring the bell on the anniversary storm rather than any other night?** Three reasons, all concrete: the storm is the only night the river is loud enough to cover the sound of a working; the town's custom puts him among the ferrymen ringing for the drowned, so one man with a bar is nobody's story; and the Archive's deadline gives him a night he privately resents. The cracked bell is the hinge of a refusal made in a storm on that date, and it answers the same weather. **Prose note:** the Archive deadline is now the **second of Greenmonth**, the province's renewal date, and the nineteenth is the human date. Both deadlines point at the same week and only the first one matters to a filing system.
- **How does Mara first become involved, given that she works above a salt merchant and Aren works a bridge?** She needs a sworn tradesman's account of what the bell is attached to, because the Archive cannot record an anchor on a clerk's word, and the bell is the only surviving object she can put in front of a magistrate. She finds him through the Old Toll Bridge's parish repair marks, by asking the ferrymen who signed the sill. She offers custody and a claim number in exchange for a promise not to ring it — undated, and he agrees to the whole of it. He does not tell her the bell rings by itself, and Chapter 4 is therefore a broken promise rather than a technicality.
- **Which of the seven villages gives Volume 01 its most immediate human stake?** **Hushwater.** It is the only one with a living claimant in Merefen, and its crossing book is the document that can be checked against a ferryman's memory. Jos Rusk is the first claim Mara personally refutes, in Batch 0002, and he is wrong about the cause and does not want to be argued with. Alder Cross carries the second stake, through Bess Alder's parish list, which is what splits the Crown's four thousand back into two hundred dead who can be named. Volume 01 keeps the two figures on separate pages and never prints a combined total.

## Final-volume questions

- Can the Gatehouse be opened to thousands without making one person's memory the lock?
- Can path communities share roads while retaining the right to refuse contact?
- Will Aren surrender the private power that makes him useful?

Chapters 1–10 are written and carded in `outline/batches/volume-01-batch-0001.md`. **Chapters 1–40 are written.** Chapters 41–50 remain planned only, at outline-block level, and are carded in `workspace/volume-01/batch-0005/PROMPT.md`. ## The window, at the Volume 13 close

**The window moved forward for Volumes 01 to 12 at the Volume 13 close, and it is the first move in this project that TRIMS the live file as well as copying it.** Every one of the five earlier moves, at the Volume 07, 08, 09, 11 and 12 closes, took an exact copy into `state/archive/` and then left the live file whole, and the live file grew back from the copy point every time. **The consequence of that convention, measured, is this: at the moment this cut was taken the five ledgers stood at 3.06 MB together, `state/continuity.md` at 984 KB holding every volume from Volume 09 forward, `state/open-threads.md` at 764 KB, `state/chapter-summaries.md` at 560 KB, `state/batch-summaries.md` at 468 KB, and `state/current.md` at 246 KB holding ten overlapping "What is true at the close of Chapter N" blocks for Chapter 560 through Chapter 650. That is roughly 780,000 tokens of state for a project whose live instruction is about 25,000 words, and Volume 14 cannot load it. The review of Volume 13 Batch 0005 recorded the arithmetic and called it a blocker, and it is one, and the fix is this cut.**

**Nothing is lost and nothing is renumbered.** `state/archive/open-threads-volumes-01-12.md` is a byte-for-byte copy of this file as it stood immediately before this cut, so the entire pre-cut ledger, guides and all, is recoverable by concatenating the archive back onto the live file at the cut point, and item 412 and thread 412 and chapter 412 and batch 412 all mean exactly what they meant. **The cut moved whole sections only, at section boundaries, and it did not touch a single item number, thread number, chapter number or batch number.** What left the live file is: **`## The window, at the Volume 08 close` and every thread section for Volumes 09, 10, 11 and 12, ending with *Threads the Volume 12 close hands to Volume 13*.**

**What stayed live, and rule 4 of `state/archive/README.md` is why.** `## Hard canon`, `## Ending constraints` and `## Knowledge control` are global and are in this file now whatever their byte offset, and so are the two canon-clarification sections and `## Initial story threads`, `## Answers to the questions the outline phase had to settle`, `## Final-volume questions` and `## Planning status` in the thread ledger. They are in the archive as well, because the archive is a copy, and a global section in both places is harmless and a global section in neither is not.

**The rule this cut changes, stated so that nobody inherits it by accident.** The old convention was *copy, and leave the live file whole*, and its stated justification was that a copy is safer than a deletion. **That is true and it is also what made the files unusable, and both halves cannot stand.** The new convention is *copy first, verify the copy, then trim the live file*, and the verification is one command: the archive must be byte-identical to the pre-cut live file, which is checked here against git rather than by reading. **The window still does not move in the middle of a volume.** It moved for Volumes 01 to 07 at the Volume 07 close, 01 to 08 at the Volume 08 close, 01 to 09 at the Volume 09 close, 01 to 10 at the Volume 11 close, 01 to 11 at the Volume 12 close, and 01 to 12 at this one, and it will not move again until Volume 14 is closed.

**What a Volume 14 writer reads, in this order, and does not read the rest of:** this guide, `state/current.md`, `outline/volumes/volume-13-close.md` in whole, and the threads this close hands on. Everything older is reachable by grep and is not to be read. **The highest item in this file is the newest, and `state/current.md` carries the figure.**

## Threads Volume 13 Batch 0001 carries to Volume 13 Batch 0002

**This section is written by the phase that wrote Volume 13 Batch 0001 and wrote its block, and it is Batch 0002's whole instruction. `outline/volume-13.md` is the plan the block was written against and is binding, `state/continuity.md` items 1440 to 1451 are the facts, and item 442 is the list of what Batch 0002 must not do. Every figure was counted on the chapter files as they stand after this block's last edit and the basis is named. Nothing above is renumbered and nothing above is resolved. Fourteen threads, numbered from 429, and the last is what the successor must not do. This section names the antagonist nowhere, prints no third of the barred kin-term, prints the word that names the founding paper nowhere, prints no System panel and no stage word, and contains no chapter of new prose.**

429. **The frame Batch 0002 inherits, and the two numbers it must derive rather than copy.** Day 930, the twenty-fifth of Mudmonth YR 318, a Sunday, is the only anchor, on the carried thirty-day month. **Block 0002 is days 1873 to 1914, forty-two inclusive, that is the eleventh of Frostmonth YR 320 to the twenty-second of Rainmonth YR 320, and the ten chapter days are the successor's to choose inside that range, derived from the day number and not read off a card or off this paragraph.** **Every date in Block 0002 is YR 320, because the year does not turn until day 1956, which is the last day of Block 0003.** The month starts inside Block 0002 are 1896 and 1926, and **1926 is outside it.** Market days are the Saturdays and the Thursdays, and **Block 0001's five were 1837, 1846, 1851, 1858 and 1872, and the block's twelve are derivable and are not printed here, because a printed day list is a certificate and a certificate is the last place a defect survives.** Derive the weekday half and the day-of-month half separately and report them as two figures and never as one, and a market-morning claim is a third. `state/continuity.md` 1440, 1450.

430. **The forged sheet, in full, because Batch 0002 inherits an event and not a document.** It arrived on 1831 in a band of about a hundred and four that a carrier lost two days bringing at a ford, on a Friday the room was shut, and the woman of about twenty-nine opened the room to take the band. **Four hands of writing, no seal, nothing on the back, a name at the top in the place where a name goes on a thing that is going to be acted on, and the name is Aren Kest.** It stands a keeper down for nine days and shuts a road for the same nine days, the matter being under question. **The road is the ferry stone to the ninth milestone with a dry ford between.** It says neither by whom nor about what. **Batch 0002 may not produce a second sheet, may not name a writer, may not state a price for the nine days in any figure, and may not have any character say out loud that the sheet was written by a person with an office.** `state/continuity.md` 1441, 1446.

431. **What the sheet did, and the one thing about it that is worse than the thing it was.** It was cut to about nine lines, printed in a district hand, posted on a Friday and read out at the district end of the ferry stone by a gatekeeper with about eleven years at that gate, **who did not check the name, for the reason the page gives.** The keeper of the road, a woman of about fifty who has kept it nineteen years, was told at the gate, was asked nothing, was shown nothing, and went home for nine days. **The nine days ran from 1861 to 1870 and the whole of what they cost is a sentence she came four days of carting to say, and it is not the nine days: for nine days nobody in about nine houses at the top end and about nine at the bottom knew whether she was stood down or had gone home for a bad week.** She keeps the road's count on a slate in her apron, in nineteen columns, with nine days taken out of the last one in a hand that is not hers, **and the slate is hers and is not for the drawer and is not in it and was not taken out of her apron by anybody.** `state/continuity.md` 1447.

432. **The three bare leaves, and there is nothing else in that room to put a thing on, and the copyist has now said so twice.** The two true things the man of about fifty-eight with a badge said on 1837, one of them **a thing with a name at the top of it is not false because the man in the room thinks it is false.** The figure **seven**, on 1851. The seven words **the man with the badge said it**, on 1861. **The register stands at seven entries, all questions, no findings. The day-book stands at nine. The press stands open with no date. The Crown's form has three heads filled and the fourth ruled and empty, and it is about seven months empty and nobody has filled it and nobody may fill it. The half leaf lies face down and nobody turns it over.** The copyist of about twenty-six has said once that she would put her hand on which of the seven is the gap inside an hour and **will not, because the three series are not derived from one another and making one out of another for a man with a page of seven lines hands nine counties a single number anybody can check.** `state/continuity.md` 1442, 1443, 1450.

433. **The seven, and the two that are not on the list, and the second road's own count.** **The ferry city, the county where a list of names was burned, the waterworks country, the coast of returning roads, the fruit valley, the glass country on the high flats, and the small one**, named in that order in `chapter-0605.md` with no figure and no distance. Not on the list: **the city under the light, which refuses, and the valley nobody can reach.** He answered for the ferry city and the fruit valley and said so out loud, and gave the reason — those two have a person in them who can refuse him — and **three have a person he can write to and a person he cannot, one has never had a list of itself in anything, and the fifth is the small one.** The small road keeps its count in **open crossings and shut weeks: nine open crossings and four shut weeks in two years, the longest shut week eleven days, the other two a fortnight and a week, and a boy of about eleven holds the whole of it in his head and is eleven and will have it for about nine more years and then will be a man of twenty who was asked a question by a stranger and will not remember it as a count.** **The conversion between that count and main-world days is named once in Block 0001 and made once and is never to be made again.** `state/continuity.md` 1443, 1445.

434. **The small road, its people, and the want, in full, because a want cannot be inferred from a name.** **Nell Craddock, 40, runs a lime kiln alone on a slope and is called that by about nine households and by nobody else.** Nine open crossings in two years, four shut weeks, both ends of the road about four days of carting from a market town of about two thousand, and **about a mile and a half of it is not made and the mile and a half is the whole difficulty.** It is passable on the days when two people turn up at the two ends, and on the days when one of them does not turn up it is not a road. A woman of about sixty has held the far end for about thirty-one years and has never once been asked what the road is. A man who keeps a lane four days below has walked up once in the last year. **Her want is that her road be written down as a road, with a figure on it, so that shutting it is something somebody has to do in daylight. Her reason for not supplying the figure is on the page and is not modesty: the first number anybody says out loud about a place is the number that place has for the rest of its life, it is said by whoever says it first, it is not chosen by anybody afterwards, and she is the one who walks it, and about four houses owe her for a season and about five do not.** She came again on the tenth open crossing, on 1872, and asked a man of thirty-two with a limp **do you want it**, twice, and he did not answer. `state/continuity.md` 1448.

435. **The man of about forty-seven and his page, and what he is and is not.** **Garvin Slade, 47, gave his age when asked and gave no trade, and came on foot on a market morning and asked which days the room was shut and was told Fridays and no others and believed it.** The page has seven lines on it, six marked, and **the seventh has nothing against it and is not struck out, on the stated ground that a struck-out line can be read as an opinion and a line with nothing against it is a line that has not been weighed.** He is right about the strain and right twice out loud, and he is wrong about one thing only, which is that the seventh is light. **He said the block's truest sentence: a road with a figure on it is a weight; a road with nothing on it is not a light thing, it is a gap, and a gap is not carried by anybody, and a gap goes first, and I have never once in my life had to be cruel to close a gap.** He asked for six marks and got none, said he had expected nothing, and said he would come back on the first of a month. He did not leave the page on the table. **He does not say whose plan anything is and no chapter of this volume may have him say.** `state/continuity.md` 1442.

436. **The culvert, and it is the same paper at a small scale, and the man of about fifty who laid the course said he has not read the instruction.** At the gate of the market town, a cart's off-side wheel went in on the Thursday of 1846 and put a hand's depth of silt across the mouth; a boy of about seventeen fetched the man with the limp and he cleared it from the second hour to the ninth with about nine barrows and a hook. **Under the weed, three courses of sill laid in lime with the middle course gone between two clean joints, and a new course laid dry across the bed, and men from the district put it there three weeks before on an instruction on a sheet with a name at the top of it.** The mason said *we were told to keep the water off the road and we did, and I am not going to be told about the bed of it by a man with a stick at the fifth hour of a market morning.* **The only number taken off that street all day was the number of barrows, written by the man of about thirty-four with a sheet without being asked, and the number went back down the lane in his coat and nobody said whose work it was.** `state/continuity.md` 1441.

437. **The three series and the number of the band, and the standing rule that the volume turned them into an engine without merging them.** **About nine hundred sheets a month out into nine counties, about a hundred and four in one band on a fixed day, about four hundred and nine parish rolls a month in off the roads, and not one of the three is derived from another.** Two contradictory warnings about the same four miles of channel were in two different series and the room was forbidden from noticing. **The number of the band may not be changed and no chapter may derive one series from another or add them.** `state/continuity.md` 1442, 1450.

438. **The relationship, and the block plants the separation without repairing anything.** **The breach is open and this volume's resolution is a separation, not a repair.** In `chapter-0603.md` the woman of about thirty-four who keeps the key says out loud, once, unasked and to the copyist rather than to the woman of about twenty-nine, that **Aren Kest and the keeper of the room were in the lane together on Saturday morning and were not in the lane together on Thursday morning, the hearing stood on both, about nine people came on each, and nobody has said why, and she is not asking.** In `chapter-0605.md` the same woman says, once, that **in four and a half years nobody in that lane has ever asked her which roads are open, and that she is noticing that now and is not asking anybody anything.** **The four and a half years were not referred to as four and a half years about the two of them in any of the ten chapters, the two of them were not in one room with a third person in it, and nothing between them was described in a document.** The barred kin-term is at **two** across six hundred and ten chapters, is spent, and this block prints no third of it, **and the word *brother* is printed nowhere in this block about anything, and the man of about thirty-four with a sheet and the man of about thirty-eight with a satchel are two men with an open breach of which this block says nothing at all.**

439. **The four that did not move, and the whole of the list, because a new volume is where this project loses facts.** The fourth head is empty and unfilled and unruled-over. The half leaf is face down and unturned and unscheduled and nobody has said what is on the other side and no interval is printed for it. The floor of the room was washed in Greenmonth YR 320 by somebody nobody knows and nobody was thanked and no interval is printed. The hearing stands with no bench in it. The shut road four hundred miles off is not reopened, is not scheduled, nobody was sent, and **the word *failed* is used of nothing in this volume.** The valley of about four hundred people is unreachable and unscheduled. About eleven hundred people are on no roll and no sheet and no list. The city on a shelf is not on a page and its three protected numerals are at zero for the whole volume. The coast with the twenty-two notches is not visited and the twenty-second is not cut. The bar of wood at the Low Gate is not lifted, not scheduled, and the four questions on its board are not asked; **Chapter 0601 may refer to the fourth head and the half leaf and Chapters 0605, 0607 and 0608 may refer to the hearing and none of them may turn a leaf, fill a head, lift a bar or open a gate.** The warm left palm is warm once in this block, at about the fourth hour, in a hand in the silt up to the wrist in `chapter-0604.md`, and **he does not look at it, nobody notices it, nobody is told, and it may not be looked at, explained or mentioned again by another character.** The notebook's separate leaf with a seventh line that says *a person* is not looked at. The mechanism is not named. The two readings of a man who published his own mistake are not chosen between, in any chapter, and the two sets of about four hundred do not overlap. **Nobody is thanked in any of the ten chapters, in this file, or in the state files written by this block, and every occurrence of the thank family in the block is a negation or is about four houses owing a lime bill.** `state/continuity.md` 1450, 1451.

440. **The name budget with the arithmetic, and what is left.** The budget for Volume 13 is five. **Batch 0001 spent two, `Nell Craddock` 40 and `Garvin Slade` 47, in `chapter-0606.md` and `chapter-0607.md`, and the arithmetic is 2 of 5 with three slots unspent, and Blocks 0002, 0003, 0004 and 0005 have to live inside those three.** A capitalised two-word sweep over the ten new files returns those two and nothing else. `Aren Kest` is at 45 across the ten and in every chapter, and a zero is a failure and not a style. `Mara Vey` is at **0** and stays at 0; the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps the room is never named in prose. `Sabra Rolf` was spent at an earlier volume and `Gallow` at `chapters/volume-12/chapter-0552.md`, and neither may be used for a new first name. `state/continuity.md` 1451.

441. **The measurements, recorded because they are facts about the world and not because they are targets, and there is no target in this thread for any of them.** **Block 0001 is 20,333 in ten chapters**, counted file by file with the title line included and the ten added, per chapter **1,981 / 1,742 / 1,753 / 2,154 / 2,144 / 2,106 / 2,268 / 1,922 / 2,228 / 2,035**, and the scene bodies **1,603 / 1,362 / 1,365 / 1,739 / 1,744 / 1,703 / 1,871 / 1,515 / 1,816 / 1,609**. The manuscript is 1,702,034 in six hundred and ten chapters. `about` is **519 in 20,333, which is 25.53 per 1,000**, on the declared whole-word-over-whitespace basis with a case-insensitive numerator. `the whole of` 22, `about four people` 7. `---` rules 0. Question marks, exclamation marks, semicolons and em dashes **16, 0, 0 and 0**. **Exact duplicate paragraphs at twelve words or more, the ten new against the six hundred before and against themselves: 0 and 0.** The 8-word shingle overlap against the six hundred before is 210 in `chapter-0602.md` and every one of them is this book's own house frame — the cost coda's opening, the Crown's form with its three heads and an empty fourth, four hands of writing, the two ends of a road — **and the paragraph-level instrument, which is the one that can see a lifted sentence, returns 0.** `state/continuity.md` 1449.

442. **And what Batch 0002 must not do, in one list, and it is this thread and not a summary of the thirteen above.** **Do not name the antagonist. Do not print a third of the barred kin-term and do not print the word *brother* about anything. Do not print the word that names the founding paper in any construction, in a document, in a quotation, in a mouth or in narration. Do not print a System panel, a stage word, a ledger word or a qualification word, and do not let a chapter say the ledger does or does not move. Do not print a word about a roof and do not mention a tile on a coping. Do not print a fifth unattributed two-line statement. Do not un-say the ninth word, soften the leak, add a tenth line to the song, or have anybody regret it in a speech. Do not produce a second forged sheet, trace the first one, name a writer, price the nine days in any figure, or have anybody say out loud that it was written by a person with an office. Do not open the gate at the end of nine miles of road, send anybody to it, or re-spend the arithmetic by which it was identified. Do not fill or rule any head, turn or time the half leaf, lift or schedule the bar, re-ask the four questions on the gate's board, count the two in three, answer the rightness, name the mechanism, or look at the roadmark, the separate leaf or the palm. Do not convert the small road's open-crossing count into main-world days, and do not convert a main-world figure into one. Do not month a path mouth, weekday a path mouth or tide a path mouth. Do not city the page, notch the coast, reopen the shut road, merge the three series, resolve the three words for one lane, choose the two readings of him, repair the breach, thank anybody, or spend a name. Do not count the small road's people, and do not print a figure for a road nobody has been asked to number — **except the figure the woman of about thirty-eight asked for in Volume 12, which is a different valley and is spent.** Do not let a chapter of Batch 0002 answer the question **do you want it**, and do not have a character ask it a second time. Do not repair `chapter-0600.md`, whose cost coda prints eight figures of time over a list of nine, and do not repair any other Volume 12 prose on a hunch. **Do not create a prompt for any batch after Batch 0002, do not write a `.done` or a `.retired` marker, and do not edit `state/phase-ledger.json`, `scripts/`, `.github/`, `.opencode/agent/`, `AGENTS.md`, `PHASE_SYSTEM.md`, `REPO_PLAN.md`, `OUTLINE_GUIDE.md`, `opencode.json`, `outline/series.md` or `outline/ending.md`.** And **do not adopt a figure from any record in this repository without counting it off the page, and do not print a collision count without printing the set it was compared against, which is the whole of 427 and 428 above.**

443. **The frame Batch 0003 inherits, and the calendar, which the volume outline's own table gets wrong in five rows and which this item supersedes.** The single anchor is **day 930, the twenty-fifth of Mudmonth YR 318, a Sunday**, on the carried thirty-day month, and the chain is built forward from that and from nothing else. **Block 0003 is days 1915 to 1956 inclusive, forty-two days, that is the twentieth of Hearthmonth YR 320 to the first of Thawmonth YR 321. The year turns on the last day of this block and that day carries a chapter: day 1956 is the first of Thawmonth YR 321.** The month starts inside Block 0003 are 1926 and 1956 and both are inside it. **Derive the weekday half and the day-of-month half from the day number separately and never from one another, and treat `outline/volume-13.md` §17's printed table as a certificate and not as an authority: that table is internally inconsistent after day 1872 and it puts 1896 on a Monday, 1926 on a Saturday, 1986 on a Friday, 2016 on a Wednesday and 2040 on a Thursday, and the correct weekdays on the derived chain are different, and this pass's own Batch 0002 datelines were derived and then checked against the anchor.** The one figure from that table that a successor may take without deriving is the month-start day list, and even that must be confirmed against the anchor. **Block 0002's ten days were 1896, 1900, 1902, 1904, 1907, 1909, 1910, 1911, 1913 and 1914, 0 mismatches on either half against the anchor, five of them market days at 1900, 1902, 1907, 1909 and 1914, and two Sundays at 1896 and 1910 on which the room over the weigh-house is shut, and the first of them is the first of a month.** Market days are the Saturdays and the Thursdays. `state/continuity.md` 1452 to 1462.

444. **The page, which is the block's object and is now on the page in its finished form, and Batch 0003 inherits it as an event rather than a document.** It is a larger sheet folded in two, printed twice, with **six ruled spaces and seven lines, six of the lines marked and the seventh bare and not struck out**, and it went round nine counties. **A mark in a space is not a vote for the arithmetic: it is a statement by a person who can be named and found and asked what their figure is, and that is the only reason any of the three marks on it is worth anything.** By the end of Block 0002 the six spaces stand at **three with nothing in them, of which about four of the three are refusals and one has not been reached and the page cannot tell them apart, and three with a mark, of which the fifth is a road that agreed to stand aside on condition of a count taken first by its own people, which is not an agreement to be shut and is not on the page distinguishable from one, and one of which carries nineteen columns of a woman's own count ruled under the line in a different hand with a nine taken out of the last one in a third hand.** **The page has no way of holding a refusal, so a refusal comes back as a blank and a blank is indistinguishable from a page nobody has reached, and by the second time round that indistinguishability is permanent.** No chapter may name who wrote it, trace it, produce a second one, or say out loud that it was written by a person with an office. `state/continuity.md` 1453, 1454.

445. **What Block 0002 settled, and the four things a successor must not reopen.** **One, the room's three-series rule is now known to be a blind spot and is not going to be replaced, on the page, by a copyist of about twenty-six who is right about the rule and is right about the blind spot and is going on keeping the three series correctly anyway; the cost of that is that there is no second virtue in that room.** **Two, a page wants six marks and the mark is not the thing: the thing is a figure said out loud by a person standing on the road who can be moved off it, and the room cannot supply that figure and refuses correctly and the refusal helps nobody and the chapter says so.** **Three, the cheapest correct fix anybody in nine counties was offered in the block is a one-day delay between a room and a gate, and the delay costs a man of about thirty-four with a sheet the whole of his office, because his office is the day, and nobody thanked the man of about forty-seven for it and nobody blamed the man of about thirty-four either, and there is no head in nine counties on which to rule a delay, so the fix is worth exactly nothing and the man of about fifty-eight with a badge said that out loud in a lane.** **Four, and this is the one Block 0003 turns on: the man of about thirty-four with a sheet keeps the only record in nine counties of what that page did, on a sheet of his own with no head over it, and it goes down the lane in his coat, and a room in nine counties cannot hold it.**

446. **Aren Kest has learned his own signature is on the forged page, and has done one thing about it, and that thing was to do nothing.** The man of about thirty-four with a sheet showed him a **copy of the sheet in his own writing, one hand, made at the counter on a Thursday and carried to the district on a Friday and kept in a satchel for about a month**; at the top of the first page, in the place where a name goes on a thing that is going to be acted on, **is his own name, in a hand that is not his.** He read it twice. **He cannot say it is not his and the page gives the reason: a man who says a page is not his is making a claim, a claim has to be believed, and being believed is a thing a person is given and not a thing a person does; and about four hundred people in that market town hold one true thing about him and about four hundred hold another, the two sets do not overlap, and he said no when he was asked whether he would put the two of them straight.** The one thing he could have done was say it out loud in that market, and **he told the man with the sheet that he was not going to do it and why, and the why is on the page and it is not modesty: saying it in a lane would tell about nine strangers which of the two sets he is in, and he has not asked either set anything.** **The man of about thirty-four with a sheet then wrote on the back of the copy, in his own hand and without being asked, that the man named on the copy was shown the copy on the fourteenth of a month at about the second hour and did not say the writing was his, and that record is in a satchel and is in no book, and nobody thanked him for it.** `state/continuity.md` 1456.

447. **The woman of about fifty, the count, and the copyist's nine, which is the block's second cost and is a new fact about the manuscript's own room.** She keeps the road from the ferry stone at the low end to the ninth milestone at the top, nineteen years, about nine households at each end, a ford dry in most summers, and **her count is nineteen columns, one for each year, of the days that road was passable out of the days in that year, the last column unfinished because the year is unfinished, with a nine taken out of it in a hand that is not hers.** She read that count out loud at a gate, in the open, in front of about nine people, on the eighteenth of a month, and put a mark in the seventh space of the page in the same breath, and said out loud that **the reason was that an empty space is a road nobody is carrying and she would rather be argued with than be left alone on a page.** **She said three things that a successor may not soften: that a count does not shut anything and is only the thing anybody can be argued with; that she has offered it once, at a gate, about her own road, on a day when a page came round, and that one time is one time and nobody may cite it; and that she still does not know which way her count runs and would like somebody in the county to tell her, and nobody has.** **And the hand is hers, and the copyist of about twenty-six admitted it in a room in front of about nine people on the nineteenth of a month, having put the nine into a slate in an apron on a Tuesday in the first week of a month because she did not know which way the count ran either and the end is where a figure you do not understand goes, and she had not told the woman in about six weeks and said she is telling her now because the count has been read out loud by a man with a page and the person who put a figure into it ought to say so before anybody uses the figure.** Nobody thanked her and nobody told her she was wrong. `state/continuity.md` 1457, 1458.

448. **The relationship, and the block plants the separation twice without repairing anything, and this is now the standing shape of the volume.** The keeper of the room **did not come down to the lane on two market mornings running, and the copyist of about twenty-six said so out loud in front of about nine people both times and nobody in the room agreed with her either time, and she said once that she is not going to say it a third time.** The copyist's finding about it is on the page and it is the volume's: **nothing in that room depends on the woman who keeps it being in it, and three series would go on being kept correctly for a month with her out of the lane, and the copyist has wanted a room like that since she was about nineteen, and she is saying it in front of the woman.** **The two of them were in one room together in none of the ten chapters. He is in the room in 0612 to the fifth hour and she comes up at the seventh; he is in the lane in 0612 and 0616 and 0620 and she goes past him; he is four days off in 0613, 0615 and 0619.** The four and a half years were not referred to as four and a half years about the two of them in any of the ten chapters and **nothing between them was described in a document and no finding is under the eighth question and there is not going to be one from him.** The barred kin-term is at **two** across six hundred and twenty chapters, is spent, and this block prints no third of it, **and the word *brother* is printed nowhere in this block about anything.** `state/continuity.md` 1459.

449. **The eighth entry, and the one change this block makes to a room that had stood still for four and a half years.** **The register stands at eight entries, all of them questions, and none of them a finding. The eighth is the question a woman of about fifty asked in the first week of a month, which is whether a keeper can be told before a page about her road is read at a gate, and which the man of about fifty-eight with a badge answered in a room with the door open by saying there is no way to agree it.** The day-book stands at nine and the eighth question went into the register and not the day-book. The press stands open with no date and nothing was printed. **The fourth head of the Crown's form is still ruled and still empty and about eight months empty and nobody has filled it and nobody may rule a fifth and no chapter of Block 0003 may fill it either.** The half leaf is still face down. The drawer about eleven inches deep is still about eleven inches deep and about four hundred returns and now also holds a leaf with a question on it and a leaf with a standing of four and a half years on it and a leaf with a page described on it. **Batch 0003 may put a ninth question in that register and may not put a finding in it.** `state/continuity.md` 1460.

450. **What did not move, and the whole of the list, so that a successor does not have to find it.** The fourth head is empty, unfilled and unruled-over. The half leaf is face down, unturned, unscheduled, and no interval is printed for it. The floor of the room was washed in Greenmonth YR 320 by somebody nobody knows and nobody was thanked and no interval is printed. The hearing stands with no bench in it. The shut road four hundred miles off is not reopened, is not scheduled, nobody was sent, and **the word *failed* is used of nothing in this volume.** The valley of about four hundred people is unreachable and unscheduled. About eleven hundred people are on no roll and no sheet and no list. The city on a shelf is not on a page and its three protected numerals are at zero for the whole volume. The coast with the twenty-two notches is not visited and the twenty-second is not cut. The bar of wood at the Low Gate is not lifted, not scheduled, and the four questions on its board are not asked. **The warm left palm is not mentioned in this block at all, and no chapter may mention it, and the notebook's separate leaf with a seventh line that says *a person* is not looked at, and the mechanism is not named and no chapter says what a roadmark is.** The small road keeps its count in nine open crossings and four shut weeks in a boy of about eleven, **no chapter of this block counted its people, printed a figure for it, or converted its count into main-world days, and no chapter of Block 0003 may.** The two readings of a man who published his own mistake are not chosen between in any chapter and the two sets of about four hundred do not overlap. **Nobody was thanked in any of the ten chapters, in this file, or in the state files written by this block, and all thirty-three occurrences of the thank family in the block are negations or are a statement that a person had not said a thing in order to be thanked.** `state/continuity.md` 1461.

451. **The name budget, and the arithmetic, and why this block spent nothing.** The budget for Volume 13 is five. **Batch 0001 spent two, `Nell Craddock` 40 and `Garvin Slade` 47. Batch 0002 spent none, so the arithmetic is 2 of 5 with three slots unspent and Blocks 0003, 0004 and 0005 have to live inside those three, and a block that can be carried by two established names and about nine people described by age and trade is the reason to hold a name back while there is still a volume left to spend one in.** A capitalised two-word sweep over the ten new files returns `Aren Kest`, `The Crown` and one line-wrap artefact and nothing else. `Aren Kest` is at **43 across the ten and in every chapter**, and **1 in 0615, 1 in 0619, 3 in 0611 and 13 in 0616, and the low figures are the two chapters in which he is off the made road four days down and a name in a chapter a man is not in is a false claim about a person.** `Mara Vey` is at **0** and stays at 0 and the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps the room is never named in prose. `Garvin Slade` is at **3** in this block and is referred to as a man of about forty-seven for the rest of it, which is his practice and not a lapse. `Sabra Rolf` and `Gallow` are spent and were not used. `state/continuity.md` 1462.

THE BASIS STATED IN THIS ITEM IS CORRECT AND IS THE ONE THE SUCCESSOR PROMPT §80 ALSO STATES: the scene body is everything above the paragraph that opens the cost coda, which is the file total less the cost line less the money column paragraph, and which therefore INCLUDES the title line and the dateline. A later repair wrongly concluded this basis was unwritten; see `state/continuity.md` 1475. This item is left standing under its own number and is not to be read as a current figure. The measurements, recorded because they are facts about the world and not because they are targets, and there is no target in this thread for any of them.** **Block 0002 is 23,823 in ten chapters**, counted file by file with the title line included and the ten added, per chapter **2,757 / 2,403 / 2,324 / 2,240 / 2,189 / 2,381 / 2,206 / 2,120 / 2,402 / 2,801**, and the scene bodies, located structurally as everything above the paragraph that opens the cost coda, **2,338 / 1,993 / 1,912 / 1,818 / 1,780 / 1,954 / 1,798 / 1,698 / 1,964 / 2,347**, minimum 1,698 at `chapter-0618.md` and maximum 2,347 at `chapter-0620.md`, against Batch 0001's 1,362 to 1,871 and Volume 12's 1,161 to 2,095. **The figure a writer should copy is the body and not the total, and the two bodies above Volume 12's maximum are at 0611 and 0620, which are the two chapters that carry the block's opening object and its close, and both were trimmed once and are left where they are. The review of `next-0007` corrected the coda-share figure this item originally carried: **11.4 per cent does not reproduce under any reading, and the measured mean is 17.8 per cent over a range of 15.2 to 19.9, against Batch 0001's 19.8 over 17.5 to 22.1. The block is still scene-heavier than its predecessor and the number was wrong by six points. And the rule *share should not rise as the prose falls* was broken inside this block, because the coda runs 408 to 454 words and the share is therefore a function of body length: 0611 at a body of 2,338 is 15.2 per cent and 0618 at a body of 1,698 is 19.9 per cent.** **Volumes 01 to 11 are 1,588,852 in five hundred and fifty chapters, Volume 12 is 92,849 in fifty, Batch 0001 is 20,333 in ten, and the manuscript is 1,725,857 in six hundred and twenty chapters, and 1,588,852 + 92,849 + 20,333 + 23,823 reproduces it, and a concatenated `cat` of all six hundred and twenty files returns the same 1,725,857 and no chapter file in the manuscript is missing its trailing newline, so the per-file and concatenated bases agree.** Ten money columns at **202, 215, 229, 234, 243, 251, 262, 271, 283 and 294 pence**, ten distinct, nine to eleven items a column, every one footing on item sum, restated price list, printed total and mark conversion at forty-eight pence a mark, no duplicated price inside a column, no price above forty-one, and **no barred numeral in any total, over-figure or printed price.** **Collision: the prior set was rebuilt in this pass as the union of three independent extractors, one keyed on the item list's footing, one on the restated enumeration and one on the mark conversion, over the six hundred and ten chapters before this block, and it is 167 distinct; the ten totals collide with none of it, and the set size is printed beside the claim because a collision report without its set is not a measurement.** `about` is **656 in 23,823, which is 27.54 per 1,000**, on the declared whole-word-over-whitespace basis with a case-insensitive numerator, against 25.53 for Batch 0001 and 24.88 for Volume 12. `the whole of` 25, `about four people` 10. `---` rules 0. Question marks, exclamation marks, semicolons, em dashes and en dashes **1, 0, 0, 0 and 0**. **Exact duplicate paragraphs at twelve words or more, the ten new against the six hundred and ten before and against themselves: 0 and 0.** **The longest run of words any chapter of this block shares with the six hundred and ten before it is forty, and it occurs three times, in 0611 and 0616 only, and all three are the cost coda's own frame** — *are eight figures of time and not one of them goes in at either end of this column* and the figures either side of it. The 8-word shingle overlap against the six hundred and ten before is 152 to 411 a chapter and every one of them is this book's own house frame, **and the paragraph-level instrument, which is the one that can see a lifted sentence, returns 0.** `state/continuity.md` 1453 to 1462.

453. **And what Batch 0003 must not do, in one list, and it is this thread and not a summary of the twelve above.** **Do not name the antagonist. Do not print a third of the barred kin-term and do not print the word *brother* about anything. Do not print the word that names the founding paper in any construction, in a document, in a quotation, in a mouth or in narration. Do not print a System panel, a stage word, a ledger word or a qualification word. Do not print a word about a roof and do not mention a tile on a coping. Do not produce a second forged sheet, trace the first one, name a writer, price the nine days in any figure, or have anybody say out loud that it was written by a person with an office. Do not fill or rule any head, put a finding in the register, turn or time the half leaf, lift or schedule the bar, re-ask the four questions on the gate's board, count the two in three, answer the rightness, name the mechanism, or look at the roadmark, the separate leaf or the palm. Do not convert the small road's open-crossing count into main-world days and do not convert a main-world figure into one. Do not month a path mouth, weekday a path mouth or tide a path mouth. Do not city the page, notch the coast, reopen the shut road, merge the three series, resolve the three words for one lane, choose the two readings of him, repair the breach, thank anybody, or spend a name. Do not count the small road's people and do not print a figure for a road nobody has been asked to number. Do not let anybody ask *do you want it* a second time and do not let anybody answer it, and do not let anybody answer the question he refused to answer in 0610 by having somebody else answer it for him in a room. Do not have a character certify, refuse certification, lift the bar, or open the gate at the end of nine miles of road, and do not re-spend the arithmetic by which that gate was identified. Do not repair `chapter-0600.md`, whose cost coda prints eight figures of time over a list of nine, and do not repair any other closed volume's prose on a hunch. Do not take a weekday or a day of month off the printed table in `outline/volume-13.md` §17, which is wrong in five rows; derive off day 930. Do not let the three blanks on the page turn into three refusals in anybody's mouth, because about four of the three are refusals and one is not reached and **the page cannot tell and no chapter of this volume may resolve that.** Do not let the woman of about fifty's nineteen columns become a rule, a practice, a precedent, a series, or a form, and do not have anybody but her explain why they are not. Do not have the man of about thirty-four with a sheet stop keeping his own untitled sheet, and do not let it go into the room's book, because there is no head for it. **Do not create a prompt for any batch after Batch 0003, do not write a `.done` or a `.retired` marker, and do not edit `state/phase-ledger.json`, `scripts/`, `.github/`, `.opencode/agent/`, `AGENTS.md`, `PHASE_SYSTEM.md`, `REPO_PLAN.md`, `OUTLINE_GUIDE.md`, `opencode.json`, `outline/series.md` or `outline/ending.md`.** And **do not adopt a figure from any record in this repository without counting it off the page, and do not print a collision count without printing the set it was compared against and the three extractors that built it, which is the whole of 427 and 428 above.**

## Threads Volume 13 Batch 0002 hands to Volume 13 Batch 0003, items 454 to 464, and the last is what Batch 0003 must not do

**Appended by the verification and repair pass over Batch 0002, which found the block already written on disk, verified every instrument, found one real defect against `outline/volume-13.md` §8 Block 2 and repaired it in `chapter-0619.md`. Items 429 to 453 stand under their own numbers and item 444 has been corrected in place for the fifth mark. Canon for the repair is `state/continuity.md` 1466 to 1472.**

454. **The fifth beat is now on the page, and it is the thing Block 0003 and Block 0004 both stand on.** A woman of about forty-three who keeps a road that is not one of the six stood at the ferry stone gate on the eighteenth of a month, four days off a made road, and said that her line is the fifth, that she is not going to argue with a page whose author is not in her county, and that **her road will stand aside and that is the whole of what she came to say.** She was told the cost and gave it in a working voice: **it is not nine days, it is the rest of it, and her people knew that before she got there because they walked it with her on a Tuesday.** Then she set the condition, and the condition is the whole of the beat: **if her name goes in that space she wants the count taken first, taken by the people who walk it, on the ground, before her line is settled.** The man of about thirty-four with a sheet asked whose count and she said ours, and **what her road keeps is the crossings that are open and the weeks the road is shut, and that has never once been a number of days in anybody's mouth and it is not going to be one.** The man of about fifty-eight with a badge said he could not grant it and was told he was not being asked to, because no head in nine counties can grant it, and the road's own people are asked instead and are not in that county. **If they take it the count is theirs and is dated by whoever walked it and not by a gate. If they will not, her line stays blank, and a blank is a thing a page cannot read.**

455. **The distinction the chapter makes, which is the volume's argument and which only one person is permitted to make.** The woman of about fifty, who has asked for nothing and been asked nothing, said the only thing she said about it, to the carter and not to the road: **that it is not a practice, and it is not the second half of what she said, and a count is the only thing anybody can be argued with, and she said that once about her own road and this is a woman asking for hers to be taken and it does not touch hers. And the other half is that she is not doing anybody a favour by asking. The road did not agree to be shut. The road agreed to be counted. Those are not the same and the page cannot tell them apart, which is the whole trouble with the page. And if anybody in nine counties writes down this morning that a road agreed to be shut on a count, they will be believed, because that is what a sentence in a hand is for.** **The request was granted by the road and not by any room, and the man of about fifty-eight with a badge is given the thirty-one-year observation on the page: a rule with no head on it is worth exactly nothing, and this is the first time he has watched one get taken anyway, by the people it was about, on a morning when nothing required it of them.** Nobody wrote it down and the gatekeeper read nothing out, because it was not on the paper.

456. **What this gives Block 0003, and it is the first thing in the volume that is a thing anybody did right.** Until the eighteenth of a month **nothing anybody in nine counties had done about that page was a practice, and the volume's standing offer has been that a practice needs somebody to have started it and nobody had.** Now there is one event, once, on one road, about that road, and **three separate people on the page say it is not a precedent in three different ways and one of them says the page cannot tell it apart from the thing it is not.** **Block 0003's reversal, in which Aren Kest closes a route on his own authority in about nine minutes in a room with about nine people in it, is now the exact opposite of this scene, and the volume can say so: on the eighteenth a road asked for a count and was told no head could grant it and asked its own people, and the road's own people were the ones who took it, and nobody thanked anybody, and on a night in Block 0003 a man in a room will shut a thing without asking anybody at all.** That contrast is the volume's spine and it is available now and was not available before this repair.

457. **The man of about thirty-four with a sheet's own untitled sheet has a fourth line on it and it is still in a satchel and still in no book.** The line records that a road on the fifth line asked for its own count to be taken by its own people, on the ground and not at a gate, before its line was settled, and he put the day on it and did not show it to anybody. **IT IS NOT ONE OF THE THREE FIGURES THAT GO DOWN THAT LANE IN A COAT, and this item said it was the fourth and was wrong, and the page said the same thing and was wrong in a worse way.** The three are a number of barrows, written on the page at `chapter-0604.md` in Block 0001; a note on the back of a copy, written at `chapter-0616.md` and given going down the lane at `chapter-0617.md` on the fifteenth; and the tally of what the page did, at `chapter-0620.md` on the nineteenth, **and 0620 says so in its own words: the third time a NUMBER has gone down that lane in a coat in about six weeks, and the first two were a number of barrows and a note on the back of a copy.** **What the man wrote on the eighteenth is not a number.** It is a thing a person had asked for, and `chapter-0619.md` now has him decline the tally in his own reasoning — *a man who has spent a month watching one figure get made out of another figure does not write the two in one line* — **and the lane is about four hundred paces long.** **No chapter of Block 0003 may let it go into the room's book, because there is no head for it, and may not have him stop keeping it, and may not put it in a count with the three numbers.**
