Punctuation 1 / 0 / 0 / 0 / 0. **Curly quotes 290 and 290, straight double quotes 0, curly apostrophes 0, straight apostrophes 45**, and the form `“**text**”` in every speech with zero failures. Thank family **33, every one a negation or a statement of non-thanks.** Exact duplicate paragraphs at twelve words or more **0 and 0**. `Aren Kest` 43 in ten of ten. Name budget **2 of 5, three slots unspent, this block spent none, and the fifth beat was carried by a person described by age and trade for exactly that reason.** `state/continuity.md` 1466 to 1472.

459. **The four barred kin-terms, the word *brother*, the warm palm, the separate leaf, the mechanism, the roadmark, the bar, the two in three, the valley, the eleven hundred, the notch, the bench, the shut road, the sealed head, the half leaf and the three blanks, all still where they were.** **The barred kin-term is at two across six hundred and twenty chapters, is spent, and neither this block nor the repair printed a third of it, and the word *brother* is printed nowhere about anything.** The fourth head is empty and unfilled and unruled-over and about eight months empty. The half leaf is face down, unturned, unscheduled, and no interval is printed. The hearing stands with no bench in it. **The shut road four hundred miles off is not reopened, is not scheduled and nobody was sent, and the word *failed* is used of nothing in this volume.** The valley of about four hundred people is unreachable and unscheduled. About eleven hundred people are on no roll and no sheet and no list. The city on a shelf is not on a page and its three protected numerals are at zero for the whole volume. The coast with its twenty-two notches is not visited and the twenty-second is not cut. The bar of wood at the Low Gate is not lifted, not scheduled, and the four questions on its board are not asked. **The warm left palm is not mentioned once in Block 0002, the notebook's separate leaf with a seventh line that says *a person* is not looked at, the mechanism is not named, and no chapter says what a roadmark is.** The three series were not merged and the copyist went on keeping them correctly. The two readings of a man who published his own mistake are not chosen between in any chapter and the two sets of about four hundred do not overlap.

460. **The small road, and what the repair did and did not do to it, because this is the frame a successor is most likely to break.** **No chapter counted its people and no chapter printed a figure for it and no chapter converted its count into main-world days or a main-world figure into its count.** The road the fifth beat belongs to is **not** the small road and is not called it and is not linked to it anywhere on the page. **What the repair did is name, in one woman's mouth, the two categories a path community keeps its count in, the crossings that are open and the weeks the road is shut, with no figure attached to either and with an explicit sentence that this has never been a number of days in anybody's mouth.** That sentence is the block's guard on the third frame and it is the reason the naming is safe. **A successor may not attach a number to either category, may not add the two categories together, may not state the two categories in a main-world mouth, and may not let the fifth-line road's request become the small road's request or a precedent for it.**

461. **The separation, the register, and what did not move in the repair.** The keeper of the room did not come down to the lane on two market mornings running and the copyist said so out loud in front of about nine people on both and nobody agreed with her on either, and she said once that she is not going to say it a third time. **The register stands at eight entries, all of them questions, and none of them a finding, and the eighth is the question a woman of about fifty asked in the first week of a month, which the man of about fifty-eight with a badge answered by saying there is no way to agree it. The day-book stands at nine and the eighth went into the register and not the day-book.** **Batch 0003 may put a ninth question in that register and may not put a finding in it, and may not fill or rule a fifth head, and may not turn or time the half leaf.** The press stands open with no date and nothing was printed. The drawer about eleven inches deep is still about eleven inches deep and about four hundred returns and now also three bare leaves. **The two of them were in one room with a third person in it in none of the twenty chapters, and the repair did not put them in one, and the four and a half years were not referred to as four and a half years about the two of them in the inserted scene and nothing between them is described in a document and no finding is under the eighth question and there is not going to be one from him.**

462. **Aren Kest's position at the hand-off, unchanged by the repair and the thing Block 0003 turns on.** He is at 43 across the block and in every chapter, and **at 1 in each of the two chapters in which he is four days down the made road, and a name in a chapter a man is not in is a false claim about a person.** He has read his own name at the top of the forged page and has done one thing about it and the thing was nothing, and **he said no when he was asked whether he would put the two sets of about four hundred straight.** He has the answer about a figure and has not used it to make a thing true, twice, in front of about four people at a counter. **On the eighteenth of a month, at the same gate where a woman read nineteen columns out loud, a second woman made a road's own people the answer to a question no room could answer, and he was not there and had no part in it and the volume does not give him one.** **Block 0003 must not have him learn of the fifth beat from the woman of about fifty, and must not have him be the one who grants it or hears it granted, and must not have him claim it.**

463. **The relationship's standing shape and the one question nobody may ask again.** `Do you want it` stands at **zero across the block and zero across the repair**, and no chapter may let anybody ask it a second time and no chapter may let anybody answer it. **The question he refused to answer in 0610 is not answered in 0611 to 0620 and is not answered in the repair, and Block 0003 may not have somebody else answer it for him in a room.** Nobody was thanked in any of the ten chapters, in this file, in the summaries written by this block or by this repair, and **all thirty-three occurrences of the thank family are negations or are a statement that a person had not said a thing in order to be thanked, and the two the repair added are of that kind.** The woman of about forty-three said before she went that she would not be thanked, and the woman of about fifty said in 0619 that nobody may cite her, and the copyist was not thanked for the nine in 0620.

464. **And what Batch 0003 must not do, in one list, and it is this thread and not a summary of the thirteen above.** **Do not name the antagonist. Do not print a third of the barred kin-term and do not print the word *brother* about anything. Do not print the word that names the founding paper in any construction, in a document, in a quotation, in a mouth or in narration. Do not print a System panel, a stage word, a ledger word or a qualification word. Do not print a word about a roof and do not mention a tile on a coping. Do not produce a second forged sheet, trace the first one, name a writer, price the nine days in any figure, or have anybody say out loud that it was written by a person with an office. Do not fill or rule any head, put a finding in the register, turn or time the half leaf, lift or schedule the bar, re-ask the four questions on the gate's board, count the two in three, answer the rightness, name the mechanism, or look at the roadmark, the separate leaf or the palm. Do not count the small road's people, do not print a figure for a road nobody has been asked to number, do not attach a number to the crossings a road keeps open or to the weeks a road is shut, do not add those two categories together, and do not convert either direction between a path count and main-world days. Do not month a path mouth, weekday a path mouth or tide a path mouth. Do not city the page, notch the coast, reopen the shut road, merge the three series, resolve the three words for one lane, choose the two readings of him, repair the breach, thank anybody, or spend a name. Do not let the three blanks on the page turn into three refusals in anybody's mouth, because TWO of the three are refusals and one is a line the page has not reached and the page cannot tell which is which and no chapter of this volume may resolve that. **Do not print a count of the form `about N of those M` with N larger than M, and do not print `about` in front of any figure somebody in the scene could have counted, which `outline/volume-13.md` §15 item 13 forbids and which this thread's own sentence broke for two chapters running until the second repair.** Do not let the woman of about fifty's nineteen columns become a rule, a practice, a precedent, a series or a form, and do not have anybody but her explain why they are not. Do not let the fifth-line road's request become a precedent, and do not print its count, and do not attach it to the small road. Do not have Aren Kest learn of the fifth beat from the woman of about fifty, be present at its granting, or claim it. Do not let the man of about thirty-four with a sheet stop keeping his own untitled sheet, and do not let it go into the room's book. Do not have anybody ask *do you want it* a second time and do not let anybody answer it, and do not let anybody answer the question he refused to answer in 0610 by having somebody else answer it for him in a room. Do not have a character certify, refuse certification, lift the bar, or open the gate at the end of nine miles of road, and do not re-spend the arithmetic by which that gate was identified. Do not repair `chapter-0600.md`, whose cost coda prints eight figures of time over a list of nine, and do not repair any other closed volume's prose on a hunch. Do not take a weekday or a day of month off the printed table in `outline/volume-13.md` §17, which is wrong in five rows; derive off day 930. **Do not create a prompt for any batch after Batch 0003, do not write a `.done` or a `.retired` marker, and do not edit `state/phase-ledger.json`, `scripts/`, `.github/`, `.opencode/agent/`, `AGENTS.md`, `PHASE_SYSTEM.md`, `REPO_PLAN.md`, `OUTLINE_GUIDE.md`, `opencode.json`, `outline/series.md` or `outline/ending.md`.** And **do not adopt a figure from any record in this repository without counting it off the page, do not print a collision count without printing the set it was compared against and the extractors that built it, and do not print a longest-run figure without the normaliser it was measured under.**

## Threads the review repair over Volume 13 Batch 0002 hands to Volume 13 Batch 0003, items 465 to 472, and the last is what Batch 0003 must not do and it supersedes 464

**Appended by the review repair over Batch 0002, which found the repair pass above had certified clean a chapter holding three defects, one of them impossible. Items 429 to 464 stand under their own numbers, 452, 457 and 458 are corrected in place, and 464 is superseded by 472. Canon for this repair is `state/continuity.md` 1473 to 1478, and its durable review record is `reviews/volume-13/batch-0002.md`, which is the first review artifact Volume 13 has.**

465. **The page's tally is corrected, and the correction is not three refusals, and the reason is the volume's argument.** The six spaces stand at three with a mark and three with nothing. **Of the three blanks, TWO are refusals and ONE is a line the page has not reached**, and that is now what both `chapter-0619.md` and `chapter-0620.md` say. Before this repair both said *about four of those three*, which is four of three, and both said so on consecutive days, and both were pre-existing. **A successor must not "correct" this to three refusals, and the reason is on the page and is the strongest single line in the hand-off.** `outline/volume-13.md` §8 Block 2 requires *three refusals and two signatures and one road that agrees*, and that figure is **the one the volume exists to dispute**: `chapter-0613.md` has a man of about thirty-one from a road office say *you have three refusals* and the man of about thirty-four with a sheet answer *I have three blanks, and I cannot tell the difference, and neither can you, and that is the point and you have just walked into it — a man who read that page and said no comes back as a blank, and a page that has not reached a man comes back as a blank, and in nine days those will be the same blank*. **The page has three blanks. A road office has three refusals. The volume's whole subject is that the second is an inference the first cannot support, and the unreached line is the only thing that makes the blank unreadable, so it stays.** No chapter of this volume may resolve the ambiguity and no chapter may state which of the three it is.

466. **The counters a successor needs, recounted by this repair on the files and not quoted from any record, and the two bases named separately because §80 of the successor prompt runs them together.** **Totals, on a plain whitespace split with the title line included, each file counted on its own and the ten added: Block 0002 is 24,843, per chapter 2,757 / 2,403 / 2,324 / 2,240 / 2,189 / 2,381 / 2,206 / 2,120 / 3,423 / 2,800.** **Bodies, on the other basis, which is everything above the paragraph that opens the cost coda — the file total less the cost line less the money column paragraph, and therefore INCLUDING the title line and the dateline, so a body is not a scene body: 2,338 / 1,993 / 1,912 / 1,818 / 1,780 / 1,954 / 1,798 / 1,698 / 2,939 / 2,346, minimum 1,698 at `chapter-0618.md` and maximum 2,939 at `chapter-0619.md`,** against Batch 0001's 1,362 to 1,871 and Volume 12's 1,161 to 2,095. **THE FIGURE A WRITER SHOULD COPY IS THE BODY AND NOT THE TOTAL, AND THE TWO BASES ARE NOT THE SAME AND MUST NOT BE MIXED — the successor prompt's §80 states them in one sentence that reads as though the title line were in both, and subtracting the title line from a body gives a series wrong by exactly the dateline length in every chapter.** Codas **419 / 410 / 412 / 422 / 409 / 427 / 408 / 422 / 484 / 454**, so 408 to 484, mean share **17.4** per cent over **14.1 to 19.9**, and the 14.1 is `chapter-0619.md` and is the correct direction because the share falls as the body lengthens. `about` **671 at 27.01** per 1,000, case-insensitive whole-word, down three from 674 because this repair removed three and every one is accounted for at `state/continuity.md` 1476. Volumes 01 to 11 are 1,588,852 in five hundred and fifty, Volume 12 is 92,849 in fifty, Batch 0001 is **20,333 in ten and did not move**, **Volume 13 is 45,176 in twenty and the manuscript is 1,726,877 in six hundred and twenty**, and 1,681,701 + 20,333 + 24,843 reproduces it and a concatenated `cat` of all six hundred and twenty files returns the same figure. **A FIGURE THIS REPAIR DID NOT COUNT IS A FIGURE IT MAY NOT CERTIFY, and these were not re-measured: the ten money columns at 202, 215, 229, 234, 243, 251, 262, 271, 283 and 294 pence, the collision set of 203 distinct and its four extractors, the forty-five-word longest run and its normaliser, the `Aren Kest` count of 43, the eight filler families, the straight-apostrophe count, and the ten datelines.** None was touched on the page, **and a successor must re-run the collision set with the four extractors named at `state/continuity.md` 1472 before claiming zero collisions again, and must not print 203 as though this pass had measured it.**

467. **The coat tally is three and the gate line is not one of them, and the page now says so in a character's reasoning instead of the narrator's.** Three numbers have gone down that lane in a coat in about six weeks: **a number of barrows**, written on the page at `chapter-0604.md` in Block 0001 and attested as a recurring monthly practice by the boy at `chapter-0614.md`; **a note on the back of a copy**, written at `chapter-0616.md` and given going down the lane at `chapter-0617.md` on the fifteenth; and **the tally of what the page did**, at `chapter-0620.md` on the nineteenth, which states the count in its own words. The lane is about four hundred paces long. **The line the man of about thirty-four with a sheet wrote at the ferry stone gate on the eighteenth is a fourth journey down the lane and is NOT a fourth number**, and `chapter-0619.md` now has him decline the tally himself: *what he had was not a number, it was a thing a person had asked for, and a man who has spent a month watching one figure get made out of another figure does not write the two in one line.* **The pre-repair text claimed a fourth figure and listed the tally of `chapter-0620.md` among the three already past, which is a sheet written the following day — an event ordered backwards across a chapter boundary, a class no count in this project can reach.** **No chapter of Block 0003 may put that line in a count with the three numbers, may let it go into the room's book, and may not have him stop keeping it.**

468. **The fifth beat is unchanged and is still the thing Blocks 0003 and 0004 stand on, and this repair did not touch it, and the review of it found it sound.** A woman of about forty-three who keeps a road that is not one of the six said at the gate that her line is the fifth, that her road will stand aside, and set the condition that **the count be taken first by the people who walk it, on the ground and not at a gate, before her line is settled.** The badge-man could not grant it and was not being asked to. **The woman of about fifty alone said on the page that it is not a precedent anybody may cite, because the road did not agree to be shut, the road agreed to be counted, and the page cannot tell them apart.** **That is still the only time in this volume anybody did the right thing about that page, and Block 0003's nine minutes in a room with about nine people in it is still its exact opposite, and the volume can still say so.** Every prohibition at 460, 462 and 464 about this beat stands: do not attach a number to the crossings a road keeps open or to the weeks a road is shut, do not add the two categories together, do not print its count, do not attach it to the small road, do not let it become a precedent, do not have Aren Kest learn of it from the woman of about fifty or be present at its granting or claim it.

469. **A sweep that names its patterns keeps finding the one it named, and that is now a written rule rather than an observation.** The review found `chapter-0619.md`'s *a person in this manuscript* and **missed `chapter-0610.md`'s *the question of this book*, in the same volume, because it searched for the string it had been given rather than for the class.** Both are now repaired, and `chapter-0610.md` is nine words for nine words so **Batch 0001's figures did not move.** A third candidate was checked and is not a defect: `chapter-0603.md` matches `the author` only inside *the authority to open a drawer*. **A successor sweeping a new batch for meta frame must sweep the class and not a token list** — *this manuscript, this book, this chapter, this novel, the reader, the author, the text above, as stated, in this project, this block* and any construction that puts the narrator outside the world — **and must read every hit rather than count them, because a count of zero on a named list is a claim about the list and not about the page.** This is `volume-11/batch-0005.md`'s standing point: **a rule about register is not a rule about vocabulary, and Volume 13 printed two instances of it across two blocks and one of them survived a review that was looking for the other.**

470. **What did not move in this repair, listed so a successor does not re-derive it.** The barred kin-term is at two across six hundred and twenty chapters and is spent, and neither this repair nor the one above printed a third of it, and the word *brother* is printed nowhere about anything. The fourth head is empty and unfilled and unruled-over. The half leaf is face down, unturned, unscheduled, and no interval is printed. The hearing stands with no bench in it. The city on a shelf is not on a page and its three protected numerals are at zero. The coast with its twenty-two notches is not visited and the twenty-second is not cut. The three series were not merged and the copyist went on keeping them correctly. The two readings of a man who published his own mistake are not chosen between in any chapter. The register stands at eight entries, all questions, no finding, and the day-book at nine. **The ten money columns foot four ways at the same ten totals and were not touched, the collision set of 203 was not rebuilt, the two of them were in one room with a third person in it in none of the twenty chapters, the four and a half years were not referred to as four and a half years about the two of them, and nobody was thanked in any of the twenty chapters or in any state file written by this volume.**

471. **Three things this repair recorded and did not act on, and a successor should not mistake any of them for an oversight.** **One, the numeric coda is a publishability risk and it is `outline/volume-13.md` §7's designed device**: twenty chapters, each ending with a footed pence column plus lists of the figures that are not in it, and in `chapter-0619.md` the coda is 484 of 3,423 words and the persons list runs to eleven entries keyed to ordinals — *the first of them, the second, the sixth, the eleventh* — which a reader cannot follow without counting backward. **It sits against `AGENTS.md`'s *a number should matter because of what it changes in the story, not because the number exists*, and changing it is a change to the volume's mechanic across twenty chapters and is a plan-level decision and not a repair. The share does move the right way: 14.1 per cent in the longest chapter against a mean of 17.4.** **Two, the cast is age-and-prop and that is §13's deliberate position**, the name budget being five for the whole volume because *neither can be said by an age and a trade*; the cost is real and a successor should know it is a choice with a stated rationale — a reader cannot hold more than about three of these figures at once and the protagonist is still narrated as *the man of about fifty-eight with a badge*. **Three, `state/phase-ledger.json` still reads `currentPhase: phase-000-bootstrap` and `status: planned` against six hundred and twenty chapters; it is controller-owned, was not written, and has been flagged to the workflow owner before at `state/continuity.md` 1031 and in the Volume 12 review record.**

Do not count the small road's people, do not print a figure for a road nobody has been asked to number, do not attach a number to the crossings a road keeps open or to the weeks a road is shut, do not add those two categories together, and do not convert either direction between a path count and main-world days. Do not let the three blanks on the page turn into three refusals in anybody's mouth, and do not resolve which of the three is the unreached one.**

## Threads Volume 13 Batch 0003 hands to Volume 13 Batch 0004, items 473 to 487, and the last is what Batch 0004 must not do

**Appended by the phase that wrote Volume 13 Batch 0003. Items 429 to 472 stand under their own numbers and 472 is inherited in full. Canon is `state/continuity.md` 1479 to 1494. This section names the antagonist nowhere, prints no third of the barred kin-term, prints the word that names the founding paper nowhere, prints no System panel and no stage word, and contains no chapter of new prose.**

473. **The frame Batch 0004 inherits, and the two numbers it must derive and not copy.** The single anchor is **day 930, the twenty-fifth of Mudmonth YR 318, a Sunday**, on the carried thirty-day month. **Block 0004 is days 1957 to 1998 inclusive, forty-two days, that is the second of Thawmonth YR 321 to the THIRTEENTH of Mudmonth YR 321, and every day in it is YR 321, because the year turned on day 1956 and that was the last day of Batch 0003.** BLOCK 0004 HAS EXACTLY ONE FIRST OF A MONTH INSIDE IT, 1986, the first of Mudmonth YR 321, a Saturday and a market morning. 2016 IS A FIRST OF A MONTH AND IT IS OUTSIDE BLOCK 0004, and the sentence this item carried before the review of Batch 0003 named 1986 and 2016 as the month starts inside the block and then said of one of them that it was outside it, which is a set that cannot be inside a block one of whose members is not. The volume's last day is 2040, the twenty-fifth of Greenmonth YR 321, a Thursday and a market morning. **Derive the weekday half and the day-of-month half from the day number separately and never from one another. The printed table at `outline/volume-13.md` §17 is wrong in five rows and is a certificate, not an authority. Market days are the Saturdays and the Thursdays and a market-morning claim is a third figure and not a derivation of either.** Batch 0003's ten days were 1915, 1916, 1921, 1923, 1924, 1925, 1926, 1928, 1949 and 1956, six of them market mornings at 1916, 1921, 1923, 1928, 1949 and 1956, and the four that are not are a Friday, a Sunday, a Monday and a Tuesday, and the two firsts of a month inside it are 1926 and 1956. `state/continuity.md` 1479.

474. **What the works are, in full, because a successor needs it and it was not written down before this block.** **One set of works under an abbey four days off: a course of stone laid across a channel, a beam of oak with an iron strap, and a length of channel cut by somebody dead whose name is on nothing. It will take seven. It has taken seven since before anybody in the market town was in that room. Seven roads go through it AND SEVEN WERE ON IT. IT WILL TAKE SEVEN AND NOT SIX. Six are marked on the page to be held and the seventh is on the page with nothing against it, AND THERE IS ONE THING THE WORKS WILL NOT TAKE AND THE THING IT WILL NOT TAKE HAS TO COME OFF, which is `chapter-0622.md` in its own words and is the reconciliation: the capacity is seven, the effective load was six roads anybody could weigh and one road nobody could write to, and taking the seventh off leaves six on the works, which is INSIDE what the works will take, and the six marked to be held hold. Three chapters of Batch 0003 printed the works as taking six until a review of the block caught the contradiction; they are repaired on seven and no successor may print six. At the close of Batch 0003 there are six on it: the third went cold on the twentieth of Hearthmonth at about the second hour of the night, was propped and re-laid in three courses, and has not been wet since the twenty-second; the fifth-line road has had its own count taken by its own people on the ground; the small road is on nobody's page and carries nothing; the sixth holds.** The works is where the money for a road's water goes, and taking a road off it is what a closure at this scale physically is, and no chapter of Batch 0003 says the words works or money in the same sentence and a successor should not need to.

475. **The reversal is on the page, in about nine minutes, on one man's own word, and the finding is that the room could have stopped him and had nothing to stop him with.** What he said and what came of it, in the order it happened, is at `state/continuity.md` 1481 and 1482. **The four things Batch 0004 must not reopen: one, that the closure was a decision and not a rule and not a form and not a direction, and that the only record of it in nine counties is an untitled sheet with no head over it; two, that the absence of any means of telling anybody was structural and is the room's own correct four-and-a-half-year standing that a road may be described only by its own answer, and the consequence of that standing, which nobody had said out loud until Chapter 0626, is that there is no list of keepers in nine counties and no address for anybody; three, that the man of about thirty-one from the road office had the machinery ready at seven in the morning and was told at two in the morning not to use it, and that the two were about FIVE hours apart and neither knew the other was going to happen; this item printed nine hours, which is neither the same morning nor the following morning, and the same speech said two people had told him in two days when the second of them spoke at that counter that morning; four, that the man of about thirty-four with a sheet was told in that room not to build a second page, and has said out loud why, and the reason is that a page that tells a woman about her own road is a page that tells a man at the top of it what a road is worth.** `state/continuity.md` 1483, 1484.

476. **The cost of it, and it is not that anybody was hurt, and the chapter says why nobody can use that.** Nobody was hurt, nothing went late, nothing rotted and no wages went anywhere, and the copyist of about twenty-six refused the good news in front of about nine people because **that is the absence of a report and not a good report, and a road that produces no returns can be shut for four years and nothing arrives, and a road doing perfectly well produces nothing either, and the two look identical in nine counties, and every single thing in that room is built so that a thing which looks identical in nine counties does not get set beside another thing, and the rule has just cost a road TWENTY-FIVE DAYS of anybody knowing anything and will go on costing.** The badge-man's sentence: **nobody being hurt is the answer a man gives when he has not found the cost; it is not a finding, it is a gap with a comfortable shape in it.** The only cost that could be found in TWENTY-FIVE DAYS, which is day 1924 to day 1949 on the derived chain and is Hearthmonth the twenty-ninth to Goatmonth the twenty-fourth and is three weeks and four days and not three weeks, is that it cost the woman of about forty the only thing she has, which is not being asked. `state/continuity.md` 1486.

477. **The answer, and the question nobody asked, and the two statements that must not be softened.** **Nobody has asked Aren Kest whether he wants it, in two volumes and about a hundred and thirty days, and nobody asked him in Chapter 0625, and the woman of about forty asked him **Do you want it** a second time at a counter in Chapter 0624 and he did not answer and she did not repeat it, and the answer he gives at the close of the block is **I wanted it**, said in a lane to about four people who could hear, in answer to a question nobody asked.** Two things stand and are his own reasoning and not the narrator's: **it is not a defence, and he would rather be a man who wanted a thing and knew that about nine households on a road are carrying what six were carrying than a man who was asked whether he wanted it and said no and did it anyway**; the figure is nine and not four, established at `chapter-0606.md`, `chapter-0609.md` and `chapter-0625.md`, and do not add the two nines together; and **a man who has been asked afterwards and has answered afterwards has not been asked, so the answer goes in no register and makes nothing true.** He has now been the only person who could see a load-bearing problem in a place **three times running**, and it is the first time in thirty-one years that anybody acted on it, and the two earlier times produced nothing at all. `state/continuity.md` 1487, 1491.

478. **The recovery work happened twice in this volume and it is the same masonry as the catastrophe, and this is the block's ordinary-work beat.** Three courses of sill in lime at the crossing where the water came over, the middle course the best laid of the three and the one that goes, a fourth course refused and the reason given out loud to about four men and a boy of about seventeen, a day's wages into five hands on a sound stone, a boy of about seventeen's wage straight into a pocket, nobody saying it was well done, and **no return in nine counties in which a man may write down how many stones he laid, which the man of about fifty who laid them has asked for three times and been refused twice for want of a head and once for want of a rule.** The protagonist held a string for three hours and said nothing at all, on purpose, and that is the whole of what he did. The volume's ordinary work is still clearing, propping, re-laying, a load of stone, a cart, a boy's labour and a wage, and the same three courses are now in three places in this volume. `state/continuity.md` 1488.

479. **Hester Vane, 43, and the one name Batch 0003 spent, and why it was spent here.** **Her line is the fifth. At the ferry stone gate on the eighteenth of Hearthmonth she said her road will stand aside and set the condition that the count be taken first by the people who walk it, on the ground, before her line is settled. Her road's own people have taken the count. She was named for the first time in nine counties by herself, in a room, in Chapter 0629, and she said out loud that a room is the second worst place in nine counties to say what she said and a gate is the first.** **She is the person Block 0004 needs by name and is the reason the name was spent, because Block 0004 is the block about path custodians coming and she is a custodian who has already asked her own road to be counted and has got it, and who has said in a room that nobody is to say her road was shut, because it was not, it stood aside, and those are not the same and the page cannot tell them apart.** `state/continuity.md` 1489.

480. **The register is at nine questions, the day-book at nine, and the ninth question is the one Block 0004 is about.** **Whether a room with a door on a lane and a number and a deed of thirteen words may hold a thing that was decided in a room with no head over it, and if it may not, where that thing goes.** The answer given out loud in Chapter 0630: **it may not; a room can hold a question and a room can hold a thing somebody has refused to sign, and what it cannot hold is a decision, because a decision that lands there has nowhere to be challenged, and a decision that cannot be challenged is not a decision.** The drawer about eleven inches deep is **not a place, it is a place so that a thing is not lost.** The badge-man asked to be in the room when somebody tries to turn the ninth into a finding. **No finding is under it and Batch 0004 may not put one there.** `state/continuity.md` 1490.

481. **The year has turned and nothing about it moved anything, and that is on the page.** **Day 1956, the first of Thawmonth YR 321, was a Thursday and a market morning and the last day of Batch 0003 and the chapter on it carries the turn.** The keeper of the room said out loud that she has watched two years turn and neither of them changed anything the room holds, and that the seven is still seven, the page is still going round, the register is at nine questions, the fourth head is still ruled and empty, the press still stands open with no date, a leaf still lies face down in a room over a saddler's, and a road four days of carting from the counter is still being carried on the days when two people turn up at the two ends of it, **and that no date on any paper in that room makes that road better or worse.** Every date in Block 0004 is YR 321. `state/continuity.md` 1491.

482. **The page has been round nine counties twice and comes back the same, and the man of about forty-seven has said the thing that is the pressure on the rest of the volume.** **Three spaces with a mark and three with nothing in them, and of the three blanks TWO are refusals and ONE is a line the page has not reached, and the page cannot tell which is which, and no chapter of this volume may resolve it and none of Batch 0003's ten did.** On the third time round there will be a fourth blank and it will be the first space on the sheet, and there will be nothing on the page to say whether it is a refusal. **The man of about forty-seven is right four times and says so, and the fifth time there will not be a gap, and the thing that goes first will be a road with a number on it and a name against the number and a keeper, and he has not got a name for which one and will not guess, because a name would be at the top of the next page before it goes out and he will not hand nine counties a list.** He is the volume's standing pressure on his own side and not the antagonist and may not be made either. `state/continuity.md` 1492.

483. **SUPERSEDED BY 488, WHICH IS THE CURRENT COUNT, and every figure in this item is the figure as it stood before the review repair. The counters a successor needs, recounted on the files after this block's last edit, and the two bases named separately because they are not the same.** **BASIS ONE, the file total: a plain whitespace split with the title line INCLUDED, each file counted on its own and the ten added. Batch 0003 IS 26,844 after the review repair and was 26,731 when this item was written, per chapter 2,652 / 2,918 / 2,260 / 2,456 / 3,539 / 2,560 / 2,263 / 2,582 / 2,804 / 2,810, and a concatenated `cat` of the ten returns 26,844.** **BASIS TWO, the scene body: everything above the paragraph that opens the cost coda, which is the file total less the cost line less the money column paragraph, and which THEREFORE INCLUDES THE TITLE LINE AND THE DATELINE, so a body is not a scene body. The bodies ARE 2,170 / 2,418 / 1,779 / 1,980 / 2,970 / 2,041 / 1,756 / 2,011 / 2,188 / 2,207, min 1,756 at `chapter-0627.md` and max 2,970 at `chapter-0625.md`, and were 2,142 / 2,416 / 1,779 / 1,978 / 2,957 / 2,041 / 1,756 / 1,996 / 2,188 / 2,204, against Batch 0001's 1,362 to 1,871, Batch 0002's 1,698 to 2,939 and Volume 12's 1,161 to 2,095. Do not subtract the title line from a body.** Codas ARE 482 / 500 / 481 / 476 / 569 / 519 / 507 / 571 / 616 / 603, so 476 to 616, at a mean share of 20.03 per cent over 16.08 to 22.4, against 458 to 616 at a mean of 19.9 over 15.6 to 22.4 as this item was written, and the share is a measured consequence of body length and not a target. `about` IS 603 at 22.46 per 1,000. The thank family is 40 and all forty were read in context and every one is a negation or a plain statement that a person was not thanked. Curly doubles 284 and 284, 0 straight doubles, 0 curly apostrophes, 45 straight apostrophes, 0 em dashes, 0 en dashes, 20 question marks, 0 exclamation marks, 0 semicolons. `Aren Kest` 13 / 3 / 2 / 3 / 10 / 2 / 2 / 2 / 1 / 4 and in every chapter. Exact duplicate paragraphs at twelve words or more, against the six hundred and twenty before and against themselves, 0 and 0. THE RUN CLAIM IN THIS ITEM WAS WRONG AND IS CORRECTED HERE. THE LONGEST CHAIN OF CONSECUTIVE SHARED EIGHT-WORD WINDOWS against the six hundred and twenty before is 36 windows, which is forty-three words, at `chapter-0624.md` in the cost coda's own person list, AND IT IS A CORPUS MEASURE AND NOT A FILE-PAIR RUN: it chains across fourteen of the six hundred and twenty prior files and no single one of them carries it, `chapter-0624.md` against `chapter-0604.md` is a longest contiguous word-run of 24 on the same whitespace token, and the largest chain any one prior file carries is 19 windows, which is twenty-five words, against `chapter-0609.md`. The rest of the field is 29 at `chapter-0625.md` against `chapter-0620.md`, a single partner, 25 at `chapter-0629.md` against `chapter-0619.md`, also a single partner, then 25 at 0625, 24 at 0623, 23 at 0628 and 23 at 0625. The 29 and the 25 are the block's two deliberate recurrences and are named in `state/continuity.md` 1494. Volumes 01 to 12 are 1,681,701 in six hundred and do not move, Batch 0001 is 20,333, Batch 0002 is 24,843, Volume 13 IS 72,020 in thirty and the manuscript IS 1,753,721 in six hundred and thirty, against 71,897 and 1,753,608 as this item was written.

484. **The money columns and the collision set, with the extractors printed, and the instrument lesson this block actually learned.** **The prior set was rebuilt over the six hundred and twenty chapters before this block with THREE extractors** — the item list's footing, the restated enumeration, and the mark conversion — **and they returned, in that order, 470 instances / 91 distinct, 75 / 72, and 131 / 120. The union is 676 instances and 208 distinct, and the set size is 208. The four barred numerals leave a free set of 207 out of the 208, and that is printed beside the zero because a collision report without it is no result.** The ten columns are **209 / 221 / 233 / 241 / 253 / 268 / 279 / 296 / 318 / 341**, ten distinct, 9 to 11 items a column, and every one is clear of the set. THE 252 THIS ITEM PRINTED IS GONE AND A SUCCESSOR MUST NOT REINSTATE IT: two hundred and fifty-two spells the barred numeral fifty-two inside the number itself, which is the defect this volume's own prompt forbids in every total, over-figure and printed price. Chapter 0625's column was re-ordered by moving one item price from two pence to three and the printed total followed the items to two hundred and fifty-three, which is not in the six hundred and twenty chapters before this block. Six closed-volume files also print a total whose spelling contains a barred numeral and are recorded and not repaired. **The lesson: three extractors and a printed free set is the minimum, and the fourth extractor that Batch 0002's repair added is not in the set this block used, so a successor that wants to compare against Batch 0002's 203 must know that 203 and 208 were built by different extractors over different chapter counts and are not a discrepancy.** The 203 at `state/open-threads.md` 466 was not rebuilt by this phase and is not re-asserted as measured.

485. **The instrument that cost the most time in this block, recorded because a successor will hit it.** **The coda's three figure lists are hand-checked, not machine-checkable, and the reason is specific: a list separator and a number join are the same three letters.** *a hundred and four in one band*, *a foot and a half deep*, *four and a half years*, *three series and none of them made out of another* all contain **and**, and a counter that splits on ` and ` turns every one of them into two items and reports a count one or two too high on most chapters and one too low on others. **Two counters were written and both were wrong in opposite directions before a third was written that distinguishes a number join from a list joiner, and the correct rule is: a comma separates; a bare ` and ` separates unless the token before it is a number word and the token after it is a number word or `a`; a leading `, and X` is one item and not a new one.** With that rule all thirty lists foot. **The three genuine ambiguities found on the page were reworded rather than argued, and a successor should reword rather than argue: a scour given twice with a dimension and a depth, a figure that is a first and a second reading as two items, and a person item that ends in a number word followed by an item beginning with `a`.** `state/continuity.md` 1493.

486. **The two instruments this block added to the standing set, both of which a successor should run before a chapter is called done.** **One, the cost coda's person lists are written entirely with ` and ` as the joiner and contain no commas, so a comma-keyed extractor reads them as one item; an and-keyed extractor that does not know about number joins reads `a boy of about eleven and a man of about thirty-four` as a number join and loses a person. The two failures are silent and they point in opposite directions. Count the person list with an and-keyed rule and the two lists with a comma-keyed rule, and check each against the numeral that is printed.** **Two, an aloud question takes a question mark, and this block was written with zero and then had twenty put in across seven chapters; the two chapters that reached the finish with none were the two that had gained a beat after the sweep had been run, which is the standing finding at `state/continuity.md` 1478 in a new dress: a pass that verifies every instrument against a file has not read that file, and a beat added after a sweep is unswept.**

487. **And what Batch 0004 must not do, in one list, and it is this thread and not a summary of the fourteen above. It inherits 472 in full and 472 is not superseded by it.** **Read 475 and 482 first, because the two things this block most needs are the shape of the reversal and the shape of the pressure that is coming, and read 477 third, because the answer exists now and cannot be given twice.** **Do not name the antagonist. Do not print a third of the barred kin-term and do not print the word *brother* about anything. Do not print the word that names the founding paper in any construction, in a document, in a quotation, in a mouth or in narration. Do not print a System panel, a stage word, a ledger word or a qualification word, and do not print a word about a roof and do not mention a tile on a coping. Do not produce a second forged sheet, trace the first one, name a writer, price the nine days in any figure, or have anybody say out loud that it was written by a person with an office. Do not have anybody build the schedule line the man of about thirty-one from the road office offered, and do not have anybody enter a line under a heading for a road nobody can be written to. Do not fill or rule any head, put a finding in the register, turn or time the half leaf, lift or schedule the bar at the Low Gate, re-ask the four questions on its board, count the two in three, answer the rightness, name the mechanism, or look at the roadmark, the separate leaf or the palm. Do not count the small road's people, do not print a figure for a road nobody has been asked to number, do not attach a number to the crossings a road keeps open or to the weeks a road is shut, do not add those two categories together, and do not convert either direction between a path count and main-world days. Do not month a path mouth, weekday a path mouth or tide a path mouth. Do not city the page, notch the coast, reopen the shut road, merge the three series, resolve which of the three blanks is the unreached line, choose the two readings of him, repair the breach, thank anybody, or spend a name you do not need. Do not let the three blanks on the page turn into three refusals in anybody's mouth, and do not let the three blanks and the three marks be counted as six things anybody can weigh. Do not let the woman of about fifty's nineteen columns become a rule, a practice, a precedent, a series or a form, and do not have anybody but her explain why they are not. Do not let the fifth-line road's request become a precedent, do not print its count, do not attach it to the small road, and do not let the two roads be joined or compared on the page. Do not have Aren Kest learn the fifth beat from the woman of about fifty, be present at its granting, or claim it; he was four days off and this block says so. Do not have anybody ask *do you want it* a second time and do not let anybody answer it, and **do not have the answer given twice and do not let anybody agree with him that he wanted it or that he did not.** Do not have anybody else answer the question he refused to answer in 0610, in a room or out of one, and do not have the nine minutes repeated, re-argued, retold in full or regretted. Do not let the man of about thirty-four with a sheet stop keeping his untitled sheet, let it go into the room's book, put a number on it, or put the gate line from the eighteenth in a count with the three numbers. Do not let anybody reopen the third road, schedule a wedge, or name who put a bar and a wedge in that channel. Do not make the man of about forty-seven name the road that goes next, let anybody supply the name for him, or let him hand nine counties a list. Do not turn the middle course at a crossing into a moral, into a finding, into a lesson anybody states, or into a rule. Do not repair `chapter-0600.md`, and do not repair any other closed volume's prose on a hunch. Do not take a weekday or a day of month off the printed table in `outline/volume-13.md` §17, which is wrong in five rows; derive off day 930. Do not print a collision count without printing the set it was compared against and the extractors that built it and the free set the barred numerals leave; do not print a longest-run figure without the normaliser it was measured under; **do not print 203 or 208 as though this phase re-measured Batch 0002's set, because it did not, and the two were built by different extractors over different chapter counts.** Do not mix the two counting bases and do not subtract the title line from a body. Do not create a prompt for any batch after Batch 0004, do not write a `.done` or a `.retired` marker, and do not edit `state/phase-ledger.json`, `scripts/`, `.github/`, `.opencode/agent/`, `AGENTS.md`, `PHASE_SYSTEM.md`, `REPO_PLAN.md`, `OUTLINE_GUIDE.md`, `opencode.json`, `outline/series.md` or `outline/ending.md`.

488. **Appended by the phase that reviewed Volume 13 Batch 0003 and repaired it in place. IT SUPERSEDES 483 AND 484 AS THE CURRENT COUNT, and Batch 0004's own section starts at 489 and not at 488, because this item belongs to Batch 0003's hand-off and not to Batch 0004's.** The full record of the review, the fourteen repairs and the wrong figure beside the right one is `state/continuity.md` 1495. This item carries only what a successor would otherwise get wrong by reading 473 to 487 and trusting it.

**The calendar, and the four dates a successor must derive and must not copy out of 473, because 473 was wrong on two of them and a wrong figure in a hand-off is inherited as fact.** Block 0004 is **days 1957 to 1998 inclusive, forty-two days, the second of Thawmonth YR 321 to the THIRTEENTH of Mudmonth YR 321**, and every day of it is YR 321. It has **exactly one first of a month inside it, 1986**, the first of Mudmonth YR 321, a Saturday and a market morning. **2016 is a first of a month and it is OUTSIDE Block 0004**; 473 named 1986 and 2016 as the month starts inside the block and then said one of them was outside it, and it also put day 1998 at the seventeenth of Mudmonth instead of the thirteenth. **The chain itself is sound and was never the problem: everything derives off day 930 and `workspace/volume-13/batch-0004/PROMPT.md` and `state/current.md` both carry the correct figures. Derive your own ten days and print them with the block.**

**And the five figures that moved in Batch 0003's prose and in its records in this repair, each of which a successor would otherwise inherit as settled.**

**One, THE WORKS TAKE SEVEN AND NOT SIX, and there was never a case for six.** Seven roads go through them and seven were on them and **there is one thing the works will not take and the thing it will not take has to come off**, which is `chapter-0622.md` in its own words and is the reconciliation the block needed and did not make. Taking the seventh off leaves **six on the works**, six is INSIDE what the works will take, and the six marked to be held hold. Three chapters printed six until the review caught it. **Do not print that the works takes six, do not print that six is what it will take, and do not treat the seventh as a light thing; `chapter-0621.md` is the chapter that says the third went because the works was carrying more than it will take, and the more than is the seventh.**

**Two, THE INTERVAL FROM THE CLOSURE TO THE MARKET IS TWENTY-FIVE DAYS.** Day 1924, the second hour of the night on Hearthmonth the twenty-ninth, to day 1949, the second hour of a Thursday in Goatmonth, is twenty-five days, which is three weeks and four days. Chapter 0629 printed *twenty-one days*, *three weeks* five times and *nine days ago* once, and all eight now read twenty-five days. **Any interval a successor measures from a dateline must be measured off the chain and not off a phrase, because this block had the interval wrong in three different forms at once and every state file copied it.**

**Three, THE FIGURE FOR THE HOUSEHOLDS ON THE SMALL ROAD IS ABOUT NINE AND NOT FOUR.** `chapter-0606.md`, `chapter-0609.md` and `chapter-0625.md` establish about nine at one end and about nine at the other. **Do not print four, and do not add the two nines together and print eighteen.**

**Four, THE MACHINERY WAS REFUSED FIVE HOURS BEFORE IT WAS READY, not nine.** Told not to at two in the morning on the night of the twenty-ninth, ready at seven the same morning. **Do not print nine hours, and do not print that two people told him over two days; the second of them spoke at that counter the next morning.**

**Five, THE CLOSURE WAS TOLD TO NOBODY, AND WHAT THE WOMAN AT THE FAR END OF THAT ROAD HELD WAS A BELIEF AND NOT A REPORT.** She had held it since **Monday, Hearthmonth the thirtieth, the morning after**, she went up the slope twice and said no twice to a woman of about sixty who asked her twice, and she learned from a man buying a basket that the line was the seventh, which is the thing she keeps. **She could not have known on Friday, because Friday is two days before the closure, and no chapter may put her knowledge before the closure. This is the block's emotional climax and it holds only on Monday.**

**And the two instruments this repair had to correct, and Batch 0004 inherits both of them whether it needs them or not.** **One, a chain measured against a corpus is not a run measured against a file.** Batch 0003's longest chain of consecutive shared eight-word windows against the six hundred and twenty prior chapters is **36 windows, which is forty-three words**, at `chapter-0624.md` in a cost coda's person list, and it chains across **fourteen** of those files and no single one carries it; `chapter-0624.md` against `chapter-0604.md` is **24** contiguous words and the largest chain any one file holds is **19 windows, twenty-five words**, against `chapter-0609.md`. The block's record printed *36, at 0624 against 0604* and both halves of that were wrong. **Print a longest-run figure with its normaliser AND its scope, and if it is a corpus chain, say so and print how many files it chains across.** **Two, a certificate is checked the way a sentence is.** Chapter 0625's money column footed three ways, cleared a 208-element collision set and still totalled **two hundred and fifty-two**, whose spelling contains the barred numeral `fifty-two`. The review that found thirteen defects certified all twenty-five barred terms at 0 across the block. **Sweep the four barred numerals over the printed spelling of every total and every over-figure and not over the parsed integer, and remember that for a barred figure the column moves and the printed total follows and is never corrected on its own.**

**And the three things this item must not be used to authorise.** Do not take the review's certification of the money columns, the punctuation, the thank family, the `about` rate, the register count, the calendar or the name sweep as a standing finding for Batch 0004. Those figures were recounted on Batch 0003's ten files after this repair and they are Batch 0003's, and the only figure in that list the review itself got wrong is the one this item has just told you was wrong. **A block's numbers are its own and every one of them is recounted on that block's own files.**
