## Threads Volume 14 Batch 0001 hands to Volume 14 Batch 0002, items 541 to 556, and the last is what Batch 0002 must not do

**This section is written by the phase that wrote Volume 14 Batch 0001 and wrote its block, and it is Batch 0002's whole instruction. `outline/volume-14.md` is the plan the block was written against and is binding, `state/continuity.md` 1538 to 1550 are the facts, and item 556 is the list of what Batch 0002 must not do. Every figure was counted on the ten chapter files as they stand after this phase's last edit and the basis is named. Nothing above is renumbered and nothing above is resolved. Fifteen threads, numbered from 541, and the last is what the successor must not do. This section names the antagonist, because naming it is an event of this volume.**

541. **The frame Batch 0002 inherits, and the two numbers it must derive rather than copy.** Day 930, the twenty-fifth of Mudmonth YR 318, a Sunday, is the only anchor, on the carried thirty-day month. **Block 0002 is days 2095 to 2148, fifty-four inclusive, that is the twentieth of Rainmonth YR 321 to the thirteenth of Fallowmonth YR 321, and every day of it is YR 321.** The month starts inside Block 0002 are 2106 and 2136, at Harvestmonth the first and Fallowmonth the first. **Every date is YR 321, because the year does not turn until day 2341, which is after the end of this volume.** Market days are the Saturdays and the Thursdays. **Derive the weekday half and the day-of-month half separately and report them as two figures and never as one, and a market-morning claim is a third.** `state/continuity.md` 1539.

542. **The instrument, in full, because Batch 0002 inherits a document and not a document's meaning.** It is at item 1540 and it is not to be restated here. **What Batch 0002 may add and what it may not:** it may add what an officer does with it in a second county, and it may not trace the abridgement, may not name who abridged it, may not produce a third paper, and **may not make the copies in the county whole without saying out loud on the page that they have been found whole, which would move the volume's engine and is a Block 0004 question at the earliest.**

543. **The block's finding, in its finished form, and it is the sentence the volume is built on.** **The three clauses are about shutting a road and the instrument takes, and not one of the three is touched by it.** Given out loud in a market street on the twelfth of Longlight by a man with a badge, in about nine minutes, in front of about nine people, and nobody thanked him. **A successor may not soften it, may not find a third clause that would have caught it, and may not have anybody say out loud that the three clauses were a mistake**, which is a Block 0004 beat and this volume's own argument.

544. **The three clauses and their holders and their exact state.** The notice, the witness, the burden, in nine hands with no copy, quoted once in Block 0001 and reprinted exactly. **The right to refuse is a fourth sentence that was never written down, reproduced word for word in Chapter 0655 and read aloud on the last day of Volume 13, and it is the sentence that says a road may refuse to name a witness and that if it refuses then nothing happens to that road and the man who said the road might be shut has not shown that it may be shut and nobody is going to be able to get inside the refusal afterwards and find out.** **A Batch 0002 chapter may put that sentence in a mouth and may not add a clause to it and may not soften it into something a road cannot refuse.**

545. **The standing defect, and it has been paid half of, and Batch 0002 inherits a shape it did not expect.** The badge-man said in a street on the twenty-fifth of Greenmonth that there is no head in nine counties with which to call a meeting, that he is not going to make one, and that in about four months somebody is going to want something out of this and there will be nobody to ask. **On the sixteenth of Longlight he counted out loud that it was twenty-one days, not four months, and said out loud that being right early about a thing you were right about is the same as being wrong about how long you had, and said he is not moving the figure, and that in about a month nine people are going to ask him whether he had offered a man a name and he will have to say that he did.** **`outline/volume-14.md` §8 Block 3 was amended by this phase so that Block 0003 gets the SECOND half of the defect, which is the four months genuinely arriving in a form nobody can refuse at a gate, and the standing question of Block 0003 is whether being early was a kindness.** Batch 0002 must not spend either half.

546. **The delegation, in full, and where it is, and what it is doing.** At item 1543. **Salla Dunnock went home from the market town on the twenty-fifth of Longlight with a woman of about sixty, a boy of about nine and a carter, and reached the coast about the twenty-ninth of Longlight. The wagon came back empty to the market town on the second of Rainmonth with a boy of about nineteen on it.** **She is now four days of road away at the start of Block 0002 and about a month has passed, and Batch 0002's problem is that she cannot send a letter in four days and there is no arrangement by which anybody here can go and ask her what she said to about nine hundred people.** **The answer she is carrying has not been tested and the question she came for has been answered twice and neither answer was it.**

547. **The column, and there is no figure for it.** **No chapter of Volume 14 may print a number for the column, and one boy counted it twice on the first of Rainmonth and gave two figures and gave neither.** A captain of about forty-four named **Halvard Rees** was at the town's own gate on the nineteenth of Rainmonth with a full copy of the instrument, read out a clause that says any person asked which place is meant shall answer, said out loud that it is the right instrument and why, said that four bridge crews are working forward on the lane and that one of them is Tovan Kest's yard, and went back down the road that night having sent about four men forward to the ford. **Where about nine hundred men are on the nineteenth of Rainmonth is not established and Batch 0002 may establish it and may not number it.**

548. **The crossing and the palm, and what may and may not be said.** At item 1544. **The crossing is done, on a road a person opened and did not open for him, in about a second and a half, in a foot of water, and it cost him the ability to ask himself whether he wants what he is doing.** **No System panel may be printed in this volume without being spent deliberately and at most once, and no chapter of Batch 0002 may name the mechanism, may not say what a roadmark is, may not look at the notebook's separate leaf, and may not have another character notice the warmth of the left palm.**

549. **The four marks and what they are.** At item 1546. **They are where the water stops. They are not a survey and not a map and not a road office, and a Crown officer who knows the day in advance does not need a column, he needs four marks and a book, and the four were put in by one man with a stick of chalk in a morning on the eighth of Longlight and will be read in about four years by another.** **A successor may add marks and may not change what this one means.**

550. **The three series, and the standing second cost of this volume, and it is now on the page.** About nine hundred sheets a month out into nine counties, about a hundred and four in one band on a fixed day, about four hundred and nine parish rolls a month in off the roads, and none of the three is made out of another. **The copyist of about twenty-six said out loud in a market street on the twelfth of Longlight that she wanted to put the sheet into one of the three and did not, and gave the reason, which is that the sheet is not a return and is not a roll and is not a sheet going out to a district, and that a fourth series in a drawer about eleven inches deep in a room with a door on a lane would be a paper out of a marshal's office kept by one woman of about twenty-nine.** **That is this volume's second cost and it is different in kind from Volume 13's: the room's correct three-series practice, which Volume 13 established as a known blind spot, is now useless for the actual problem, because the instrument that is taking the works is not a record and cannot go into one and could not be put into one without being made into something else.**

551. **The imperfection, still the same imperfection, and it is now two.** **One, the standing one: the three clauses cover roads that can be written to, because every clause requires somebody to be found.** **Two, and it is new to this volume and it was named out loud in a market street by a person who is not the protagonist: the clauses cover a man's gesture, and a paper that makes no gesture reaches past all three of them, and nine counties have nobody to ask.** Nobody has called either hole small and no chapter of Batch 0002 may call either of them small.

552. **The relationship, and it is open, and this block moved it and did not mend it.** **The woman of about twenty-nine who keeps the room over a weigh-house is `Mara Vey` and is named in prose, once, in Chapter 0651, in a lane, because a carter asked whose sheet a cart was on.** The separation is a resolution and is not mended: they are four hundred paces apart, the work continues, they keep separate allies and the right to disagree, and nothing between them is in any document. **They are in one room together with a third person in it in Chapter 0659, and it is the first time in the manuscript that they have been, and nothing is mended.** **She refused to say the thing he had found for him, and said out loud that if she said it then nine counties would have it out of a woman who keeps a room and not out of the man who found it, and that in about a year that would be the version.** The barred kin-term was at two across the whole manuscript and was spent, **and this volume prints it four times at most and has printed it twice, both in a refusal of a bar in Chapter 0654.** **The four and a half years are never referred to as four and a half years about the two of them.**

553. **The protagonist's position at the end of Block 0001, in full, because a successor must not soften it.** **He has the finding and has had it for three weeks and two days and has said it in front of about nine people exactly once, and what he said was one true small thing about a ford.** He has been asked four times in his life whether he wants what he is doing and has answered it once, in a lane in Thawmonth, to about four people who could hear. **The badge-man has now asked him a fifth time, to be the name a warrant goes in a place for, and he has said no and given the reason, and the badge-man has said out loud that he is not going to ask twice.** **Nobody has asked him whether he wants to cross. Nobody has said out loud that he can go further now. He said both of those things to a stone in a foot of water and to nobody else and there is no record of them anywhere.** **Four hundred paces of lane, two doors, and about nine people who have stopped looking at him and started waiting for him to speak, and he has not.**

554. **The name budget, the arithmetic, and what this block spent.** The budget for Volume 14 is six. **Block 0001 spent two: `Salla Dunnock` in Chapter 0652 and `Halvard Rees` in Chapter 0660. `Mara Vey` is a return, not a spend, and is recorded at `state/continuity.md` 1538.** **Four slots remain and Blocks 0002 to 0005 have to live inside them.** **A capitalised two-word sweep over the ten files returns only `Aren Kest`, `Tovan Kest`, `Salla Dunnock`, `Mara Vey`, `Halvard Rees`, `Hester Vane`, `Edric Hunn` and the two words for a made road and the four that name a Crown body.** `Hester Vane` is an established return from Volume 13 and is identified in Chapter 0655 as one of the about three people who stood up in the street. **The antagonist's name is printed ONCE in this block and the cap for the volume is FOUR.**

555. **The measurements, and the two instruments a successor must not re-verify and the three it must.** **Do not recheck:** the file total of 31,848, the ten per-chapter totals, the ten bodies, the ten money columns and their four-way footing, the collision result of zero against a set of 211 distinct built by the declared extractor over the 650 prior files, the exact-duplicate count of 0 and 0, and the 58-window shared run, **which is a deliberate reprise and not a defect and which a successor will find and must leave alone.** **Recount rather than inherit:** the calendar, because the printed table at `outline/volume-13.md` §17 is wrong in five rows; **any money collision count, because a collision set is a set of numbers plus the extractor that built it and the extractor for this block is at `state/continuity.md` 1549 and at `state/batch-summaries.md`;** and **the age of the protagonist, which is flagged and not acted on and which a volume close may resolve if it says it is doing one.**

556. **And what Batch 0002 must not do, in one list, and it is this thread and not a summary of the fifteen above.** **Do not name the antagonist again. Do not print the kin-term a third and fourth time except where the block earns both, and never as a joke and never about a third person. Do not print the word that names the founding paper in any construction, in a document, in a quotation, in a mouth or in narration. Do not print a System panel without spending it deliberately and at most once, and do not print the word *stage*, the word *ledger* or the word *qualification*, and do not name the mechanism and do not say what a roadmark is. Do not look at the notebook's separate leaf or at the roadmark. Do not let another character notice the warmth of the left palm. Do not trace the abridgement of the copies, name who abridged them, or produce a third paper. Do not print a figure for the column, and do not count it. Do not spend the standing defect's two halves. Do not fill or rule any head, put a finding in the register, turn or time the half leaf, lift or schedule the bar at the Low Gate, re-ask the four questions on its board, count the two in three, answer the rightness, or look at the mechanism. Do not get the road that went quiet back, do not name it, do not send anybody to it, do not have anybody come back from it, do not resolve it in either direction, and do not say it does not matter. Do not reopen the shut road four hundred miles off and do not send anybody to the coast and do not cut the twenty-second notch. Do not merge the three series or derive one from another and do not change the band figure. Do not convert a path's open-crossing count into main-world days, and do not convert the three weeks again, because the conversion is made once, in Chapter 0657, by a man with a sheet, and never made again. Do not month a path mouth, weekday a path mouth or tide a path mouth. Do not thank anybody, in any chapter, in any speech, or in any state file this volume writes. Do not call either of the two holes in the arrangement small. Do not mend the separation and do not put them in a room as though they had not been four hundred paces apart. Do not print the manuscript total as anything but 1,836,946 and do not print a batch total as anything but 31,848.**

## Threads Volume 14 Batch 0002 hands to Volume 14 Batch 0003, items 557 to 570, and the last is what Batch 0003 must not do

**This section is written by the phase that wrote Volume 14 Batch 0002 and wrote its block, and it is Batch 0003's whole instruction. `outline/volume-14.md` is the plan the block was written against and is binding, `state/continuity.md` 1551 to 1560 are the facts, and item 570 is the list of what Batch 0003 must not do. Every figure was counted on the ten chapter files as they stand after this phase's last edit and the basis is named. Nothing above is renumbered and nothing above is resolved. Fourteen threads, numbered from 557, and the last is what the successor must not do. This section names the antagonist's office, and not the name, because the name is spent for this block and the cap for the volume is four.**

557. **The frame Batch 0003 inherits, and the two numbers it must derive rather than copy.** Day 930, the twenty-fifth of Mudmonth YR 318, a Sunday, is the only anchor, on the carried thirty-day month. **Block 0003 is days 2149 to 2202, fifty-four inclusive, that is the fourteenth of Fallowmonth YR 321 to the seventh of Wolfmonth YR 321, and every day of it is YR 321.** The day before the block, day 2148, is Fallowmonth the thirteenth and a Sunday, which is the day Chapter 0670 is written on. The month starts inside Block 0003 are **the first of Embermonth at day 2166 and the first of Wolfmonth at day 2196**, and **on the chain this project runs, which is the one that reconciles against all twenty datelines of this volume, Embermonth the first is a Thursday and a market morning and Wolfmonth the first is a Saturday and a market morning.** **The printed table at `outline/volume-13.md` §17 says Saturday for the first and Monday for the second and is wrong in both, and the five rows it gets wrong are all named at `state/continuity.md` 1561, and its month starts are right and its weekdays are the broken half.** **Every date is YR 321, because the year does not turn until day 2341, which is after the end of this volume.** Market days are the Saturdays and the Thursdays. **Derive the weekday half and the day-of-month half separately and report them as two figures and never as one, and a market-morning claim is a third.** `state/continuity.md` 1551.

558. **THE BLOCK'S ENGINE, and it is a sentence, and it is now the volume's engine in its hardest form.** **The second clause is the one that is working, and it has taken a horse off a mile and a half in nine seconds, and not one of the three clauses touches it, and the mechanism is a man in a livery being polite.** At item 1552. **And the climax's finding, which is new and which is the thing Block 0003 has to build on, is that none of the three clauses is broken and the ford is shut anyway, because the clauses are about a man who proposes and a man carrying out a paper is not a man who proposes.** At item 1559. **A successor may not soften either, may not find a clause that would have caught either, and may not have anybody say out loud that the three clauses were a mistake**, which is a Block 0004 beat and this volume's own argument.

559. **The instrument, unchanged, and what Block 0003 may add.** The full text is at `state/continuity.md` 1540 and 1541 and is not restated here. **The clause that is now working in the county is the second one**, at 1552. **The clause that has defeated the rule is the one that compels an answer without naming a place**, and it is at 1559. **What Block 0003 may add** is a Crown officer being competent and honest inside a room and a county discovering something about the answering. **What it may not do**: trace the abridgement, name who abridged the county copies, produce a third paper, or make the county copies whole. **The county's copies are a quarter of the instrument and the captain's fair copy has nine pages, and `Maud Trenchard` has a fair copy of the whole in a bag that she cannot put down anywhere, and none of those three facts may be joined this block** beyond the bag, which is hers.

560. **The delegation and the two answers, both on the page and neither of them an answer.** `Salla Dunnock, 38`, of the coast of returning roads, went home on the twenty-fifth of Longlight with a woman of about sixty, a boy of about nine and a carter, and reached the coast about four days later. She had three answers and none of them answers her question: **the badge-man's, that nobody has asked her anything and the Crown has not asked her anything either, which the badge-man says out loud on the twentieth of Rainmonth is the truest thing he has said in a year and is not an answer; and the ferry city's no, which is about them and not about her; and her own, at the ford, that the road is not shut and the not-asking is the part that is doing it.** Nobody in nine counties has been to the coast since she went, nobody has asked her what she said to about nine hundred people, and the answer she is carrying has not been tested. **Block 0003 may not send anybody to the coast and may not cut the twenty-second notch.**

561. **The small road's letter and the refusal of the register, both of which are load-bearing and neither of which may be undone.** At 1553 and 1554. **The letter goes on a leaf with no head over it in a drawer about eleven inches deep and it is read out at the boards on the first of every month, and the first of Fallowmonth was the day Chapter 0664 is written on, and the next first of a month after Block 0002 closes is the first of Embermonth at day 2166, which is eighteen days off, so a successor may have the copyist read it again on a first of a month and may not add to it, may not put it in the register, and may not enter a finding under any of the nine questions.** The count of nine crossings open and four shut weeks in two years is a count of a thing and is not converted into days, and the conversion was made once in Chapter 0657 and is not made again.

562. **SUPERSEDED BY 1609 AND BY `outline/volumes/volume-14-close.md` §6, WHICH RECOUNT THE BUDGET ON FIRST PRINTINGS. THE FIGURE *FIVE ARE SPENT AND ONE REMAINS* IN THIS ITEM WAS NEVER SUPPORTED BY THE NAMES IT LISTS, AND IT WAS CARRIED FORWARD UNEXAMINED THROUGH THREE BLOCKS. THE VOLUME CLOSED AT FOUR OF SIX WITH TWO SLOTS UNSPENT, being `Salla Dunnock`, `Halvard Rees`, `Maud Trenchard` and `Nell Drury`, with `Mara Vey`, `Hester Vane`, `Sera Quen`, `Tovan Kest` and `Edric Hunn` all returns. A SUCCESSOR MUST NOT INHERIT THE FIVE.** **`Maud Trenchard`, 56, and what she is, and what she will and will not do.** At 1555. **She is the one name this block spends, and the name budget for Volume 14 is six, so five are spent and one remains: `Mara Vey`, `Salla Dunnock`, `Halvard Rees` and `Maud Trenchard` are spent, `Hester Vane` is an established return and is not a spend, and ONE name remains for Blocks 0003, 0004 and 0005 between them.** A capitalised two-word sweep over the ten new files returns only `Aren Kest`, `Tovan Kest`, `Halvard Rees` and `Maud Trenchard`. **She will not come back. She goes home with nine pages in a bag and a decision she had not made when she arrived, and she says out loud that she is not going to refuse and is not going to pretend she has, and that the next instrument will be on a desk in the district in about nine days.** A successor may not bring her back into this county and may not have her refuse.

563. **The two holes in the arrangement, both restated out loud in this block, and neither called small.** At 1556. **Hole one, the standing one: the three clauses cover roads that can be written to, because every clause wants somebody found. Hole two: the three clauses are about a man doing a thing to a road, and a paper does not do a thing to a road, and there is nobody in nine counties whose business it is to say that a paper has no business here.** **Hole two now has a door in it, because the paper has turned into a man, and a man at a gate can be answered and the answer goes in a small book, and there is not one clause about a book and not one about a person who has been asked and has answered without anybody having offered them the chance to say no first.** **No chapter of Block 0003 may call either hole small, and no chapter may resolve either one, and neither may be filled in this block.** The name of the second hole was given by the woman of about thirty-six and not by the protagonist, and a third person giving it is not a new hole and may not be printed as one.

564. **The cost of Block 0002, in full, and it is a person, and the manner is the manuscript's.** At 1557. **A young man of about twenty-two, a carter's mate, went up the mile and a half with a barrow on the seventh of Harvestmonth and did not come down, and he is not got back, and nobody was killed and nobody has been told anything, and his name is on nothing.** A woman of about forty-nine who keeps a house four doors up the market street has a fortnight and has not worked out what it turns into, and the badge-man has asked her once and will not ask twice. **A successor may not find him, may not have him come back, may not kill anybody for shock, and may not have anybody say that nothing has happened.** **This road is the mile and a half where the made road stops being made and it is NOT the road that went quiet in Thawmonth, and the two must not be described in any vocabulary a reader could confuse, and the road that went quiet is not named, visited, sent to, or resolved in this block either.**

565. **The third thing from the ferry city, in full, and it is the engine of the second half of the volume.** At 1558. **A city that was never severed has resolved, without being asked, that if anybody in nine counties asks which place a Crown paper is meant, the city will answer and will name the place and will not refuse to name it, and it is going to do that in about nine days, and it will be asked by somebody who is not the Crown.** The badge-man has said out loud in a market street that **nine counties cannot refuse a thing a city says about it**, and a question was asked aloud in that market on the fifth of Fallowmonth and nobody answered it, and the words are **then the question is whether to ask them.** **The protagonist is not told in time to act on it: he is four miles down the lane, he is told at about the sixth hour by a man who did not send for him, and he says out loud that he is five hours late for the market and on time for the crossing and that there is no arrangement that would have put him in the room where a county thinks.** **The man of about thirty-four with a sheet says the second thing that matters: the only people in nine counties who can get to that crossing are four, and one of them is the badge-man, who said in a market street on the first of Harvestmonth that he will not ask anybody anything for a month.** **Block 0003 is the block in which somebody decides to ask them, and the outline's reversal is that the asking requires the paths to accept Crown authority and that a ferrywright from a city that was never severed stands up and says four sentences about a gate.**

566. **The protagonist's position at the end of Block 0002, in full, because a successor must not soften it.** **He has the finding and has had it for about ten weeks, dating from the eighth of Longlight, which is the only datable origin the manuscript gives, and has said it out loud exactly once, which was one true small thing about a ford, and about nine people turned round and looked at him while he said it.** He has been the man a thing arrives for for four years. **The badge-man asked him to be the name a warrant goes in a place for on the twelfth of Longlight, he said no and gave the reason, and the badge-man said in a street that he was not going to ask twice and that in about a month nine people were going to ask whether he had offered it.** **Nobody has asked him whether he wants to cross and nobody has said out loud that he can go further now.** He has said nothing in front of about nine people for eight weeks and about four people in every market street are waiting for him to speak and about five are not, and the badge-man has twice said out loud that they are waiting. **What has changed in this block: he has learned that the four-mark logic is a craft and not a discovery, and a county can say no to a man and not to a craft; and he has learned that the two halves of his county do not meet, that the four people who keep a mile and a half of it and the nine people in a market street are not the same people and have never been in the same lane in the same weather; and he has learned that he would be one of four who could get to that crossing, and that he cannot get to the room where the county thinks.** He still holds the rest of the finding and has still not said it.

567. **The separation, and it is open, and this block moved it once and did not mend it.** **In Chapter 0670, on the thirteenth of Fallowmonth, a Sunday, the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps the room came down the lane to the boards with the register under her arm, which she does not do on a day that is not a market morning, and stood at about four paces from the high end of the boards where Aren Kest was, and neither of them moved for the whole of the hour, and they said one thing to each other in about nine hours of that morning, and the thing was about the register: the fourth line is ruled and it is empty, and I know it is.** **Nobody said anything else to anybody and about four people at the boards understood that two people who keep separate things had said one sentence to each other in the open about a book, and about five understood that something had happened, and nobody thanked anybody for either of it.** The four hundred paces and the two doors stand and the work continues and the right to disagree stands. **The four and a half years are never referred to as four and a half years about the two of them and nothing between them is in any document, and no chapter of Block 0003 may mend it, may not put them in a room as though they had not been four hundred paces apart, and may not put a second exchange in the same chapter as a third one.** They were last in one room together with a third person in it in Chapter 0659 and nothing was mended then either.

568. **SUPERSEDED BY 1609 AND BY `outline/volumes/volume-14-close.md` §6, WHICH RECOUNT THE BUDGET ON FIRST PRINTINGS. THE FIGURE *FIVE ARE SPENT AND ONE REMAINS* IN THIS ITEM WAS NEVER SUPPORTED BY THE NAMES IT LISTS, AND IT WAS CARRIED FORWARD UNEXAMINED THROUGH THREE BLOCKS. THE VOLUME CLOSED AT FOUR OF SIX WITH TWO SLOTS UNSPENT, being `Salla Dunnock`, `Halvard Rees`, `Maud Trenchard` and `Nell Drury`, with `Mara Vey`, `Hester Vane`, `Sera Quen`, `Tovan Kest` and `Edric Hunn` all returns. A SUCCESSOR MUST NOT INHERIT THE FIVE.** **The name budget, the arithmetic, and what this block spent.** The budget for Volume 14 is six. **Block 0001 spent two: `Salla Dunnock` in Chapter 0652 and `Halvard Rees` in Chapter 0660. Block 0002 spent one: `Maud Trenchard` in Chapter 0663. `Mara Vey` is a return and not a spend, and is recorded at `state/continuity.md` 1538. `Hester Vane` is an established return from Volume 13 and is not a spend. FIVE are spent and ONE remains, and Blocks 0003, 0004 and 0005 have to live inside that one.** **The antagonist's name is at 0 in this block and the cap for the volume is FOUR, and Block 0001 spent one and Block 0003 has one and Block 0005 has one and the fourth only if a chapter requires it.** `Sabra Rolf` was spent at an earlier volume, `Gallow` at `chapters/volume-12/chapter-0552.md`, and neither may be used for a new first name.

569. **The measurements, and the two instruments a successor must not re-verify and the three it must.** **Do not recheck:** the file total of 34,987, the ten per-chapter totals, the ten bodies, the ten codas, the ten money columns, the ten price sets after the review repair, and their four-way footing, the collision result of zero against a set of **177 distinct** built by the declared extractor over the 660 prior files, the cross-file exact-duplicate count of 0, the one within-block duplicate and the fact that it is the letter read twice, and the eight-word run figures. **Recount rather than inherit:** the calendar, because the printed table at `outline/volume-13.md` §17 is wrong in five rows; **any money collision count, because a collision set is a set of numbers plus the extractor that built it, and the extractor for this block is at `state/continuity.md` 1560 and in the batch summary, and the scope is the whole file rather than the last two paragraphs, which is a change of scope and not a change of method;** and **the age of the protagonist, which is flagged and not acted on and which a volume close may resolve if it says it is doing one.**

570. **And what Batch 0003 must not do, in one list, and it is this thread and not a summary of the fourteen above.** **Do not name the antagonist again. Do not print the kin-term at all, in any form, in any mouth, because `chapter-0654.md` spends the volume's cap of four and the word is at 0 in Block 0002. Do not print the word that names the founding paper in any construction, in a document, in a quotation, in a mouth or in narration. Do not print a System panel without spending it deliberately and at most once, and do not print the word *stage*, the word *ledger* or the word *qualification*, and do not name the mechanism and do not say what a roadmark is. Do not look at the notebook's separate leaf or at the roadmark, and do not let another character notice the warmth of the left palm. Do not trace the abridgement of the copies, name who abridged them, or produce a third paper, and do not make the copies in the county whole. Do not print a figure for the column and do not count it. Do not spend either half of the standing defect, which is reserved for this block, and do not shorten the twenty-one days and do not say the four months. Do not fill or rule any head, put a finding in the register, turn or time the half leaf, lift or schedule the bar at the Low Gate, re-ask the four questions on its board, count the two in three, answer the rightness, or look at the mechanism. Do not get the road that went quiet back, do not name it, do not send anybody to it, do not have anybody come back from it, do not resolve it in either direction, and do not say it does not matter. Do not find the carter's mate of about twenty-two and do not have anybody say that nothing has happened. Do not reopen the shut road four hundred miles off, do not send anybody to the coast, and do not cut the twenty-second notch. Do not merge the three series or derive one from another, and do not change the band figure. Do not convert a path's open-crossing count into main-world days, and do not convert the three weeks again, and do not month, weekday or tide a path mouth. Do not thank anybody, in any chapter, in any speech, or in any state file this volume writes, and note that a Crown officer's formulaic courtesy at a gate is in the same category and is rewritten out. Do not call either of the two holes in the arrangement small and do not resolve either one. Do not mend the separation and do not put a second exchange between the two of them in any chapter. Do not print the manuscript total as anything but 1,871,933 and do not print a batch total as anything but 34,987. Do not write the united front as a plan that works, and do not have a ferrywright expose anything before she stands up in a room with about nine people in it and says four sentences about a gate, and do not have `Sera Quen` appear anywhere earlier in this block.**


## What the review repair over Volume 14 Batch 0002 hands to Batch 0003, items 571 to 574, and 574 is added to item 570 rather than replacing it

**Item 570 is the list of what Batch 0003 must not do and it stands whole and unrevised. These four items are additions to it and they are the things a successor would otherwise have to find by reading the ten files and finding them wrong.**

571. **A successor must not put the block's engine back where the review-repair pass took it out of it.** **The second clause is the one that is working, and the finding that it is, and that the mechanism is a man in a livery being polite, and that there is no clause in about nine hands about a person being asked, is stated once, in Chapter 0662, and only there.** Chapter 0661 states a different and weaker thing, which is that a paper which asks nothing gets an answer anyway, and it does so by the badge-man declining to read the three clauses in a lane a second time. **A successor may not walk the three clauses clause by clause in an early chapter of Block 0003 and reach the same place, because Chapter 0655 did that walk in the market street on the twelfth of Longlight and Chapter 0662 did it in the market on the twenty-third of Rainmonth, and that is twice, and three is a habit.**

572. **A successor must not find the missing lad, must not establish what happened to him, and must not let a character speculate that he may be at the top of a made road.** **The only evidence in the block is a crew man's account and his own correction of it, and both are on the page at `state/continuity.md` 1557 and in `chapter-0667.md`, and the account is that the lad came up behind the crew at about the fourth hour of the afternoon, went on with them about a mile to a gate that is on no map any of them has, and was not with them at about the seventh hour when the crew turned off the coast road for quarters.** Nobody has found him and nobody has entered him in anything.

573. **A successor must not print a figure for the column, and must also not print the column's rough size in a crowd.** The review found the block's own rough figure, about nine hundred, standing inside a market street that the same chapter counts at about four hundred people, and it was the market's figure being carried in a mouth that did not own it. **Nine counties's rough figure for a column is about nine hundred and it may be repeated as somebody's estimate, and it may not appear as a count of the people standing in a street.** The same applies to a crowd: about four hundred people in a market street, about two hundred of whom stop what they are doing, and no other figure.

574. **A successor must not repeat a chapter's closing beat.** The review found that the block's last page was a rerun of a page two chapters earlier, the mason and the top course and the mallet, and rebuilt it. **Three images belong to the road-crew chapter and to no other chapter in this block: the three courses of sill at the town's own gate, the line of stone on the bank, and the mallet on it.** The last page of a block is a consequence of the block's decision, and in this block the consequence of the decision is a name in a book and a woman standing in water at a gate. **And a successor must not close a chapter by saying that a thing it has already said is the whole of it, which the review found in two chapters and which the house has used deliberately exactly once, in the right-to-refuse sentence, and which is recorded at `state/continuity.md` 1508 and 1549.**

## Threads Volume 14 Batch 0003 hands to Volume 14 Batch 0004, items 575 to 592, and the last is what Batch 0004 must not do

**This section is written by the phase that wrote Volume 14 Batch 0003 and wrote its block, and it is Batch 0004's whole instruction. `outline/volume-14.md` is the plan the block was written against and is binding, §8 Block 4 and §5.1 are where Batch 0004's own work is, `state/continuity.md` 1567 to 1579 are the facts, and item 592 is the list of what Batch 0004 must not do. Items 557 to 574 are inherited whole and 570 and 571 to 574 are inherited as additions to it. Nothing above is renumbered and nothing above is resolved. This section names the antagonist's office and not the name, because the name was printed once in this block and the cap for the volume is four and one remains for Block 0005.**

575. **The frame Batch 0004 inherits, and the two numbers it must derive rather than copy.** Day 930, the twenty-fifth of Mudmonth YR 318, a Sunday, is the only anchor, on the carried thirty-day month. **Block 0004 is days 2203 to 2256, fifty-four inclusive, that is the eighth of Wolfmonth YR 321 to the FIRST of Hearthmonth YR 321, which is the last day of the block, and every day of it is YR 321.** The month starts inside Block 0004 are **the first of Frostmonth at day 2226, which on this chain is a Monday and not a market morning, and the first of Hearthmonth at day 2256, which on this chain is a Wednesday and not a market morning.** **The printed table at `outline/volume-13.md` §17 prints Wednesday for the first of Frostmonth and Saturday for the first of Hearthmonth, and it is wrong in both; its five wrong rows are named at `state/continuity.md` 1561 and two of them are these.** **Derive the weekday half and the day-of-month half separately and report them as two figures and never as one, and a market-morning claim is a third. The first of a month inside Block 0004 is therefore NOT a market morning on either occasion, and the second of them is the last day of the block, and a Batch 0004 writer must not assume either is a market morning.** `state/continuity.md` 1567.

576. **The united front, on the page, wrong, and not withdrawn, and the whole of what a successor needs to know about it.** At `state/continuity.md` 1569 in full. **Nine hundred words, nine numbered clauses, written on the twenty-ninth of Fallowmonth and left on a table under a cloth in the hired room over a saddler's with nobody's name on them and nobody's name taken off them.** The sixth clause is the wrong one. **A successor may not withdraw it, may not repair it, may not print the whole of it again, and may not have anybody revise it into a shape that works.** It is quoted in three short pieces only, **at `state/continuity.md` 1569 and in Chapters 0673, 0674 and 0676, and the clause text must not be reproduced a fourth time in one piece.**

577. **The reversal, four sentences, and the fact that it has not been answered.** At `state/continuity.md` 1572. **A road cannot be told anything because there is nobody at the end of it to be told, so a name on a road is a name a man can say at a gate for the rest of his life.** Nobody in nine counties has an answer to that. **A successor may not have anybody produce one, may not soften it, may not print those four sentences again, and may not have the ferrywright return, since she went back up the made road on the Wednesday of the eighteenth and refused a copy.**

578. **The standing defect is spent, both halves, and the figure did not move.** At `state/continuity.md` 1571. **The second half paid on the eighth of Embermonth in a form nobody in nine counties can refuse at a gate, and the badge-man did not move the figure and did not state the longer figure.** The question of whether being right early was a kindness was asked out loud and **answered by the woman of about fifty and not withdrawn anywhere, and the answer is not going to be improved on in Block 0004, and no chapter of Block 0004 may restate it.** **Block 0004 must not spend either half again, must not shorten the twenty-one days, must not state the four months, and must not move the figure.**

579. **The list, and what it is, and the difference between this block and Block 0004.** At `state/continuity.md` 1575 and 1571. **A printed list of about thirty counties in order with one road against each, given out of a room about nine days before the eighth of Embermonth by a man who read out two counties and the day and handed it across and did not read the rest of it.** **By the close of Block 0003 the forward party had taken about nine roads off it and had not read the last twenty-one.** **THE DIFFERENCE A SUCCESSOR MUST NOT CONFUSE: in Batch 0003 the two roads that sent nothing are NOT the two the column is standing on; in Block 0004 the two that send nothing ARE the two the column is standing on, and that change is Block 0004's beat and not this block's.**

580. **The three series and the second cost, both on the page, and both useless for the problem.** At `state/continuity.md` 1575. **A road that has decided something is not a return and is not a roll and is not a sheet going out to a district, and the copyist said it in a room over a saddler's on the tenth, and a man with a satchel said it in a market street on the seventeenth and said it better.** **The three series are unchanged, none is derived from another, the band figure of about a hundred and four on a fixed day has not changed, and about four hundred and nine parish rolls a month come in.** **Block 0004 may say the second cost once more and in a third mouth, and may not resolve it, and may not merge or derive anything.**

581. **The copyist's letter and the promise, and the fact that the promise has been discharged and is not to be revived.** **The small road's letter was read out at the boards on the twenty-third of Rainmonth in Chapter 0662 and again on the first of Harvestmonth in Chapter 0664, which is the volume's one long reprise at seventy-eight words and it stands and is not to be repaired.** **On the first of Embermonth she declined to read it a third time and gave the reason out loud, and on the first of Wolfmonth she said nine words, which are that it stands and that it is a decision and not a promise broken.** **A successor may not read it out again, may not add to it, may not put it in the register, and may not enter a finding under any of the nine questions.** The letter is on a leaf with no head over it in a drawer about eleven inches deep. **The count of nine crossings open and four shut weeks in two years is a count of a thing and is not converted into days by anybody, and the conversion was made once in Chapter 0657 and is not made again.**

582. **The answer nobody in nine counties can give, and what it is, and what a successor may not do with it.** At `state/continuity.md` 1574. **On the sixteenth of Embermonth a woman of about thirty-nine on a lane two days off said no at her own gate four feet before a man of about thirty-one asked her, and he wrote it on the back of that gate's page and not in the small book.** **She said she cannot promise to say it twice and can promise that about nine people on that lane will know somebody said it first.** **A successor may not have anybody find out whether the man came back, may not say what happened at the second gate on the fourteenth of Rainmonth, and may not turn one person saying no at one gate into a practice, a rule or a name.**

583. **The cost of Batch 0003, in full, and the manner is the manuscript's.** At `state/continuity.md` 1573. **About nine tons of cut stone in the low approach to a crossing nine miles off, put there on the fourth of Fallowmonth and not collected, and four of about nine households going over a bank in about nine minutes every time a loaded cart crosses, and nobody having said no to anything because there has never been a thing to say no to.** **A woman of about forty-four walked nine miles to say no to a paper before she was asked it and her reason is a crossing, a Tuesday, and a man in a livery who was polite about it.** **A successor may not lift the stone, may not have anybody remove it, may not have a Crown officer apologise for it, and may not have the woman of about forty-four change her mind in this volume.**

584. **The ordinary half, in full, because Batch 0004 owns the long recovery beat and must not rerun this one.** At `state/continuity.md` 1576. **Nine people turned out at a barrel drain nobody ordered, four of the nine could not lift the chain at the second pull and five could, the boy of about seventeen counted a hundred and forty barrow trips, and a man of about fifty-one said out loud in a lane that he has been standing in nine inches of water on Fridays for nine weeks and was told he would not be thanked for it and would not be argued with.** **The barrow drain on the mile and a half is not the culvert at the market town's own gate, and the three courses of sill, the line of stone on the bank and the mallet on it belong to `chapter-0668.md` and to no chapter of this block and to no chapter of Block 0004 except as something nobody picks up.**

585. **The block's close, and the three things that stand unrepaired, and the small concrete decision that is not the protagonist's.** At `state/continuity.md` 1577. **The plan is not withdrawn. The defect is paid and the figure has not moved. The answer about being early was a kindness and it was to him, and it stands. And a woman of about fifty-nine who keeps the second gate from the top of the mile and a half decided to go and ask somebody one question, and nobody sent her, and she knocked on a door at the top of that lane in the rain.** **What the person inside the door said is not in Chapter 0680 and is not to be invented here without a card that says so.**

586. **The separation, and it moved once in Block 0003 and was not mended.** **In Chapter 0680 the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps the room was on the thirteen stairs of the weigh-house with the register shut and did not come down, and Aren Kest was at the high end of the boards, and they did not speak, and it is said on the page that nine people in that market street noticed and about five did not.** **There has never been an exchange of words between them in this block, so there are none outstanding.** The four hundred paces and the two doors stand, the work continues, the right to disagree stands, **and nothing between them is in any document.** The four and a half years are never referred to as four and a half years about the two of them.

587. **SUPERSEDED BY 1609 AND BY `outline/volumes/volume-14-close.md` §6, WHICH RECOUNT THE BUDGET ON FIRST PRINTINGS. THE FIGURE *FIVE ARE SPENT AND ONE REMAINS* IN THIS ITEM WAS NEVER SUPPORTED BY THE NAMES IT LISTS, AND IT WAS CARRIED FORWARD UNEXAMINED THROUGH THREE BLOCKS. THE VOLUME CLOSED AT FOUR OF SIX WITH TWO SLOTS UNSPENT, being `Salla Dunnock`, `Halvard Rees`, `Maud Trenchard` and `Nell Drury`, with `Mara Vey`, `Hester Vane`, `Sera Quen`, `Tovan Kest` and `Edric Hunn` all returns. A SUCCESSOR MUST NOT INHERIT THE FIVE.** **The name budget, the arithmetic, and what this block spent.** The budget for Volume 14 is six. **Block 0001 spent two: `Salla Dunnock` in Chapter 0652 and `Halvard Rees` in Chapter 0660. Block 0002 spent one: `Maud Trenchard` in Chapter 0663. Block 0003 spent NONE.** `Mara Vey` is a return and not a spend, recorded at `state/continuity.md` 1538. `Hester Vane` is an established return from Volume 13 and is not a spend. **`Sera Quen` is an established return from Volume 01 and is not a spend.** **FIVE are spent and ONE remains, and it is not spent on the ferrywright and it goes to a person who cannot be a person without one.** `Sabra Rolf` was spent at an earlier volume, `Gallow` at `chapters/volume-12/chapter-0552.md`, and neither may be used for a new first name. **The antagonist's name is at 1 in this block and the cap for the volume is FOUR: one spent in Chapter 0655, one spent in Chapter 0675, one due in Block 0005, and the fourth only if a chapter requires it.**

588. **The measurements, and the two instruments a successor must not re-verify and the three it must.** **Do not re-verify:** the file total of 33,246, the ten per-chapter totals, the ten bodies, the ten codas, the ten money columns, the ten price sets, their four-way footing and their conversion, the collision result of zero, the cross-file and within-block exact-duplicate counts of zero, and the eight-word run figures. **Recount rather than inherit:** the calendar, because the printed table at `outline/volume-13.md` §17 is wrong in five rows and two of them fall inside Block 0004; **any money collision count, because a collision set is a set of numbers plus the extractor that built it, and the extractor for this block is at `state/continuity.md` 1579 and in the batch summary, and the scope is the whole file rather than the last two paragraphs**; and **the age of the protagonist, which is flagged and not acted on and which a volume close may resolve if it says it is doing one.**

589. **The manuscript after this block.** **1,905,179 words in six hundred and eighty chapters.** 1,871,933 in six hundred and seventy, recounted on the files this pass, plus 33,246. **Volumes 01 to 13 are 1,805,098 in six hundred and fifty chapters and did not move by one word**, because nothing outside `chapters/volume-14/` and the state files was touched. **Volume 14 is 100,081 in thirty chapters at a mean of 3,336, and its three blocks are 31,848, 34,987 and 33,246.**

590. **The standing prose debt has now been addressed three times and the ceiling is this volume's own, not Volume 13's.** Volume 14 Block 0001 ran at a mean of 3,185, Block 0002 at 3,404 and **Block 0003 at 3,325**, against Volume 13's five blocks at a mean of 2,679. **About sixty per cent of the manuscript is at under half the Volume 01 chapter length and Volumes 09 to 12 stand at 1,856 to 2,961 and remain unrepaired, and no chapter of Batch 0004 may be written as though the ceiling were Volume 13's.**

591. **What Batch 0004 is for, in one sentence, and it is not this block's job.** **A coalition that exists without a charter: two of the seven roads write and two send nothing, and the two that send nothing ARE the two the column is standing on, which is the difference from this block; the register gains its tenth question and still no finding; and the recovery beat is long, and it is the road crew work at the market town's own gate, and the fourth course of stone on the bank is not laid and nobody picks the mallet up.** `outline/volume-14.md` §8 Block 4 and §5.1.

592. **And what Batch 0004 must not do, in one list, and it is this thread and not a summary of the seventeen above. Items 570 and 571 to 574 are inherited whole and stand, and this list is written as additions to them and not as a replacement for them.** **Do not withdraw the united front and do not repair it and do not print the whole of the plan again in one piece, and do not print those four ferrywright sentences again.** Do not name the antagonist except for the one printing that remains, and use the words *that name* and *the Marshal's hand* for every other reference. Do not print the kin-term at all, in any form, in any mouth. Do not print the word that names the founding paper in any construction. Do not print a System panel without spending it deliberately and at most once, and do not print the word *stage*, the word *ledger* or the word *qualification*, and do not name the mechanism and do not say what a roadmark is. Do not look at the notebook's separate leaf or at the roadmark, and do not let another character notice the warmth of the left palm. Do not trace the abridgement of the copies, name who abridged them, produce a third paper, or make the county copies whole, and do not bring `Maud Trenchard` back into this county. Do not print a figure for the column and do not count it. Do not spend either half of the standing defect again, do not shorten the twenty-one days, do not state the four months, and do not move the figure. Do not improve on the answer about whether being early was a kindness. Do not fill or rule any head, rule a fifth line, put a finding in the register, turn or time the half leaf, lift or schedule the bar at the Low Gate, lay or schedule the fourth course, pick the mallet up, or re-ask the four questions on its board. Do not get the road that went quiet in Thawmonth back, do not name it, do not send anybody to it, do not have anybody come back from it, do not resolve it in either direction, and do not say it does not matter. Do not find the carter's mate of about twenty-two and do not have anybody say that nothing has happened to him. Do not reopen the shut road four hundred miles off, do not send anybody to the coast, and do not cut the twenty-second notch. Do not merge the three series or derive one from another, and do not change the band figure. Do not convert a path's open-crossing count into main-world days, do not convert the three weeks again, and do not month, weekday or tide a path mouth. Do not thank anybody, in any chapter, in any speech, or in any state file this volume writes. Do not call either of the two holes in the arrangement small and do not resolve either one. Do not mend the separation and do not put an exchange between them in any chapter. Do not read the small road's letter out again. Do not lift the nine tons of stone and do not have anybody remove it. Do not have the woman of about forty-four change her mind, and do not have the woman of about fifty-nine's knock answered in a way that turns one question into a practice. Do not confuse this block's two roads that sent nothing with Block 0004's, which are the two the column is standing on.

## Volume 14, Batch 0005 hand-off, written by Batch 0004, items 593 to 611

593. **THE FRAME BATCH 0005 INHERITS, AND THE TWO NUMBERS IT MUST DERIVE RATHER THAN COPY.** Day 930, the twenty-fifth of Mudmonth YR 318, a Sunday, is the only anchor, on the carried thirty-day month. **Block 0005 is days 2257 to 2310 inclusive, fifty-four days, that is the second of Hearthmonth YR 321 to the twenty-fifth of Goatmonth YR 321, and 2310 is the last day of the volume and every day of it is YR 321, because the year does not turn until day 2341.** Month starts inside Block 0005 are **the first of Goatmonth at day 2286, a Monday and not a market morning, and that is the only month start inside the block.** **The day before the block, day 2256, is the first of Hearthmonth, a Wednesday, and not a market morning, and that is the day Chapter 0690 was written on.** Market days are the **Saturdays and the Thursdays**, and there are about eight of them in the fifty-four days of this block. **Derive the weekday half and the day-of-month half separately and report them as two figures and never as one, and a market-morning claim is a third. Do not read a weekday off the printed table at `outline/volume-13.md` §17, which is wrong in five rows, and the fifth of those five, day 2256, is the day before this block.** `state/continuity.md` 1580.

594. **THE UNITED FRONT, ON THE PAGE, WRONG, AND NOT WITHDRAWN, AND THE WHOLE OF WHAT A SUCCESSOR NEEDS.** At `state/continuity.md` 1569 in full and unchanged. **Nine hundred words, nine numbered clauses, on a table in the hired room over a saddler's under a cloth that nobody has established who put there, with nobody's name on them and nobody's name taken off them, and two people refused a copy and neither was argued with.** The sixth clause is the wrong one and it is a Sundering with a different word at the front of it, and **in Chapter 0684 the protagonist said out loud in a market street that the sixth clause is wrong and that he is not going to change it, and nobody in nine counties has an answer to it.** **A successor may not withdraw it, may not repair it, may not print the whole of it again, may not print the sixth clause again in one piece, and may not have anybody revise it into a shape that works.**

595. **THE REVERSAL, FOUR SENTENCES, AND THE FACT THAT IT HAS NOT BEEN ANSWERED.** At `state/continuity.md` 1572. **A road cannot be told anything because there is nobody at the end of a road to be told anything, so a name on a road is a name a man can say at a gate for the rest of his life, and that is what a Sundering is.** **A successor may not have anybody produce an answer, may not soften it, may not print those four sentences again, and may not have the ferrywright return, and she does not appear in any of Batch 0004's ten files.**

596. **THE COALITION THAT HAS NO DOCUMENT, AND ITS FINDING, IN FULL.** At `state/continuity.md` 1581 and 1582. **Nine counties has about nine people and no document, and the finding of Block 0004 is that a thing with no document cannot be signed, cannot be given away and cannot be taken off anybody, and that this is not a failure of the arrangement but the shape of it.** **The badge-man said in nine words twice that a thing with no head is not a smaller thing, it is a harder thing to get at, and that nobody in this county is going to name the thing, because naming a thing is how a thing gets got at.** The word *charter* is at 0 in Block 0004 and the manuscript's word for it is a thing with no name at the top of it. **Block 0005 is the block in which the coalition wins, and the outline's shape is that the coalition's authority is entirely negative and entirely local and it wins by refusing to name a road, and that is consistent with the finding and is not an answer to the reversal.**

597. **THE TENTH QUESTION AND THE STANDING HEADS, IN FULL.** At `state/continuity.md` 1584. **The register holds ten questions and no finding under any of them, and the tenth question is, on the page: of a thing with no head, and of a road that will not be named, what may be said to have been decided, and by whom it may be brought to.** The day-book stands at eleven entries. **The fourth ruled head over a place where a finding goes is empty and has been empty for about eleven months, the copyist has said out loud in three months and in two rooms that she is not going to rule a fifth head and that neither is anybody else, the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps the room says the fourth head ought not to be there and is right and is not going to stop saying it and is refused in four words.** **A successor may not fill the fourth head, may not rule a fifth head, may not put a finding under the tenth question, and may not turn the half leaf.**

598. **THE SECOND MOUTH AND THE TWO ROADS THAT SENT NOTHING, IN FULL, AND THE DIFFERENCE A SUCCESSOR MUST NOT CONFUSE.** At `state/continuity.md` 1585. **Two of the seven roads have written and two have sent nothing, and THE TWO THAT SENT NOTHING ARE THE COAST ROAD AND THE FOUR MILES AT THE FORD, AND THEY ARE THE TWO THE COLUMN IS STANDING ON.** **In Batch 0003 the two that sent nothing were the waterworks country and the glass country and were NOT the two the column was on, and a successor must not confuse the two states.** A road that has written is not a return and is not a roll and is not a sheet going out to a district, the fact that a road answered goes into nothing on Wednesday and is still in nothing on Thursday, **and the copyist has said this in four markets and in a room over a saddler's and in a market street in a fourth form, and Block 0005 may say it once more in a fifth mouth and may not resolve it.**

599. **THE SENTENCE, THE WIDOW OF THE WITNESS CLAUSE, AND THE MAN WHO WILL NOT GIVE A NAME, IN FULL.** At `state/continuity.md` 1586. **A man of about fifty-two who keeps four miles of made road under contract for about nineteen years has said out loud in a market street that he will not name a witness, and has said that the man who said a road might be shut has not shown that it may be shut, and that in four and a half years not one road has been shut.** **The badge-man has made that sentence the block's by saying it twice out loud in a market street in the man's own words, and his reason is that in about nine days a serjeant is going to arrive with a sheet and a question, and about nine people in nine counties are going to be asked which of the two they want, and he is not going to let anybody be asked that in a room.** **Nobody has said out loud whether a clause that cannot be used is the best thing this county has built in four and a half years or the end of it, and Block 0005 may put that question to a market street again and may not answer it.**

600. **THE COST OF BLOCK 0004, IN FULL, AND THE HOUSE'S MANNER.** At `state/continuity.md` 1587. **A woman of about forty-nine who keeps a kitchen and four beds on the made road lost a fortnight of a hired boy of about fourteen at fourpence a day and two beds of trade out of four, because a man of about thirty-one in a livery asked her for a witness on the twenty-third of Wolfmonth, was refused politely, and came back on the twenty-seventh for a person who could read a number out loud, and found out in her own yard, and the boy went on the twenty-eighth.** Nobody was hurt, nobody was killed, nobody was told anything, the boy is in no danger, **and she said the word twice is the wrong word and that she has not got a better one. A successor may not have the boy come back, may not have him found, may not have anybody say that nothing has happened to him, and may not turn the two visits at one door into a practice or a rule.**

601. **THE COIN AND ITS PRICE, IN FULL, AND THAT IT IS THE ONLY ASSET THE COALITION HAS.** At `state/continuity.md` 1588. **About nine people in nine counties will get up and go to a gate and say no in the open in daylight and leave a paper where a person can find it, and nobody has ever asked them to do it as a body, and the reason is that the asking is a head.** The price of it is four named things: the woman of about thirty-nine at a gate two days off, four households at a crossing nine miles off, a boy of about fourteen in no danger, and four people on a Friday at a cut who are not thanked for it. **The badge-man has said in nine words that nobody asks and the nine turn up, and a man of about thirty-three who sells cloth three doors up has nine names in his head and has refused to write them down, and four people in that market street asked him for the list and five did not.** **Block 0005's holding is this coin being spent without being asked, and a successor may not turn it into a muster, a list, a head or a name at the top of a thing.**

602. **THE ORDINARY HALF AND THE STANDING CONDITION OF THE CUT, IN FULL, BECAUSE BLOCK 0005 MUST NOT RERUN IT.** At `state/continuity.md` 1589 and 1584's neighbourhood. **Three courses of sill under the silt at the market town's own gate, the middle course gone and not cracked and getting wider every week for about eleven weeks, about nine feet of silt cleared on the first of Frostmonth in the rain by about nine people and a crew of nine, and the stone for a fourth course lying in a line on the bank with the mallet on top of it where anybody coming in can see it.** **The fourth course is not laid, the mallet is not picked up, and nobody is scheduled to pick it up. The three images belong to `chapter-0668.md` and to `chapter-0686.md` and to no other chapter in this volume: the three courses of sill, the line of stone on the bank, and the mallet on it.** **A successor may show them once, as the standing condition of the work, and may not use them as a closing beat in three chapters, and may not lay the fourth course and may not pick the mallet up.** The boy of about seventeen decided on the first of Frostmonth that he is not going to be a mason and said he would be at a sand yard on Thursday. **The barrow drain on the mile and a half is not the culvert at the market town's own gate and the two must not be described in a vocabulary a reader could confuse.**

603. **THE CAPTAIN'S DIFFICULTY, IN FULL, AND THAT NOBODY IN THIS VOLUME CALLS HIM EITHER THING.** At `state/continuity.md` 1590. **A captain of about forty-four with a sealed warrant, honest, competent, not a knave and not stupid, who has been taking one road in one county at a time off a list of about thirty since the eighth of Embermonth, has not read the last twenty-one, and has a serjeant somewhere every three days with a sheet and about four men and no warrant of his own.** He has nine men on a road in this county and about nine he cannot spare. **The instrument is not the difficulty and the shape is, and a thing with no end in it is a thing a man cannot be wrong about.** He said in a market street that he would like nine more mistakes. **A successor may not have anybody say out loud that the three clauses were a mistake, may not make him a knave, may not have him apologise for anything, and may not have a Crown officer's formulaic courtesy at a gate put back in.**

604. **THE SMALL CONCRETE DECISION OF THE BLOCK 0004 CLOSE, IN FULL, AND THE THREE THINGS THAT STAND UNREPAIRED.** At `state/continuity.md` 1591. **The united front, the tenth question with no finding, the bar of wood at the foot of the lane by the Low Gate, and the fourth course of stone are the four things that stand, and none of them is repaired and none of them is scheduled.** **A man of about sixty-two with a wain and a team of four has walked his fourth and best horse into a field four miles off, has said that he is not going to say yes a fifth time and is not going to say no either, and has said the price of it out loud, which is the sand contract for Tuesdays, about nine days a year and the whole of what that field is for.** **A successor may not have the horse come back out of the field as a decision, may not have the man explain himself twice, and may not have anybody thank him for it.**

605. **THE RELATIONSHIP, AND WHAT IT MAY AND MAY NOT DO IN BLOCK 0005.** At `state/continuity.md` 1592. **Aren Kest is in all ten of Batch 0004's files. There is no exchange of words between him and the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps the room in any of them, and there were none outstanding at the close of the block before this one.** He does not knock on a shut room, and he stood outside a shut room in the rain in Chapter 0683 and was on a step and said nothing in Chapter 0688. **The four hundred paces and the two doors stand, nothing between them is in any document, and the four and a half years are never referred to as four and a half years about the two of them.** **The separation is a resolution and it is not mended in this volume, and `outline/volume-14.md` §10 item 5 requires the two of them to work together and keep the argument in Block 0005, and that is compatible with the four hundred paces, and a successor may put them in one room with a third person in it and may not mend anything.**

606. **SUPERSEDED BY 1609 AND BY `outline/volumes/volume-14-close.md` §6, WHICH RECOUNT THE BUDGET ON FIRST PRINTINGS. THE FIGURE *FIVE ARE SPENT AND ONE REMAINS* IN THIS ITEM WAS NEVER SUPPORTED BY THE NAMES IT LISTS, AND IT WAS CARRIED FORWARD UNEXAMINED THROUGH THREE BLOCKS. THE VOLUME CLOSED AT FOUR OF SIX WITH TWO SLOTS UNSPENT, being `Salla Dunnock`, `Halvard Rees`, `Maud Trenchard` and `Nell Drury`, with `Mara Vey`, `Hester Vane`, `Sera Quen`, `Tovan Kest` and `Edric Hunn` all returns. A SUCCESSOR MUST NOT INHERIT THE FIVE.** **THE NAME BUDGET, THE ARITHMETIC, AND THE ONE THAT IS LEFT.** The budget for Volume 14 is six. **Block 0001 spent two, Block 0002 spent one, Block 0003 spent none and Block 0004 spent NONE.** **FIVE are spent and ONE remains, and it goes to a person who cannot be a person without one, and `outline/volume-14.md` gives the last name to Block 0005.** **The antagonist's name is at 0 in Block 0004 and the volume cap is FOUR, and exactly ONE printing remains, and it belongs to Block 0005.** `Sabra Rolf` and `Gallow` may not be used for a new first name, and a capitalised two-word sweep over Block 0005's ten files must return only established names and the two words for a made road.

607. **THE CALENDAR, THE TABLE THAT IS WRONG, AND THE ONE INCONSISTENCY THAT IS STILL FLAGGED AND NOT ACTED ON.** At `state/continuity.md` 1561 and at `state/current.md`. **The printed calendar table at `outline/volume-13.md` §17 is wrong in five rows and the five are day 2106, day 2136, day 2166, day 2196 and day 2256, and the fifth of those is the day before Block 0005.** The month starts in that table are the reliable half and the weekdays are the broken half. **And one inconsistency is still flagged and not acted on: `state/continuity.md`'s hard canon fixes Aren Kest at thirty-two in YR 312 and `outline/series.md`'s time ledger puts the series across about twelve years, which would make him forty-one on the last day of this volume. This volume carries thirty-two forward, and Tovan Kest is placed in his late twenties on the same basis, and Sera Quen at about thirty-four. The reconciliation belongs to a volume close that says it is doing one.**

608. **THE STANDING PROSE DEBT, AND THE CEILING FOR BLOCK 0005.** **Volume 14 Block 0001 ran at a mean of 3,185, Block 0002 at 3,404, Block 0003 at 3,325 and Block 0004 at 3,696, against Volume 13's five blocks at a mean of 2,679.** About sixty per cent of the manuscript is at under half the Volume 01 chapter length and Volumes 09 to 12 stand at 1,856 to 2,961 and remain unrepaired. **No chapter of Block 0005 may be written as though the ceiling were Volume 13's, and Block 0005 is the climax, so its action pages belong at the top of this volume's range and not below it.**

609. **THE MANUSCRIPT AFTER BLOCK 0004, AND THE THREE INHERITED FIGURES A SUCCESSOR MUST RECOUNT.** **1,942,137 words in six hundred and ninety chapters.** 1,905,179 in six hundred and eighty, recounted on the files this pass, plus 36,958. **Volumes 01 to 13 are 1,805,098 in six hundred and fifty chapters and did not move by one word**, because nothing outside `chapters/volume-14/` and the state files was touched. **Volume 14 is 137,039 in forty chapters at a mean of 3,426, and its four blocks are 31,848, 34,987, 33,246 and 36,958.** Do not re-verify: the file total of 36,958, the ten per-chapter totals, the ten bodies, the ten codas, the ten money columns, their four-way footing and their conversion, the collision result of zero, the cross-file and within-block exact-duplicate counts of zero, and the eight-word run figures. **Recount rather than inherit: the calendar, because the printed table is wrong in five rows and one of them falls immediately before this block; any money collision count, because a collision set is a set of numbers plus the extractor that built it, and the extractor for Block 0004 is at `state/continuity.md` 1595 with a union of 199 and a free set of 199 of 199, and the scope is the whole file rather than the last two paragraphs; and the age of the protagonist, which is flagged and not acted on and which the volume close may resolve if it says it is doing one.**

610. **WHAT BATCH 0005 IS FOR, IN ONE SENTENCE, AND IT IS THE CLIMAX.** **Nine days of held crossings, the column does not take the ferry city and does not take the works because nobody in nine counties will name a road to him, Aren Kest lets a bridge go on his own authority in about nine minutes to stop a forced cull, Tovan Kest stands in a street four days later and refuses to blame the path communities for it in public and refuses to let anybody blame Aren Kest either, the Gatehouse is exposed, and the volume's last main-world page is a man four days off on a made road with a stick, hearing a bell answer across water he did not ring.** `outline/volume-14.md` §8 Block 5, §9 and §11. **Block 0005 spends the last of the six names and the one remaining printing of the antagonist's name.**

611. **And what Batch 0005 must not do, in one list, and it is this thread and not a summary of the seventeen above. Items 570 and 571 to 574 are inherited whole and stand, and this list is written as additions to them and not as a replacement for them.** **Do not name the antagonist except for the one printing that remains, and use the words *that name* and *the Marshal's hand* for every other reference, and do not exceed the volume cap of four. Do not print the kin-term at all, in any form, in any mouth, in any chapter of this volume, because `chapter-0654.md` spends the volume's cap of four and the word is at 0 in Blocks 0002, 0003 and 0004. Do not print the word that names the founding paper in any construction. Do not print a System panel without spending it deliberately and at most once, and do not print the word *stage*, the word *ledger* or the word *qualification*, and do not name the mechanism and do not say what a roadmark is; and note that *charter*, *custodian* and the whole *fail* family are at 0 across Block 0004 and the house treats them as zero, so *charter* is not to be used for the coalition unless a card says so. Do not look at the notebook's separate leaf or at the roadmark, and do not let another character notice the warmth of the left palm. Do not trace the abridgement of the copies, name who abridged them, produce a third paper, or make the county copies whole, and do not bring `Maud Trenchard` back into this county. Do not print a figure for the column and do not count it, and do not print the rough nine hundred as a count of the people in a street, and remember that a market street in this county is about four hundred people of whom about two hundred stop. Do not spend either half of the standing defect again, do not shorten the twenty-one days, do not state the four months, do not move the figure, and do not improve on the answer about whether being right early was a kindness. Do not withdraw or repair the united front, do not print the whole of it again, and do not print the sixth clause in one piece. Do not answer the ferrywright's four sentences and do not have her return. Do not fill or rule any head, put a finding under the tenth question, turn or time the half leaf, lift or schedule the bar at the Low Gate, **lay or schedule the fourth course, or pick the mallet up**, and do not use the three courses of sill, the line of stone and the mallet as a closing beat in three chapters. Do not get the road that went quiet in Thawmonth back, do not name it, do not send anybody to it, do not have anybody come back from it, do not resolve it in either direction, and do not say it does not matter. Do not find the carter's mate of about twenty-two, do not have him come back, and do not have anybody say that nothing has happened to him. Do not reopen the shut road four hundred miles off, do not send anybody to the coast, and do not cut the twenty-second notch. Do not merge the three series or derive one from another, do not change the band figure, and do not put a number in the drawer. Do not convert a path's open-crossing count into main-world days, do not convert the three weeks again, and do not month, weekday or tide a path mouth. Do not thank anybody, in any chapter, in any speech, or in any state file this volume writes, and note that a Crown officer's formulaic courtesy at a gate is in the same category. Do not call either of the two holes in the arrangement small and do not resolve either one. Do not mend the separation and do not put an exchange between Aren Kest and the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps the room in any chapter. Do not read the small road's letter out again. Do not lift the nine tons of stone and do not have anybody remove it. Do not have the woman of about forty-four change her mind, and do not answer the knock at the top of the mile and a half in a way that turns one question into a practice; **and note that neither of those two women appears in any of Block 0004's ten files and a successor should not have to go and find them.** Do not confuse Block 0005's two roads that sent nothing with Block 0003's, and do not turn the coin into a muster, a list, a head or a name at the top of a thing. Do not print the manuscript total as anything but 1,942,137, do not print this block's total as anything but 36,958, and do not print Volume 14 as anything but 137,039, and recount all three before relying on them and again after your own last prose edit, because a repair to the pages of Batch 0004 moved these three numbers in four files and left them stale, which is recorded at `state/continuity.md` 1597. And do not introduce a new final enemy, and do not add a chapter or a block to this volume.
