NO CHAPTER WAS REWRITTEN, NO PARAGRAPH OF SCENE PROSE WAS REPLACED, AND THE DIFF IS FOURTEEN CHANGED LINES AND SIX FEWER WORDS.** **WHAT A SUCCESSOR MUST KNOW IS THAT NONE OF THE ELEVEN MOVED THE STORY: neither refusal moved, the acceptance did not move, the thing at the left hand did not move, nobody looked over the ninth of the nine stones, the one sheet of paper did not move, the four holes did not move, the separation did not mend and did not worsen, thirty-two stands, all ten money columns foot unchanged at 55, 56, 57, 58, 59, 136, 166, 232, 236 and 332, the thank family is still 156 and every one of the 156 is a negation, `Edric Hunn` is still at 0 across the ten files and BOTH OF THE VOLUME'S TWO PRINTINGS STAND UNSPENT FOR BLOCK 0005, AND THE NAME BUDGET IS STILL FOUR OF SIX SPENT WITH TWO SLOTS FOR BLOCK 0005.**

673. **THE TWO FIGURES A SUCCESSOR INHERITS FROM THIS BLOCK ARE NOW THESE TWO, AND THE OLD ONES ARE WITHDRAWN.** **Batch 0004 is 33,058 words, per file 3,312 / 3,183 / 3,308 / 3,224 / 3,176 / 3,433 / 3,618 / 3,285 / 3,435 / 3,084, mean 3,305 truncated and 3,306 rounded, minimum 3,084 at `chapter-0740.md` and maximum 3,618 at `chapter-0737.md`. Volume 15 is 134,178 in forty chapters. The manuscript is 2,111,708 in seven hundred and forty chapters. 33,064, 134,184, 2,111,714, 3,078, 3,321, 3,233, 3,429 and the claim that the block mean is exact on either convention were correct before this pass and are NOT correct after it. THE FOUR FIGURES THAT NEVER MOVED AND MAY BE INHERITED ARE 1,977,530, 172,432, 32,404 AND 34,009.**

674. **THE HEARTHMONTH RULING, AND IT IS SETTLED AND A SUCCESSOR IS NOT TO RE-OPEN IT.** **A BARE `Hearthmonth` IN A CHAPTER OF VOLUME 15 DATED IN YR 322 MEANS HEARTHMONTH YR 321, DAYS 2256 TO 2285, WHICH IS THE LAST HEARTHMONTH BEFORE THE VOLUME OPENED ON DAY 2311, AND IT IS A PAST MONTH.** Every past use in the forty files is correct on that reading, including the cloth on the bench at the low end of the boards, which nine files date to the middle of Hearthmonth. **HEARTHMONTH YR 322 IS DAY 2616 AND IT IS INSIDE NO CHAPTER OF THIS VOLUME, WHICH ENDS ON DAY 2610, AND IT MAY NEVER BE USED AS A PAST ANCHOR.** `*Before Hearthmonth*` remains a future reference and the protagonist's decision about going up the lane before Hearthmonth remains PLOTTED AND LOAD-BEARING at `chapter-0709.md` and `chapter-0710.md`, and NO CHAIN THAT PUT HEARTHMONTH IN THE PAST MAY BE ADOPTED TO FIX ANYTHING. **THE REPAINT OF THE BOARDS IS LEFT UNDATED AND MAY NOT BE RE-DATED TO A HEARTHMONTH.** TWO RESIDUALS ARE RECORDED RATHER THAN REPAIRED AND ARE THE CLOSE'S BUSINESS, NOT BATCH 0005'S: `chapter-0709.md` prints Hearthmonth *eleven weeks away*, which neither year gives, and the cloth predates the boards by about a hundred and twenty days on the YR 321 reading, which is odd but is not a calendar impossibility. Full text at `state/continuity.md` 1682.

675. **THE VOLUME-CLOSE RECORD IS WRITTEN BY THE BLOCK 0005 PHASE, AND THAT IS THE PROJECT'S OWN ESTABLISHED PRACTICE AND NOT A DEPARTURE FROM IT.** The review of Batch 0004 raised the question, because `AGENTS.md` says that a phase which finds a volume complete creates exactly one volume-close prompt, while `workspace/volume-15/batch-0005/PROMPT.md` §5 and §8 instruct Batch 0005 to write `outline/volumes/volume-15-close.md` itself and to create no further phase. **BOTH READINGS WERE CHECKED AGAINST WHAT THIS REPOSITORY HAS ACTUALLY DONE AND THE PROMPT'S READING IS THE ONE THAT HAS BEEN FOLLOWED: `workspace/volume-14/` holds only batch prompts, `batch-0002` through `batch-0005`, and there is no close-prompt directory for Volume 14 anywhere under `workspace/`, and `outline/volumes/volume-14-close.md` exists. THE LAST BATCH OF A VOLUME HAS WRITTEN THAT VOLUME'S CLOSE RECORD IN THIS PROJECT, EVERY TIME IT HAS BEEN TESTED.** The prompt therefore stands as written and `AGENTS.md` was not edited, because `AGENTS.md` is controller-owned and because the generic rule and the specific instruction are not actually in conflict: a phase that finishes the manuscript's last chapter has not found a volume complete at the moment it starts, so the volume is not yet complete and the close is correctly emitted at the end of the same phase. **A SUCCESSOR IS NOT TO CREATE A SIXTH BATCH AND NOT TO CREATE A VOLUME-CLOSE PROMPT.** The Volume 10 gap remains a records gap at item 539 and is not to be filled by this.

676. **THE TEN FACTS A SUCCESSOR INHERITS FROM BLOCK 0005, IN SHORT, AND THIS IS THE WHOLE OF THE HAND-OFF OUT OF VOLUME 15.** **One,** a light has been on the top of that shoulder at about the fourth hour every morning since the twenty-sixth of Embermonth, and it left nine feet by nine hundred yards of dead grass in the line of a boundary, and the scar is on the back of a roadkeeper's board in a cart shed and about nine people in nine counties can point at it. **Two,** the Many-Handed is crossed and the volume's one System panel is spent, and the panel is eleven words, carries no number, and stands alone at `chapter-0746.md`: *What is held by one hand is taken by one hand.* **Three,** the cost is on the page and is not cured: his leg, his hearing on the side the stick is on, and the fact that he cannot go up that hill on his own. **Four,** the origin is answered and the answer is a boundary and a refusal, it is 122 words at `chapter-0748.md`, it contains no body and no reunion and no notes, and the scar is in the boundary and not in the kerbed road. **Five,** both refusals of Block 0004 stand, the acceptance stands, neither has been argued with, and neither of the two men who gave them has been asked anything since. **Six,** nobody is past the ninth of the nine stones and the dead grass stops there. **Seven,** a leaf with one thumb on it says a place is shutting its own end before Hearthmonth and is not asking anybody for anything. **Eight,** the antagonist's name is printed twice in the volume and both printings are documents and both are spent. **Nine,** two incompatible answers to who is going to hold nine hundred yards of wall have been given out loud and they do not agree. **Ten,** a request from the road office four hundred miles off for a name and a copy of a drawing is in an office four days off that has been unable to answer anything since the first of Goatmonth YR 321. `state/continuity.md` 1683 to 1694.

677. **THE MANY-HANDED AND THE PANEL AND THE COST, IN FULL, AND WHAT A VOLUME 16 WRITER MAY NOT UNDO.** The crossing is at `chapter-0746.md`, on the twenty-fifth of Wolfmonth, day 2580, at about the fourth hour, on the grass below the mapped ground, with three other people on it. **The asking is what makes it cost, and the four asks of the first of Wolfmonth are the reason the three are there**, and the whole of the block's method is that a fact one man saw at the fourth hour of a morning is one man's fact and that four people who have each seen a thing once have not seen it between them. The three who came said yes in their own trades and not as a favour, and the man of forty-one was asked and did not answer, which is not a refusal. **Ten routes are held and no route is opened, closed or cut, the mechanism is not explained, no chapter says what a roadmark is, and the separate leaf of the notebook that says *a person* is still not looked at.** **A successor may not print a second System panel in Volume 16 without a new volume budget, may not say that the crossing can be undone by asking fewer people, and may not have the leg or the ear get better.**

678. **THE SCAR, THE MAP, AND THE ORIGIN, IN FULL, AND THE THREE WALLS A VOLUME 16 WRITER MAY NOT GET ROUND.** **The scar is nine feet wide and about nine hundred yards long, it is in the grass, it is dead inside its own width and standing outside it, it is not cured, it is not going to close over, and nobody in nine counties is going to be told that it is a small thing.** **The map is the back of a roadkeeper's board in a cart shed four days off, and the roadkeeper has said out loud in thirty-five words that he is not going to draw it again and not going to give the board away, and about nine people have stood in front of it and nobody in nine counties has asked him for it.** **The origin is a boundary and a refusal: the oldest road in this county does not go anywhere, it stopped where the boundary began, the boundary is nine hundred years old, nine stones at one height across the top of it are a thing a man on the made road at the bottom could read, and nobody is there to find.** **WALL ONE: nobody has been past the ninth of the nine stones and the dead grass stops there, and a successor may not show the far side of those stones as though it had been earned here.** **WALL TWO: the kerbed road is whole and the boundary is not, and a successor may not turn that into a story about a road that was closed, because the road was not closed and was never a road to anywhere.** **WALL THREE: there is no body and there are no notes and nobody is coming up that lane with an answer, and a successor may not produce Ilyra Kest's route notes, quote them, mention them, or know that they exist.**

679. **THE NAME BUDGET FOR VOLUME 15 CLOSED AT FOUR OF SIX SPENT AND TWO UNSPENDED, AND THIS BLOCK SPENT NONE, WHICH IS A FACT AND NOT A FINDING.** `Orme Skell`, `Dav Rundle`, `Tarr Lyster` and `Corve Sallow` are the four spends, all in Batches 0001, 0002 and 0004. **Batch 0005 spends NO name and a capitalised two-word sweep over its ten files returns nothing that is not an established name, a place, or an artefact of the money coda.** The two unnamed people who walk the boundary on the first of Frostmonth are a man of about fifty-one and a woman of about forty-four and they are carries, not spends. **THE VOLUME'S NAME BUDGET THEREFORE CLOSES AT FOUR OF SIX WITH TWO SLOTS UNSPENDED, and a Volume 16 writer must read `outline/volume-16.md` for its own budget and must not spend this volume's two.** `Sabra Rolf` and `Gallow` may not be used for a new first name and `Iven` and `Marrow` may not be used as a surname.

680. **THE ANTAGONIST'S NAME IS SPENT TWICE IN VOLUME 15 AND BOTH PRINTINGS ARE DOCUMENTS, SO THE CAP IS CLOSED.** Blocks 0001, 0002, 0003 and 0004 printed it zero times. **Block 0005 printed it twice, at `chapter-0745.md` and `chapter-0749.md`, both times inside a printed sheet read out loud at the boards, and both times in the volume's two cap at `outline/volume-15.md` §16 item 4.** Every other reference in the volume is *that name* or *the Marshal's hand*, and the single reference to the hand in Batch 0002 is at `chapter-0720.md`. **No chapter of Volume 15 puts the antagonist in a room and no chapter of Volume 16 may either until its own budget says so.**

681. **THE CALENDAR AS IT STANDS AT THE CLOSE OF VOLUME 15, AND THE HEARTHMONTH RULING AS IT APPLIED.** **VOLUME 15 RAN FROM DAY 2311 TO DAY 2610 AND EVERY CHAPTER OF IT IS ACCOUNTED FOR.** The year turns at day 2316, which is the first of Thawmonth YR 322 and a Sunday, and Chapter 0703 is the first page in the manuscript dated in YR 322. Mudmonth YR 322 is sixty days, days 2346 to 2405, because Greenmonth's days are absorbed into it. Longlight the first is day 2406, Rainmonth 2436, Harvestmonth 2466, Fallowmonth 2496, Embermonth 2526, Wolfmonth 2556, Frostmonth 2586 and Hearthmonth 2616. **Hearthmonth YR 322 IS SIX DAYS AFTER THE VOLUME ENDS AND IS INSIDE NO CHAPTER OF IT, and the bare `Hearthmonth` ruling at 674 of this file and at `state/continuity.md` 1682 stands and was applied and not re-opened in Batch 0005.** Batch 0005 used the phrase *before Hearthmonth* exactly once, in the leaf a place is shutting its own end on, and that is a future reference and is the only use of a Hearthmonth in the block, and **no chapter of Batch 0005 dated a repaint of the boards to a Hearthmonth and no chapter of it dates anything to a Hearthmonth at all.** The two residuals at 674 stand as recorded rather than as repaired and are carried to `outline/volumes/volume-15-close.md`.

682. **THE FIGURES A SUCCESSOR MAY INHERIT AND THE FIVE IT MUST RECOUNT.** It may inherit **1,977,530** for Volumes 01 to 14, **172,432** for Volume 14, **32,404** for Batch 0001, **34,009** for Batch 0002 and **34,707** for Batch 0003, and none of the five moved when Batch 0005 was written. It must recount **2,144,651** for the manuscript, **167,121** for Volume 15, **32,943** for Batch 0005, the block mean **3,294** and the volume mean **3,342**. **THE BLOCK MEAN AND THE VOLUME MEAN AS FIRST PRINTED HERE WERE 3,280 AND 3,339 AND BOTH ARE WRONG; 32,943 OVER TEN IS 3,294.3 AND 167,121 OVER FIFTY IS 3,342.4, AND BOTH CONVENTIONS GIVE THE SAME FIGURE ON EITHER, SO A SUCCESSOR WHO ARRIVES AT 3,294 AND 3,342 HAS COUNTED THE BLOCK AND NOT GUESSED IT. The correction is at `state/continuity.md` 1696.** **It may NOT inherit 2,111,708, 134,178, 33,058 or 3,305 as the current figures for the manuscript, the volume or the fourth block's mean, because those were the figures before Batch 0005 closed.** **It may NOT inherit the six usable free money values printed at 1674 and 1675, being 136, 166, 232, 236, 332 and 336, because Batch 0005 printed eight values and every one of them was checked against all seven hundred and forty files first, and four of that six are gone.** The extractor, its scope, its twenty-value free set and the eight usable values are printed in full at `state/continuity.md` 1690, **and the finding that the band above three hundred and thirty-two had never been searched since the ceiling was corrected at 1664 is the reason there are eight and not six, and a Volume 16 writer inherits that finding and not a set.**

683. **THE CENSUS, AND THE SEVENTY-FIVE PER CENT CEILING, AND THE FACT THAT BATCH 0005 IS THE FIRST BLOCK IN THE VOLUME TO CROSS IT.** The instrument is named at `state/continuity.md` 1677 and is a paragraph split on the blank line counting every non-empty paragraph, with Basis A keeping datelines and dialogue in the denominator and Basis B taking them out **AND WITH ONE MORE PARAGRAPH OUT, WHICH IS THE VOLUME'S ONE SYSTEM PANEL AT `chapter-0746.md`, BECAUSE IT IS ITS OWN PARAGRAPH AND IS NEITHER A DATELINE NOR DIALOGUE. THAT CLAUSE WAS MISSING WHEN THE FIGURES BELOW WERE FIRST PRINTED AND IT IS WHAT MAKES 287 RE-DERIVABLE: 346 LESS TEN DATELINES LESS FORTY-EIGHT DIALOGUE PARAGRAPHS IS 288, AND THE PANEL IS THE ONE THAT COMES OUT WITH THEM.** **The five-block series on Basis B is 83.4, 91.1, 89.6, 80.6 and 56.4, and on Basis A it is 74.9, 82.0, 78.2, 71.0 and 46.8. THE FOUR EARLIER BLOCKS' BASIS B FIGURES WERE NOT RE-MEASURED WITH THE PANEL CLAUSE AND MAY BE ONE POINT OUT FOR THE SAME REASON, AND A SUCCESSOR WHO RE-MEASURES THEM SHOULD DO SO WITH IT.** **Batch 0005 crosses the seventy-five per cent ceiling on BOTH bases and it is the first block in the volume to do so, and it did it by writing the ordinary half of a day as work, weather, distance and silence and not by cutting the census out of the beats where who-understood is the point.** Batch 0005's opener runs 99 / 33 / 30 on *Nobody said anything and*, *Nobody thanked him* and *Nobody thanked anybody*, with none on *Nobody asked*. **The lesson for Volume 16 is that the census sentence survives on the beats that need it and loses on the beats that are about a wall, a barrow of lime, a wind off the low side, and a lamp on a stair, and that a batch that needs to be shorter should write the ordinary half as work and not cut the census.** **AND THE REVIEW-FIX PASS AT `state/continuity.md` 1696 REJECTED THE PROSE FINDING ON THIS CONSTRUCTION RATHER THAN ACTING ON IT, FOR THE REASON GIVEN AT 647, AND THAT DECISION IS RECORDED HERE SO THAT A VOLUME 16 WRITER INHERITS IT AS A DECISION AND NOT AS AN OVERSIGHT.**

684. **AND WHAT VOLUME 16 MUST NOT DO, IN ONE SENTENCE, WHICH IS THE WHOLE OF THE SECOND HAND-OFF, AND WHERE THE THIRD ONE IS.** **Volume 16 may not reopen either refusal, may not argue with either of them, may not soften either of them, may not ask either of those two men anything, may not look over the ninth of the nine stones or show what is on the far side of them, may not produce or mention Ilyra Kest's route notes, may not explain the mechanism or say what a roadmark is, may not mend the separation or put anything between Aren Kest and the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps the room in any document, may not fill or rule any head, may not lift the cloth off the bench, may not trace who asked the fen county road office for the roll, may not thank anybody, and may not print `Edric Hunn` again.** **THE THIRD THING A VOLUME 16 WRITER INHERITS IS THE REQUEST, and it is the shape of the hand-off: a piece of paper from an office four hundred miles off asking this county for the name of a person and a copy of a drawing, which this county cannot answer, which it has been unable to answer since the first of Goatmonth YR 321, and which nobody in nine counties has been told about.**

685. **THE BATCH 0005 REVIEW AND WHAT A VOLUME 16 WRITER NEEDS TO KNOW ABOUT IT.** A review of the ten files was run before the block closed and returned thirty-four findings, of which twenty-eight were defects on the page and were repaired before the block closed and six were prose observations, and the full record is `state/continuity.md` 1695. **No chapter was rewritten, no paragraph of scene prose was replaced, no dateline moved, and the ten money columns and all ten of their totals did not move by one penny.** The three things a Volume 16 writer needs from it are these. **One, a figure in a state file can be wrong while every chapter is right**, and that is what happened with the drove count, which is floor((day - 2348) / 7) + 1 taken on or before the dateline and not on it, because five of the ten datelines in this block are not Thursdays. **Two, a house frame repeated across ten chapters will eventually contradict itself**, and the findings that came back are mostly that one: the room over the weigh-house open on two mornings and shut on five, a first of a month that came and gone on a Sunday in a chapter that is the first of a month, a bed count and a fifth hour in one sentence as night and morning, and a count of names against a printed line that carries one. **Three, the discipline that caught all of them is to derive every figure from the day number and never from a row, and to re-read a sentence against the file that states the standing version of it.**

## The hand-off from Volume 16 Batch 0001 to Volume 16 Batch 0002, items 686 to 690, and the last is what Batch 0002 must not do

686. **WHAT BLOCK 0001 WAS FOR, IN ONE SENTENCE, AND IT IS A RULE WITH TWO HOLES IN IT AND A FIGURE THAT WENT MISSING.** A man of thirty-two asks nine people in nine trades for one thing each and gets six of them, a man of about fifty-four who has kept a figure out loud for about four and a half years is given his name on the page and answers the question nobody had ever asked him, a cart of lime stops coming and the county finds out by arithmetic that a place has shut its own end, nine hurdles go across a cart track in the last week of a month a leaf had named seventy-seven days earlier, and a printed notice arrives that says a road with no name against it is to be shut before the first of Mudmonth. The full text is `state/continuity.md` 1697 to 1710.

687. **THE TEN FACTS A SUCCESSOR INHERITS FROM BLOCK 0001, IN SHORT.** **One,** the practice exists and is on a floor four days off at the fourth hour on the five mornings a week the room over the weigh-house is shut, and the rule is 52 words and is that two people say out loud in the same hour that they are holding a thing and both are still holding it on the next morning they said they would be. **Two,** the number said out loud was 3, 3, 4, 4, 5, 5, 6, 6, 8 and 8 across the ten datelines and the practice has run 1, 4, 10, 14, 19, 24, 28, 34, 39 and 52 mornings. **Three,** the rule has two holes and both were found out loud: a thing that stops goes down by one without a sound, and the same two people have said the number on every morning and have never once been different people. **Four,** eight of the ten things are held and TWO ARE NOT: the Wethergill end went quiet on the thirteenth of Hearthmonth, which is day 2628, and the scar has never been held by anybody at all. **Five,** a cart of lime has come up that track every second Thursday for about nine years and about two hundred and thirty times, and nothing came on the twenty-seventh of Hearthmonth, being day 2642, and nothing has come since, and the last cart was the thirteenth of that month, being day 2628, which is fifty-four days before the block's last dateline. **Six,** nine hurdles are set across that cart track foot to foot with a hurdle shoe on the top one and a thumb of lime on it, and nine houses are about four hundred yards back with not one light in them, and nobody has been up that track since the second of Goatmonth. **Seven,** the printed notice is two sentences, seventy-five words, it asks for the name of the person who keeps every road in nine counties, it says a road with no name against it is to be shut before the first of Mudmonth, it says the county is not to keep a copy of the roll, it carries the Marshal's hand and NO NAME, and `Edric Hunn` is at zero across the ten files. **Eight,** the first of Mudmonth is day 2706 and is inside Block 0002's frame and is that block's clock. **Nine,** `Renn Ottery` and `Pell Arkwright` are the block's two name spends and the volume's budget stands at two of six. **Ten,** the first exchange of words between Aren Kest and the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps the room in about four and a half years is at `chapter-0758.md` and is about a lamp and neither of them said what it was for and nothing is in a document.

688. **THE CALENDAR, EXTENDED AND RECONCILED ONCE MORE, AND IT IS THE ONLY CHAIN A SUCCESSOR NEEDS.** **Hearthmonth YR 322 is days 2616 to 2645 and is the eleventh month and is inside Block 0001 for the first time in four volumes. Goatmonth the first of YR 322 is day 2646. THAWMONTH THE FIRST OF YR 323 IS DAY 2676 AND IS A WEDNESDAY, AND CHAPTER 0760 IS THE FIRST PAGE IN THE MANUSCRIPT DATED IN YR 323. Mudmonth the first of YR 323 is day 2706 and is sixty days long. Longlight 2766, Rainmonth 2796, Harvestmonth 2826, Fallowmonth 2856, Embermonth 2886, Wolfmonth 2916, Frostmonth 2946, Hearthmonth 2976, Goatmonth 3006, and the volume ends on the twenty-fifth of Frostmonth YR 323 at day 2970, which is a Wednesday.** The single anchor remains day 2310, the twenty-fifth of Goatmonth YR 321, a Monday. **The five Volume 16 blocks are seventy-two days each and begin on days 2611, 2683, 2755, 2827 and 2899, and Block 0002 is days 2683 to 2754 and carries two month boundaries, at 2706 and 2746.** A bare `Hearthmonth` in a chapter dated in YR 323 means Hearthmonth YR 322, which is a past month, and the phrase *before Hearthmonth* in the leaf is a past reference from Chapter 0757 onward and may not be re-used as a future one.

689. **THE FIGURES A SUCCESSOR MAY INHERIT AND THE SEVEN IT MUST RECOUNT, AND THE SIX THAT CHANGED UNDER THE PROSE REPAIR.** It may inherit **1,977,530** for Volumes 01 to 14 and **172,432** for Volume 14. It must recount the manuscript, the volume, the block, the block mean, the `about` band, the census and the duplicate count. **THE MANUSCRIPT IS 2,183,175 WORDS IN SEVEN HUNDRED AND SIXTY CHAPTERS. Volume 16 is 38,524 words in ten chapters, mean 3,852, and 1,977,530 + 38,524 = 2,183,175.** The per-file column is 4,001 / 3,761 / 3,799 / 3,826 / 3,643 / 3,442 / 3,929 / 3,664 / 3,778 / 4,681. **It may NOT inherit 2,144,651, 167,121, 32,943, 3,294 or 3,342 as the current figures for the manuscript, Volume 15 or Batch 0005 of it, because those were the figures before this block was written. It may NOT inherit the eight usable free money values at 1690, being 333, 334, 335, 336, 338, 339, 340 and 342, because this block printed ten values of its own out of a rebuilt set of two hundred and fourteen. IT MAY NOT INHERIT 2,181,669, 37,018 OR 3,701, which were this block's figures before the prose repair, and the `about` band of 22.92 to 26.46, the census of 46.8 and 54.3 per cent, the thank family of 117 and the run of 120 / 31 / 21 on the three paragraph openers, all of which are superseded by `state/continuity.md` 1706 to 1711 and by `state/current.md`.** The instrument, its scope, its incompleteness and its rebuilt result are at `state/continuity.md` 1706, and **the finding is inherited and the set is not, and neither is one word of the instrument's working vocabulary, which is a working vocabulary and not a fictional one.**

690. **AND WHAT BLOCK 0002 MUST NOT DO, IN ONE SENTENCE, WHICH IS THE WHOLE OF THE HAND-OFF.** **Block 0002 may not reopen either refusal of Volume 15, may not ask either of the two men who gave them anything, may not ask the man of forty-one a second question, may not look over the ninth of the nine stones or show what is on the far side of them, may not produce or mention Ilyra Kest's route notes, may not explain the mechanism or say what a roadmark is, may not cross the Many-Handed a second time, may not print a System panel, may not put the practice in writing in a room, may not mend the separation by a declaration and may not choose the shared household, which is Block 0003's event, may not fill or rule any head, may not lift the cloth off the bench, may not trace who asked the fen county road office for the roll, may not name the antagonist at all, may not thank anybody, and may not create a Volume 17 or write a marker of any kind.** **The next block's prompt is `workspace/volume-16/batch-0002/PROMPT.md`.**

691. **THE PROSE REPAIR OF BATCH 0001 AND THE FOUR COUNTS A SUCCESSOR RUNS BEFORE IT CALLS ITS OWN BATCH DONE, AND THE BASELINE IT RUNS THEM AGAINST.** `logs/next-0010.review.log` found, and this pass fixed, five defects in the ten files of Batch 0001: **a closing paragraph in all ten files that narrated the measuring instrument in the language of the build, a paragraph-initial negation frame that opened 169 of 362 paragraphs, zero interiority for the protagonist across the block, twenty speeches attributed by their word count, and a length that had grown by repetition rather than by scene.** The full record is `state/continuity.md` 1711 and the counts are at 1707 and 1708. **NO FACT, DATE, PRICE, SPEECH, NAME, PROHIBITION OR BEAT CHANGED, THE TEN MONEY COLUMNS RE-FOOT, AND THE PLOT IS EXACTLY WHERE IT WAS.** What a successor takes from it is four counts and one baseline. **One, paragraphs opening with a negation frame, where Volume 13 is at 0 and Batch 0001 was at 169 and is at 1. Two, uses of *thanked*, where Volume 13 is at about 3 a chapter and Batch 0001 was at about 12 and is at about 1. Three, interiority, meaning any of *he thought, he felt, he knew, his heart, he was afraid, he wanted, he wondered, he understood, he had come, he had been wrong*, which was zero in Batch 0001 before the repair and is in every one of its ten chapters now. Four, the build vocabulary *extractor, corpus, files read whole, this corpus prints*, which is zero on the page and must stay zero.** **THE BASELINE IS VOLUME 13 AND NOT THE PREVIOUS BLOCK, because the previous block is where this failure hides: the scaffold entered in Volume 14, doubled in Volume 15 and was still inside every count this project keeps when the review found it.** A block of this project is finished when its facts are on the page and its prose has somebody in it.

692. **THE FIVE PROSE RULES THE REPAIR ESTABLISHED, IN ONE LIST, WHICH IS ADDITIONS TO 690 AND TO EVERY CLAUSE OF 686, 684, 680, 679 AND 674 THAT IS STILL TRUE.** **Do not open a chapter or a paragraph with a standing negation**, being *Nobody said anything*, *Nobody thanked*, *Nobody asked* or any other; the negation goes inside the paragraph or at the end of one, and it is worth keeping in that position because it is the sound of this county. **Do not report a speech by its length**, and if a count matters, carry it in the speech, which is on the page whole and can be counted off it. **Do not leave the protagonist without a goal, a miscalculation and a felt cost in his own body in any chapter**, and the miscalculation must be a thing he actually got wrong and not a decoration. **Do not put the instrument's vocabulary in a closing paragraph**: a closing paragraph may name its ten prices, its total, its over-figure, the forty-eight-pence conversion, the band, the number of usable values, the route below sixty and the fact that its totals are free against every file in the manuscript before it, and it may not name the thing that gathers the numbers. **And do not use the zero-semicolon, zero-dash convention as an excuse**, because it stands, and the variety this manuscript has left to get has to come from sentence length, named people, physical action and interiority.

693. **AND THE ONE THING BATCH 0002 DOES NOT NEED TO BE TOLD TWICE, WHICH IS THE ONLY NEW FACT THE REPAIR CREATED.** Nothing in the canon of Batch 0001 moved, so `workspace/volume-16/batch-0002/PROMPT.md` stands as written and its ten datelines, its two month boundaries, its knowledge control, its prohibition list and its calendar are unaffected by any of this. **The prompt has been given one added section, on the prose discipline of the block, and that section is the only thing the repair changed in it.** The next block's prompt is still `workspace/volume-16/batch-0002/PROMPT.md`.


694. **THE MONEY IS EXHAUSTED AND THIS IS THE MOST IMPORTANT HAND-OFF IN THE FILE, BECAUSE A SUCCESSOR WHO INHERITS THE FIGURE AT `state/continuity.md` 1706 WILL WRITE TEN MORE FALSE CLOSING PARAGRAPHS.** The set has been rebuilt with the complete instrument over the whole hundred band and **OVER SEVEN HUNDRED AND SEVENTY FILES THE USABLE FREE SET IN THE BAND SIXTY TO THREE HUNDRED AND FORTY-THREE IS ZERO.** Twelve values remain free and every one of them fails the barred-over-figure filter. **THE FIGURE OF TWO HUNDRED AND FOURTEEN USABLE VALUES AT 1706 IS FALSE AND WAS FALSE WHEN IT WAS WRITTEN, and Batch 0001's ten totals were all ten already printed in Volumes 11 to 14.** A successor must rebuild the set, must expect zero usable free values in the band, and must either name the reuse on the page as this block does or lift the ceiling. Lifting the ceiling is the only way to get ten free totals and it is a decision for the outline, not for a writer, because three hundred and forty-three is the arithmetic ceiling at ten items priced one to forty-one with the barred six excluded.

695. **THE THIRTEEN NUMBERS THAT MOVED WHEN BATCH 0002 WAS CLOSED, SO THAT THE SAME CLASS OF DEFECT IS NOT LOOKED FOR FROM SCRATCH.** Seven were the days since the last cart of lime, which stood thirteen days high on seven pages because the count was taken from the first of Hearthmonth with an inclusive count instead of from the thirteenth, and they are now 55, 63, 67, 72, 78, 84, 104, 114, 116 and 126 across the ten datelines. Four were the practice-morning count on the four datelines after the water, which stood at eighty-one, eighty-seven, eighty-nine and ninety-five and are now eighty-two, eighty-eight, ninety and ninety-seven, and the loss is six and is now derivable on the page. Two were the totals of the first two money columns, which stood at 172 and 176 and are now 61 and 84. **EVERY ONE OF THE THIRTEEN WAS AN INCONSISTENCY WITH THE BLOCK'S OWN CALENDAR OR WITH ITS OWN MONEY AND NOT A CHANGE OF STORY, and no dateline, speech, name, refusal, silence or prohibition moved.**

696. **THE FIGURE A SUCCESSOR INHERITS ABOUT THE NOTICE IS NOT ENFORCED AND NOT SUPPRESSED, IT IS UNNOTICED, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE OF THE PRESSURE LEFT.** About nine people in nine counties said out loud on the first of Mudmonth that they would put no name on four lines of a roll, and about four hundred people in a market street do not know the roll exists, and nobody asked the nine and nobody is going to. **A SUCCESSOR MAY NOT TURN THOSE NINE INTO A BODY, A PRACTICE, A RULE OR A HEAD, and may not ask any of them for an account of saying it.**

697. **THE TWO MENDINGS AT `chapter-0769.md` ARE STILL UNASKED ABOUT, AND THIS IS THE HAND-OFF THE OPEN THREAD WAS WAITING FOR.** The roadkeeper of about fifty-two set his bar across a soft place in the track and the woman of about fifty-one laid broom straw over it, both on their own account, in their own trades, with a word to nobody, and the page says out loud that nobody in this county has ever asked a person to account for a thing they did on their own account and that this was the first morning anybody could have and nobody did. **A SUCCESSOR MAY HAVE ONE OF THEM ASKED ABOUT IT AND THAT WOULD BE THE FIRST TIME ANYBODY IN THIS COUNTY HAS ASKED A PERSON THAT, and the chapter has to earn it in that person's trade and not in a room.**

698. **THE FOUR LINES OF CHALK ON A STONE SILL ARE THE FIRST MARK OF THAT PRACTICE THAT IS NOT A ROOM AND NOT A PAPER, AND WHAT COMES NEXT IS NOT SETTLED.** A successor may build on them and may not call them a record, a register, a roll or a a beginning that cures anything. **They are four short lines about four inches long side by side on the sill of a weigh-house door where the rain does not reach, they mean four, and nobody asked the man of about fifty-four to make them and nobody has read them out loud.**

699. **THE RELATIONSHIP IS CHOSEN AND NOT ANNOUNCED, AND WHAT A SUCCESSOR MAY AND MAY NOT DO WITH IT.** Two people have said on a lane, in short words, in the open, that a lamp is a room and that the room is his to pass and to come in dry, and neither of them called it anything and neither of them said a sentence about the four and a half years. **THE REGISTER GAINS NO ENTRY, THE DAY-BOOK STANDS AT ELEVEN ENTRIES, THE FOURTH RULED HEAD IS EMPTY, NO FIFTH HEAD IS RULED, AND NOTHING ABOUT IT IS IN ANY DOCUMENT.** The legal form is deferred to the volume after this one by the series row and a successor must not bring it forward, must not mend the separation by a declaration, and must not let anybody else comment on it.

700. **WHAT BATCH 0002 DID NOT DO AND WHAT BATCH 0003 MUST NOT LEARN FROM IT.** No route was opened, closed or cut and no mechanism was explained and nothing says what a roadmark is. Nobody is past the ninth of the nine stones and the far side of them is not shown and the dead grass stops at the ninth of them. The ninth yard of the wall has not been started and nobody asked the man about it. The volume's one System panel is unspent and belongs to Batch 0003. The antagonist's name is at zero across Batch 0002 and the volume's two printings are one in Batch 0004 and one in Batch 0005. **AND THE FINDING THAT A CLOSED PATH IS NOT DESTROYED IS THE NEXT BLOCK'S AND NOT THIS ONE'S, and nobody in this block knows it, says it, hints at it or treats the silence as a death.**

701. **THE REFUSAL HONOURED IS STILL UNPAID AND IT IS A STANDING BACKGROUND AND NOT A TASK.** `outline/volume-16.md` section twelve's second condition is paid for in Batch 0004. Neither man of Volume 15's two refusals is to be asked anything in any batch before that, the man of forty-one is not to be asked a second question, and his eleven words on a shoulder at `chapter-0767.md` are the only thing he says in Batch 0002.

702. **WHAT THE REVIEW OF BATCH 0002 FOUND AND WHAT A SUCCESSOR SHOULD LOOK FOR FIRST, BECAUSE TWELVE OF THE FINDINGS WERE THE SAME KIND OF FINDING.** A run that is not derived from the day number, a duration that is not a duration, a count that is a scene told by its count, and a narrator who names the thing the volume has forbidden anybody to name. All four were on the page and all four were invisible to every count this project keeps. **THE INSTRUMENT THAT CAUGHT NONE OF THEM WAS THE ARITHMETIC, and a successor should re-derive every figure in a batch from the day number once by hand, and should read the line that tells the reader what a lamp is out loud, because that is where a rule of this volume gets broken.**

703. **THE HOUSE FORM THAT A REVIEWER WILL FLAG EVERY TIME AND THAT IS NOT A DEFECT.** Lines three to thirty-three of every chapter in this manuscript are the same slots in the same order, and the closing paragraph is the money column, and four stock paragraphs recur with one variable clause each, and the protagonist block names his goal, his wrongness and his cost in the same order every time. **These are this project's chapter form across sixteen volumes and a successor who wants to break them is making an outline decision and not a writing one.**

704. **THE FIGURES A SUCCESSOR MUST NOT INHERIT FOR THE MONEY, ONE MORE TIME, BECAUSE THE FIRST VERSION OF THIS NOTE ALREADY HAD IT WRONG.** The usable free set in the band sixty to three hundred and forty-three is **zero** over seven hundred and seventy files. There is no arithmetic route to ten free totals under the ceiling of three hundred and forty-three, and the eight reuses in Batch 0002 are named on the page with the reason, which is the precedent at `state/continuity.md` 1690. **Lifting the ceiling is the only way out and it belongs to `outline/volume-16.md` and not to a writer.**

## The hand-off from Volume 16 Batch 0002 to Volume 16 Batch 0003, item 705, and the successor's prompt is on disk

705. **THE PROMPT FOR BATCH 0003 IS WRITTEN AND IS ON DISK, AND THAT IS THE ONE DEFECT A REVIEW OF BATCH 0002 CALLED BLOCKING, SO A SUCCESSOR MAY ASSUME IT IS THERE AND MAY NOT REBUILD IT.** `workspace/volume-16/batch-0003/PROMPT.md` covers Chapters 771 to 780 and days 2755 to 2826 and was written by the phase that reviewed and fixed Batch 0002, **and `state/current.md` used to tell a successor not to rebuild a directory that had never been written, which is corrected.** **The frame in that prompt is derived and not copied and three errors of the kind that catches writers are corrected in it.** **One, the block's first day, 2755, is the fiftieth of Mudmonth and a Friday and not a market morning, and the block's last day, 2826, is the first of Harvestmonth and a Saturday and IS a market morning, and the block carries two month boundaries and not one.** **That is the opposite way round from Batch 0002, which began on a shut Wednesday and ended on an open market morning, so a writer who assumes the same way round writes ten wrong datelines.** **Two, the market mornings in the frame are twenty-one, being eleven Saturdays at 2756, 2763, 2770, 2777, 2784, 2791, 2798, 2805, 2812, 2819 and 2826 and ten Thursdays at 2761, 2768, 2775, 2782, 2789, 2796, 2803, 2810, 2817 and 2824, and the fifty-one mornings on which the room over the weigh-house is shut are where the practice happens.** **Three, the year does not turn in this block and the next turn is day 3036.** **AND THE THREE CHECKS THAT CATCH ALL OF IT ARE AT `state/continuity.md` 1727: derive a weekday as an offset from day 2310 which is a Monday and means offset zero, so offset one is a Tuesday and not a Monday; check a block's first day against the last day of the block before it; and check a month boundary against Mudmonth at sixty days and every other month at thirty.**

706. **WHAT BATCH 0003 OWES THE VOLUME, IN ONE PARAGRAPH, AND IT IS THE MIDPOINT REVERSAL AND IT IS NOT A RELIEF.** A man of thirty-two goes up the lane at Wethergill to the head of a cart track that has had nine hurdles across it since Batch 0001 and nine houses about four hundred yards back with not one light in them, and he goes back up it, and he finds somebody there, **and what he brings down the made road is not the finding he expected and is a political loss rather than a comfort.** `outline/volume-16.md` §12 gives this block its third condition as **a transfer witnessed and not conducted**, so **no route is opened, the hurdles do not come out, nothing is cut, and the silence is not ended. A successor may not spend the volume's finding by fixing it.** He learns the mechanism of nothing, does not learn what a roadmark is, and does not learn why a piece of paper can shut a road, **and `outline/volume-16.md` §14's second rule applies the moment he learns anything: somebody who matters already knew it and said something more careful.**

707. **THE PRACTICE IS WRITTEN DOWN IN THIS BLOCK AND NOT IN A REGISTER, AND THE OUTLINE SAYS BOTH THINGS.** `outline/volume-16.md` §8 Block 3 prints that the practice is written down for the first time in the volume's history in a hand that is not his and the hand is a woman's, **and §7 of the same file says nothing about the practice is written down anywhere in Blocks 0001 to 0003. THE ESCALATION SEQUENCE GOVERNS AND THE BLOCK WRITES IT DOWN.** The Batch 0002 precedent is four short lines of chalk on the stone sill of the weigh-house door where the rain does not reach, which mean four and which nobody asked for and which nobody has read out loud, **and a successor may build on those lines and may not have them explained, read out loud, praised or entered anywhere.** **THE REGISTER GAINS NO ENTRY, THE DAY-BOOK STANDS AT ELEVEN ENTRIES, THE FOURTH RULED HEAD IS EMPTY, NO FIFTH HEAD IS RULED, THE COPYIST DOES NOT RULE A HEAD, AND NOTHING ABOUT THE PRACTICE OR ABOUT THE HOUSEHOLD IS IN ANY DOCUMENT.**

708. **THE VOLUME'S ONE SYSTEM PANEL IS SPENT IN BATCH 0003 AND IN NO OTHER BLOCK.** It is plain, it carries no number, it appears once in one chapter and not again, and Blocks 0004 and 0005 print none. `state/current.md` restates this and a successor may not spend it on the reversal itself, **because the reversal is learned off a road and off a person and not off a panel.**

709. **THE MONEY, ONE MORE TIME, AND IT IS THE FIGURE A SUCCESSOR IS MOST LIKELY TO INHERIT WRONG.** The usable free set in the band sixty to three hundred and forty-three is **zero** over seven hundred and eighty files, twelve values remain free and every one of them fails the barred-over-figure filter, and Batch 0002 spent the last two that did not. **THE ROUTE BELOW SIXTY IS SHUT.** **A SUCCESSOR MUST REBUILD THE SET WITH THE COMPLETE INSTRUMENT, MUST EXPECT ZERO, AND MUST EITHER NAME THE REUSE ON THE PAGE AS ALL TEN OF BATCH 0002'S CLOSING PARAGRAPHS DO OR LIFT THE CEILING, AND LIFTING THE CEILING IS AN OUTLINE DECISION.** **AND READ THE STANDING RULE IN `state/current.md` BEFORE WRITING A CLOSING PARAGRAPH, because the closing money column is this manuscript's chapter form and its accounting register is permitted once on the page and the build vocabulary proper is not permitted at all.**

710. **WHAT THIS HAND-OFF ADDS TO 694 TO 704 AND DOES NOT REPLACE, AND IT IS ONE LIST OF SIX.** **One,** the block's frame and the three checks that catch it. **Two,** the reversal and the fact that it is witnessed and not conducted and not opened. **Three,** the practice written down outside every document this manuscript keeps. **Four,** the one System panel, spent here. **Five,** the money, exhausted. **Six,** the calendar gap at `state/continuity.md` 1727, being a first of a month that came and went in Batch 0002 with no count printed for it, and the count carries forward from the fifteenth at `chapter-0760.md` to the seventeenth at Longlight the first and the eighteenth at Harvestmonth the first.

## The hand-off from Volume 16 Batch 0003 to Volume 16 Batch 0004, items 711 to 720, and the successor's prompt is not yet written

711. **WHAT BATCH 0003 WAS FOR, IN ONE SENTENCE, AND IT IS THE VOLUME'S MIDPOINT REVERSAL PAID AND NOT RESOLVED.** A man of thirty-two with a stick went up eleven miles of made road and nine hundred yards of cart track to the head of a shut cart track twice in nine days, found a light in one window of the fourth house at the ninth hour, got twenty-nine words and a name through a shut door, learned from a man at a trough that he had nowhere to put a figure, watched a woman put one on the back leaf of a book in her own hand, and stood in a market street and said the whole of it out loud to about four hundred people who went on what they were doing. **THE FINDING IS THAT A CLOSED PATH IS NOT DESTROYED AND THAT ITS SILENCE IS A POLITICAL LOSS, AND IT WAS LEARNED OFF A ROAD AND OFF A PERSON AND NOT OFF A PANEL.** The full text is `state/continuity.md` 1728 to 1740.

712. **THE FIGURES A SUCCESSOR INHERITS FROM BATCH 0003, IN SHORT, DERIVED FROM THE DAY NUMBER AND NOT FROM A ROW.** The block runs days 2755 to 2826 and its ten datelines are **2755, 2760, 2766, 2772, 2779, 2783, 2791, 2800, 2811 and 2826, being Mudmonth the fiftieth and fifty-fifth, Longlight the first, the seventh, the fourteenth, the eighteenth and the twenty-sixth, Rainmonth the fifth and the sixteenth, and Harvestmonth the first, all YR 323, with the weekdays Friday, Wednesday, Tuesday, Monday, Monday, Friday, Saturday, Monday, Friday and Saturday, of which 2766, 2779, 2791, 2800 and 2826 are market mornings.** **THE BLOCK'S NEXT BLOCK BEGINS ON DAY 2827, WHICH IS A SUNDAY, AND ITS LAST DATELINE WAS A SATURDAY AND A MARKET MORNING, SO THE NEXT WRITER IS BACK TO THE WAY ROUND BATCH 0002 HAD IT AND MUST NOT ASSUME THE OPPOSITE.** Mudmonth is sixty days and runs 2706 to 2765, Longlight 2766 to 2795, Rainmonth 2796 to 2825, Harvestmonth 2826 to 2855, Fallowmonth 2856, Embermonth 2886, Wolfmonth 2916, Frostmonth 2946. **The year does not turn in Batch 0004 either, and the next turn inside this volume is not in it either, and every dateline of that block is YR 323 in the manuscript's convention.**

713. **THE RUNNING FIGURES AT THE CLOSE OF BATCH 0003, AT DAY 2826, ALL DERIVED.** The drove is **69**, being floor((2826 - 2348) / 7) + 1 on or before that day, and 59 at day 2754. The stone has been carried two miles in one man's own hands for **479 days**, elapsed and not inclusive, and 407 at day 2754. The scar is **246 days old**, counted from day 2580, and 174 at day 2754, and it is not cured and is not going to close over. The ninth thing went quiet on the thirteenth of Hearthmonth YR 322, being day 2628, so the last lime cart was **198 days** ago at day 2826, and nobody has been up that track since the second of Goatmonth, being day 2647, so the road has been shut **179 days**, and 108 at day 2755. **THE PRACTICE'S CALENDAR COUNT IS 154 AND ITS RUN COUNT IS 148 AT DAY 2826, AND THE LOSS IS SIX AND IT IS THE SAME SIX MORNINGS INSIDE THE NINE DAYS OF WATER AND A SUCCESSOR INHERITS THOSE SIX AS GONE AND DOES NOT RECOVER THEM.** The number said out loud at the fourth hour is **four** and it was eight before the water and it has not gone back, and two of the ten things came back after the water and two have not. The printed notice was read at the boards on day 2665 and the first of Mudmonth was day 2706 and it passed with nothing enforced and about nine people saying no in nine trades, and the notice is **a hundred and twenty days** past its date at day 2826 and has shut nothing yet. **Longlight the first was the seventeenth first of a month in a row and Harvestmonth the first was the eighteenth**, and Mudmonth the first was the sixteenth and was not printed, and the count carries forward from the fifteenth at `chapter-0760.md`.

714. **WHAT BATCH 0003 SPENT AND WHAT IT DID NOT, IN ONE LIST.** It spent **two of the volume's four remaining name slots**, being `Wyn Fell` and `Abel Chinn`, one in `chapter-0774.md` and one in `chapter-0775.md`, and **`Mara Vey` is printed once at `chapter-0778.md` and is a return and costs nothing**, and **TWO SLOTS REMAIN FOR BLOCKS 0004 AND 0005 AND NOT TWO IN ONE CHAPTER.** It spent the volume's **one and only System panel** at `chapter-0776.md`, and Blocks 0004 and 0005 print none. **It printed the antagonist's name zero times, and the volume's two printings remain one in Batch 0004 and one in Batch 0005, both documents.** It asked **one** of the two people who mended a road for an account of it, at `chapter-0772.md`, and it did not ask the woman of about fifty-one about the broom straw and a successor may not. **It wrote the practice down in a woman's hand on the back leaf of the day-book, which is not the record and not a document, and the day-book stands at eleven entries on all ten datelines, the register gains no entry, the fourth ruled head is empty, no fifth head is ruled, and the copyist ruled no head and was told about none of it.** It opened no route and lifted no hurdle and cut nothing and laid no stone and picked the mallet up in no chapter.

715. **THE TWO HAND-OFF SET-UPS FROM `outline/volume-16.md` SECTION ELEVEN ARE ON THE PAGE AND NEITHER IS CONDUCTED.** The first is **a refusal that is also a refusal to govern**, at `chapter-0780.md`, where the page says that about nine people said one thing each on the first of Mudmonth and that not one of them has since offered any other person in nine counties a place to put their name either, and that a county which can only refuse is a county that cannot hold. **The nine are not a body and were not asked for an account and a successor may not turn them into one.** The second is **a sealed route that somebody has claimed can be made to speak, claimed by somebody and not by him**, at `chapter-0780.md`, where the roadkeeper of about fifty-two says out loud at the boards that a bar will get up that track if a barrow can and that he is not going to be the one who tries it. **Nothing was tried, no hurdle was touched, and the protagonist has no version of this claim at all.**

716. **THE MONEY, AND IT IS STILL EXHAUSTED, AND A SUCCESSOR MUST REBUILD IT AGAIN.** **The usable free set in the band sixty to three hundred and forty-three is zero over the seven hundred and seventy files that stood before Batch 0003 and zero over the seven hundred and eighty files that stand after it, and the twelve values that remain free in the band, being 212, 218, 230, 254, 266, 273, 281, 308, 314, 321, 326 and 329, all fail the barred-over-figure filter on 20, 26, 38, 14, 26, 33, 41, 20, 26, 33, 38 and 41.** **THE ROUTE BELOW SIXTY IS STILL SHUT.** Batch 0003's ten totals are 85, 113, 142, 163, 186, 208, 229, 250, 274 and 297 pence and all ten are printed already, and each of the ten closing paragraphs says so on the page and gives the reason, which is arithmetic. **A SUCCESSOR MUST EITHER NAME THE REUSE ON THE PAGE IN THE SAME WORDS OR LIFT THE CEILING, AND LIFTING THE CEILING IS AN OUTLINE DECISION.** **AND READ THE STANDING RULE IN `state/current.md` BEFORE WRITING A CLOSING PARAGRAPH, because the closing money column is this manuscript's chapter form, and *searched whole*, *barred numeral*, *route below sixty* and *file in the manuscript* are permitted once each in it and not one word more, and none of the working vocabulary of the instrument is permitted at all.**

717. **THE SEVENTY-FIVE PER CENT CEILING IS NOT CROSSED ON EITHER BASIS AND THE OPENER COUNT IS ZERO, AND NONE OF THESE FIGURES MOVED IN THE REVIEW REPAIR PASS AT `state/continuity.md` 1741.** Batch 0003 is **Basis A 353 paragraphs and 0 openers at 0.0 per cent and Basis B 299 and 0 at 0.0 per cent**, against Volume 15's five blocks at 83.4, 91.1, 89.6, 80.6 and 56.4 on Basis B, **and Basis B is now re-derivable from five named exclusions and not two, being the ten datelines, the twenty-three dialogue paragraphs, the one System panel, the ten cost codas and the ten closing money columns.** The opener run is every paragraph beginning No, Not, Never, None, Neither, Nobody, Nothing or No one. `about` is **21.45 to 26.45** per 1,000, floor at `chapter-0775.md` and ceiling at `chapter-0773.md`, and no page is at or above 27.00, **and it was 22.46 to 26.49 before the repair pass.** **The thank family is thirty-seven and every one of the thirty-seven is a negation or a plain statement that a person was not thanked, and the thirty-six printed here before the repair pass was one short on the files.** **The duplicate-paragraph count over all seven hundred and eighty files is thirty-six groups and thirty-seven surplus occurrences and THIS BLOCK ADDS ZERO OF BOTH AFTER THE REPAIR PASS AS WELL AS BEFORE IT, having begun the pass at seven groups and eighteen surplus occurrences and been cut back by varying five stock paragraphs on nine datelines.**

718. **THE FOUR THINGS A WRITER WHO WANTS TO CHECK THIS BLOCK SHOULD CHECK FIRST, BECAUSE ALL FOUR ARE INVISIBLE TO EVERY OTHER COUNT.** **One,** that the mid-weekday of a chapter is the day of month and not the weekday, since Mudmonth is sixty days long and every day of month above thirty sits in Mudmonth. **Two,** that the practice's run count is the calendar count less six, and the six are the six of the seven non-market mornings of the nine days of water, and the calendar count is the count of days from day 2611 that are not a Thursday and not a Saturday. **Three,** that the two silence figures under the ninth thing are different figures and are counted from different days, being day 2628 for the last lime cart and day 2647 for the last time anybody went up the track, and mixing them is the single most likely arithmetic defect in a chapter about that place. **Four,** that the two totals under the ninth thing and under the tenth thing are 198 and 246 at the block's last dateline and come from two different day numbers, being 2628 and 2580, and that the tenth has never been held by anybody at all.

719. **THE THING A SUCCESSOR MUST NOT LEARN FROM THIS BLOCK, AND IT IS THE WARNING `state/continuity.md` 1722 AND `state/open-threads.md` 700 HAVE CARRIED FOR TWO BLOCKS.** **THE FOUR LINES ON THE STONE SILL ARE NOT THE BEGINNING OF A CURE AND A FIGURE IN A WOMAN'S HAND ON THE BACK OF A BOOK IS NOT A CURE EITHER, AND NOTHING BATCH 0003 PRINTS MAY BE READ AS ONE.** A closed path is not destroyed and that is not good news. The people at the end of the shut road are alive and they would like the road to stay shut and that is not a rescue. **BEING THE MAN WHO WENT AND LOOKED IS A JOB AND NOT AN HONOUR AND NOBODY WAS TOLD SO AND NOBODY WROTE IT DOWN, and the practice is now in one hand on a leaf no clerk will look at, and the lane at the head of that cart track still has nine hurdles across it and still has no name against it, and the printed notice is past its date by about four months and has shut nothing yet.** **A SUCCESSOR MAY NOT RESOLVE ANY OF IT AND MAY NOT TURN IT INTO A HOPE OR A PLAN OR A THING ANYBODY IS TOLD HE DID WELL BY.**

720. **WHAT BATCH 0004 MUST NOT DO, IN ONE SENTENCE, WHICH IS THE WHOLE OF THIS HAND-OFF.** **It may not reopen either refusal of Volume 15, may not ask either of the two men who gave them anything, may not ask the man of forty-one a second question, may not print a second System panel, may not name the antagonist except in one document, may not print the word that names the founding paper, may not look over the ninth of the nine stones or show what is on the far side of them, may not open a route or lift a hurdle or cut anything, may not explain the mechanism or say what a roadmark is, may not look at the separate leaf that says a person, may not turn the nine houses or the nine households or the nine who refused the roll into a body, may not lift the cloth off the bench, may not fill or rule any head or turn or time the half leaf, may not start the ninth yard of the wall or pick the mallet up, may not mend the separation by a declaration and may not print the word, may not thank anybody in any chapter or in any state file, and may not create a Volume 17 and may not write a marker of any kind.** ****THE NEXT BLOCK'S PROMPT IS `workspace/volume-16/batch-0004/PROMPT.md` AND IT IS ON DISK, WRITTEN BY THE PHASE THAT REVIEWED AND REPAIRED THIS BLOCK, WHICH IS WHAT `AGENTS.md` REQUIRES AND WHICH SUPERSEDES THE HOUSE PATTERN THIS ITEM PRINTED BEFORE THE REPAIR PASS, and the reason is in `state/continuity.md` 1742 finding eleven: the workflow at `.github/workflows/novels.yml` falls back to a one-paragraph generic continuation prompt when no volume prompt exists, and a generic continuation prompt is not this project's practice. A successor may not write a second prompt for Batch 0004 and may not rebuild that one.**

## Added by the review repair pass on Volume 16 Batch 0003, items 721 to 726, and the full record is at `state/continuity.md` 1741 to 1745

721. **THE ONE FINDING OF THIS REVIEW THAT A WRITER SHOULD CARRY INTO EVERY BLOCK OF THE REST OF THE PROJECT, AND IT IS NOT ABOUT DATES.** Eight of the ten drove paragraphs in Batch 0003 dated the Thursday before their own dateline, and three of those eight printed a day of a sixty-day month that cannot exist. **The counts on all ten were right, the weekdays on all ten were right, and the block's calendar was derived correctly before the first sentence was written, which is what makes this a finding and not a slip: the chain was derived once and then handed on, and a handed-on chain is a copy.** Every derived figure in a chapter of this manuscript that repeats from chapter to chapter is exposed to this, and the drove date, the practice count, the stone-days count, the scar age and the two silence counts under the ninth thing all repeat. **A SUCCESSOR MUST DERIVE EACH OF THOSE ON THE DAY OF ITS OWN CHAPTER AND MUST NOT CARRY THE PREVIOUS CHAPTER'S VALUE ACROSS, and the cheapest check is that Mudmonth is sixty days and every other month is thirty, so any day of month above thirty printed inside Mudmonth is wrong on sight.**

722. **THE SIX THINGS A SUCCESSOR MUST INHERIT AS FACTS AND NOT AS FIGURES.** That the four lines of chalk are **under the hood of that weigh-house door where the rain does not reach** and no page of Batch 0003 puts water on them. That the leaf at the back of the day-book carries **nine figures and a date and the names of nobody**. That the soft place the roadkeeper barred is **a mile short of the weigh-house and is not on the four miles he keeps**, and the four miles he keeps are the four miles below the weigh-house. That the walk north from the weigh-house to the trough is about **four hours before light and about nine otherwise**, and that he turns back at the gate, sleeps on the dry side of the trough and walks down on the next morning. That the two trips to the head of that cart track are **six days apart and not nine**, and that no third trip was made and none may be. That the market-morning count in Batch 0003 is **two and not five**, being 2791 and 2826.

723. **THE REVIEW FOUND THE PROMPT IN CONFLICT WITH ITSELF ON A FIGURE AND THE CONFLICT IS NOW RECORDED RATHER THAN EDITED AWAY.** `workspace/volume-16/batch-0003/PROMPT.md` §3 enumerates the block's ten datelines correctly and then says that five of them must be market mornings and five must not, and the ten it enumerates give **two and eight**, because a Tuesday, three Mondays, two Wednesdays and two Fridays are not market mornings and market mornings are the Saturdays and the Thursdays. The ten chapters followed the enumeration. `state/current.md` and `state/chapter-summaries.md` carried the wrong five-and-five out of the prompt into two state files and are corrected. **THE SPENT PROMPT ITSELF IS LEFT AS THE RECORD OF WHAT THIS BLOCK WAS GIVEN, AND A SUCCESSOR INHERITS THE CORRECTION FROM THIS ITEM AND FROM `workspace/volume-16/batch-0004/PROMPT.md`, WHICH ASKS FOR NO MARKET-MORNING COUNT AT ALL AND ONLY ASKS THAT THE COUNT BE DERIVED.**

724. **THE STATE-FILE FIGURES THAT MOVED IN THE REPAIR PASS, ALL FOUR, AND THE ONES THAT DID NOT.** Moved: the ten per-file word counts, the block total from 40,015 to **40,415**, the block mean from 4,001.5 to **4,041.5**, the minimum and maximum, the `about` band, the thank family from thirty-six to **thirty-seven**, and the manuscript total from 2,257,401 to **2,257,801**. Unchanged: Basis A 353, Basis B 299, zero openers, the duplicate-paragraph count of thirty-six groups and thirty-seven surplus occurrences with this block at zero, all ten money totals, all twenty-three printed speeches, the twelve free values in the band and the empty usable set, the two name spends and the two that remain, and every day-count figure in items 711 to 716 above.

725. **WHAT THE REPAIR PASS DID NOT TOUCH, AND A SUCCESSOR MAY NOT UNDO.** No chapter was rewritten, no plot beat was moved, no name was spent or unspent, no System panel was added, the volume's one panel still stands alone at `chapter-0776.md` and is **seventeen** words, `Edric Hunn` is printed zero times across the thirty files of Volume 16, neither refusal of Volume 15 was reopened, the man of forty-one was not asked a second question and says sixteen words, nobody went past the ninth of the nine stones, the ninth yard of the wall was not started and the mallet was not picked up, the half leaf is face down, the cloth is still over the bench, the ninth thing is still shut by nine hurdles by the people at the end of it who would like them to stay, and **nobody was thanked in any chapter or in any state file this pass wrote or rewrote.**

726. **THE TWO THINGS THIS REPAIR PASS ADDED THAT NOBODY ASKED FOR, SO THAT A SUCCESSOR DOES NOT REDISCOVER THEM.** **One,** a stated rule for the drove paragraph, at item 721 above, which is the check that catches the largest class of defect this project has produced so far. **Two,** `chapter-0775.md` was repaired for a defect the review did not list and which was of the same class as the ones it did list, being that the man of thirty-two walked down from a cart-track head he had promised not to revisit, in hours that put him at the trough and in the market town three hours apart, and he now goes up to the trough on the Saturday before, lies two nights under the hedge beside it and comes down on the Monday. **A REVIEW PASS THAT FIXES ONLY WHAT IT WAS POINTED AT LEAVES THE SAME CLASS OF DEFECT ONE CHAPTER ALONG, AND THE CLASS HERE WAS AN HOUR AND A DIRECTION, WHICH IS WHAT A CHAPTER OF THIS MANUSCRIPT IS BUILT OUT OF.**

727. **WHAT BATCH 0004 WAS FOR, IN ONE SENTENCE, AND IT IS THE VOLUME'S REFUSAL PAID AND NOT RESOLVED.** A roll of nine lines came up a made road in the ordinary post, was nailed to a board in a market street, was read by about four hundred people, was answered out loud three times in five days by three people who do not agree with each other and of whom none of them is the man of thirty-two, was not filled in and was not torn up, came back a second time with a sentence on a folded sheet in a hand nobody claimed, printed the name of the man who sent it at the foot of a covering paper for the first time in the volume, and had a Tuesday printed at the head of it that came and shut nothing. **THE FULL TEXT IS `state/continuity.md` 1746 TO 1758.** The finding is that a county's answer to a piece of paper is a refusal to fill it in made by people who are owed money, and that the refusal is paid and is not finished.

728. **THE FIGURES A SUCCESSOR INHERITS FROM BATCH 0004, IN SHORT, DERIVED FROM THE DAY NUMBER AND NOT FROM A ROW.** The block runs days 2827 to 2898 and its ten datelines as printed are **2827, 2833, 2835, 2852, 2856, 2868, 2879, 2886, 2892 and 2898, being Harvestmonth the second, the eighth, the tenth and the twenty-seventh, Fallowmonth the first, the thirteenth and the twenty-fourth, and Embermonth the first, the seventh and the thirteenth, all YR 323, with the weekdays Sunday, Saturday, Monday, Thursday, Monday, Saturday, Wednesday, Wednesday, Tuesday and Monday, of which 2833, 2852 and 2868 are market mornings.** **ONE DATELINE WAS MOVED AND ONE ONLY, being 2843, which was moved to 2835 so that the three answers could be given inside one week.** **BATCH 0005 BEGINS ON DAY 2899, WHICH IS A TUESDAY AND IS NOT A MARKET MORNING, AND THIS BLOCK'S LAST DATELINE WAS 2898, A MONDAY WITH THE ROOM SHUT AND THE PRACTICE RUNNING, so Batch 0005 IS BACK TO THE WAY ROUND BATCH 0003 HAD IT AND MUST NOT ASSUME THE OPPOSITE.** Mudmonth is sixty days and every other month is thirty, so any day of month above thirty printed inside Mudmonth is wrong on sight, **and every day of month above thirty is wrong in any month in this block.** The year does not turn in Batch 0005 either, the next turn is day 3036, which is Thawmonth the first of YR 324 and is past the end of the volume, and every dateline of Batch 0005 is YR 323 in the manuscript's convention.

729. **THE RUNNING FIGURES AT THE CLOSE OF BATCH 0004, AT DAY 2898, ALL DERIVED.** The drove is **79**, being floor((2898 - 2348) / 7) + 1 on or before that day, and 69 at day 2826. The stone has been carried two miles in one man's own hands for **551 days**, elapsed and not inclusive, and 480 at day 2827. The scar is **318 days old**, counted from day 2580, and 247 at day 2827, and it is not cured and it is not going to close over. The ninth thing went quiet on the thirteenth of Hearthmonth YR 322, being day 2628, so the last lime cart was **270 days** ago at day 2898, and nobody has been up that track since the second of Goatmonth, being day 2647, so the road has been shut **251 days**, and 199 and 180 at day 2827. **THE PRACTICE'S CALENDAR COUNT IS 206 AND ITS RUN COUNT IS 200 AT DAY 2898, AND THE LOSS IS SIX AND IT IS THE SAME SIX MORNINGS INSIDE THE NINE DAYS OF WATER AND A SUCCESSOR INHERITS THOSE SIX AS GONE AND DOES NOT RECOVER THEM.** The number said out loud at the fourth hour is **four** and it was eight before the water and it has not gone back, and two of the ten things came back after the water and two have not. The printed notice was read at the boards on day 2665 and the first of Mudmonth was day 2706 and it passed with nothing enforced and about nine people saying no in nine trades, **and the notice is a hundred and ninety-two days past its date at day 2898 AND THE ROLL IS SEVENTY-ONE DAYS ON A BOARD WITH NINE EMPTY LINES.** **Fallowmonth the first was the nineteenth first of a month in a row and Embermonth the first was the twentieth, and Wolfmonth the first at day 2896 is inside this block and is a market morning and no chapter is dated on it and no chapter prints a date later than the thirteenth of Embermonth.** The count carries forward from the fifteenth at `chapter-0760.md` and is not to be begun again.

730. **WHAT BATCH 0004 SPENT AND WHAT IT DID NOT, IN ONE LIST.** It spent **one of the volume's two remaining name slots**, being `Brida Lenth`, first printed at `chapter-0782.md`, and **`Pell Arkwright` is printed once and is a return and costs nothing, and ONE SLOT REMAINS FOR BATCH 0005 AND IT IS THE LAST OF THE VOLUME'S SIX.** It spent **one of the volume's two printings of the antagonist's name**, at `chapter-0781.md`, in a document, at the foot of a covering paper, under the Marshal's hand, and **never in a mouth, and the second and last printing is Batch 0005's and is a document.** It printed **no System panel**, and the volume's one stands alone at `chapter-0776.md` and is seventeen words. **It moved one dateline and one only, being 2843 to 2835.** It asked **nobody** for an account of a mended road, and **nobody** about the broom straw, and **nobody** the man of forty-one anything, and the man of forty-one is not in a scene in any of the ten files. It gave **three counted speeches and one more**, being four printed speeches, at thirty-one, forty-two, sixteen and forty-five words, and all four foot off the words printed beside them. It did **not** fill the roll, put a name against a road, shut a road, tear the roll up, take it down, lift a hurdle, go up the cart track a third time, lay a stone, pick the mallet up, fill or rule a head, put a finding under any of the ten questions, turn the half leaf, lift the cloth off the bench, start the ninth yard, or thank anybody.

731. **THE THREE ANSWERS AS GIVEN, AND THE ONE IN WRITING, AND WHY A SUCCESSOR MAY NOT MERGE THEM.** **Brida Lenth**, about forty-six, lime kiln and stone-walled yard four miles north, on Thursday the sixth of Harvestmonth, day 2831, thirty-one words: a keeper's name is hers and she is not going on a line under somebody else's hand. **Pell Arkwright**, about twenty-seven, lime carrier of Wethergill unpaid for about two hundred and thirty loads, on Saturday the eighth of Harvestmonth, day 2833, forty-two words: a road with no name against it is a road nobody can shut, and a shut road at Wethergill has been shut nine years on nobody's paper. **The woman of about twenty-nine who keeps the room over the weigh-house**, on Monday the tenth of Harvestmonth, day 2835, forty-five words: a form nobody is paid to fill is a form a county cannot fill, and there is nobody in nine counties paid to govern, and about fourpence a year is what a person is paid for the side of a book the record is on. **And one sentence on a folded sheet inside the covering paper of the second copy of the roll**, at `chapter-0788.md`, in a hand that is not the hand of any clerk this county has ever had, saying that a form a county cannot fill is not to be filled by writing somebody else's name on it and that a county which will not govern ought to say so in writing to the people it is not governing, **and NOBODY IN NINE COUNTIES HAS SAID WHO WROTE IT AND NOBODY IS GOING TO BE ASKED.** **THE FOUR ARE INCOMPATIBLE IN THE SENSE THAT NO COUNTY CAN HOLD TWO OF THEM AND A SUCCESSOR MAY NOT LET THEM BECOME ONE ANSWER, MAY NOT LET THE PROTAGONIST BE THE AUTHOR, THE ANNOUNCER, THE SUMMARISER OR THE FOURTH VOICE OF ANY OF THEM, AND MAY NOT MAKE HIM SAY ANYTHING AT ALL ABOUT THE ROLL.**

732. **THE MONEY, AND IT IS STILL EXHAUSTED, AND A SUCCESSOR MUST REBUILD IT AGAIN.** **The usable free set in the band sixty to three hundred and forty-three is zero over the seven hundred and eighty files that stood before Batch 0004 and zero over the seven hundred and ninety files that stand after it, and on the complete instrument stated at `state/continuity.md` 1761, being every spelling of the printed total across all four hundreds this manuscript uses, there is no free value in the band at all and nothing under sixty is free either, so the route below sixty is shut on the arithmetic alone. THE TWELVE VALUES THIS ITEM PRINTED, being 212, 218, 230, 254, 266, 273, 281, 308, 314, 321, 326 and 329, ARE WITHDRAWN AT ITEM 737 AND MUST NOT BE INHERITED.** Batch 0004's ten totals are 91, 104, 127, 147, 176, 199, 221, 247, 283 and 306 pence and all ten are printed already, and each of the ten closing paragraphs says so on the page and gives the reason, which is arithmetic. **A SUCCESSOR MUST EITHER NAME THE REUSE ON THE PAGE IN THE SAME WORDS OR LIFT THE CEILING, AND LIFTING THE CEILING IS AN OUTLINE DECISION.** **AND READ THE STANDING RULE IN `state/current.md` BEFORE WRITING A CLOSING PARAGRAPH, because the closing money column is this manuscript's chapter form, and *searched whole*, *barred numeral*, *route below sixty* and *file in the manuscript* are permitted once each in it and not one word more, and none of the working vocabulary of the instrument is permitted at all.**

733. **THE SEVENTY-FIVE PER CENT CEILING IS NOT CROSSED ON EITHER BASIS AND THE OPENER COUNT IS ZERO.** Batch 0004 is **Basis A 304 paragraphs and 0 openers at 0.0 per cent and Basis B 270 and 0 at 0.0 per cent**, against Volume 15's five blocks at 83.4, 91.1, 89.6, 80.6 and 56.4 on Basis B, and **Basis B for this block has FOUR named exclusions and not five, because this block prints no System panel, being the ten datelines, the four dialogue paragraphs, the ten cost codas and the ten closing money columns, and 304 less ten less four less ten less ten is 270.** The opener run is every paragraph beginning No, Not, Never, None, Neither, Nobody, Nothing or No one. `about` is **22.85 to 26.71** per 1,000, floor at `chapter-0782.md` and ceiling at `chapter-0786.md`, **and no page is at or above 27.00.** The thank family is **thirty** uses and every one of the thirty is a negation or a plain statement that a person was not thanked. **The duplicate-paragraph count over all seven hundred and ninety files is thirty-six groups and thirty-seven surplus occurrences and THIS BLOCK ADDS ZERO OF BOTH**, the same instrument over the seven hundred and eighty prior files giving the same thirty-six and thirty-seven, which is the check that the zero is a zero.

734. **THE THING A SUCCESSOR MUST NOT LEARN FROM THIS BLOCK, AND IT IS THE WARNING 719 AND 1722 CARRY FOR A THIRD TIME.** **THE FOUR LINES ON THE STONE SILL ARE STILL NOT WRITING AND A FIGURE IN ONE WOMAN'S HAND ON THE BACK OF A DAY-BOOK IS NOT A CURE, AND A SENTENCE ON A FOLDED SHEET IN A HAND NOBODY CLAIMED IS NOT A CURE EITHER.** A closed path is not destroyed and that is not good news. The people at the end of the shut road are alive and they would like the road to stay shut and that is not a rescue. **THE TUESDAY CAME AND SHUT NOTHING AND THAT IS NOT A VICTORY AND IS NOT THE END OF THE MATTER, and the roll is still on the board with nine empty lines on it and a second copy over it, and being paid is not the same as being finished.** Being the man who went and looked is a job and not an honour and nobody was told so and nobody wrote it down, and the practice is in one hand on a leaf no clerk will look at and on no other paper at all. **A SUCCESSOR MAY NOT RESOLVE ANY OF IT AND MAY NOT TURN IT INTO A HOPE OR A PLAN OR A THING ANYBODY IS TOLD HE DID WELL BY, AND MAY NOT READ THE FOLDED SHEET AS THE WOMAN'S.**

## Added by the review repair pass over Volume 16 Batch 0004, items 736 to 739, and the full record is at `state/continuity.md` 1759 to 1763

736. **THE THREE FIGURES A SUCCESSOR MUST RECOUNT AFTER THIS REPAIR PASS, AND THE TWO THAT ARE RIGHT, AND THE ONE THAT IS NOW INSTRUMENTED.** **The manuscript total is 2,298,318 over seven hundred and ninety chapters, the Volume 16 total is 153,667 over forty files, and the Batch 0004 total is 40,517 over ten files, and the roll-up now foots: 1,977,530 + 167,121 + 38,524 + 34,211 + 40,415 + 40,517 = 2,298,318, and the column at `state/current.md` no longer claims it foots when it does not.** Volumes 01 to 14 at **1,977,530** and Volume 14 alone at **172,432** may still be inherited. **The `about` band is 22.80 to 26.84 per 1,000 on a case-sensitive count of the lowercase token `about`, floor at `chapter-0781.md` and ceiling at `chapter-0786.md`, and on a case-insensitive count the same ten files read 23.29 to 27.02 with the ceiling at `chapter-0787.md`, so A SUCCESSOR WHO QUOTES A BAND MUST SAY WHICH COUNT IT USED, and the case-insensitive count is the safer one to write to.** And **the money collision set is now stated with an instrument that returns the same number twice: all 284 values in the band sixty to three hundred and forty-three are printed and none is free, over the seven hundred and eighty files that stood before Batch 0004 and over all seven hundred and ninety on disk, and nothing under sixty is free either.**

737. **THE FIGURE THAT WAS WRONG AND THE ONE THAT WAS NOT, BECAUSE A SUCCESSOR WILL MEET BOTH AGAIN AND BOTH LOOK RIGHT.** **The twelve free values published for this block, being 212, 218, 230, 254, 266, 273, 281, 308, 314, 321, 326 and 329, are withdrawn, because five of them are demonstrably printed in the manuscript and the instrument behind them omitted the *a hundred and …* spelling this manuscript does print.** **The conclusion that the usable free set is zero was right and stands, and it is right on the complete instrument for a stronger reason than the one printed, being that there is no free value in the band at all.** **THE DANGER IS NAMED SO THAT IT IS NOT REPEATED: run without the *a hundred* spelling the instrument returns two free values, 166 and 266, and 166 has an over-figure of 22 which is not one of the barred six, so on that incomplete instrument a successor would find a usable value and would print a total that every file before it has already printed.** And **the claim that the only free value under sixty is fifty-two is withdrawn too, fifty-two being printed in one hundred and ten files of the seven hundred and ninety and in one hundred and twenty of the eight hundred and NOT IN THE ONE HUNDRED AND EIGHTEEN THIS ITEM AND THREE OTHERS CARRIED, which no scope returns, and nothing at all under sixty is free.**

738. **THE FINDING THAT WAS CHECKED AND REJECTED, AND IT IS THE ONE A SUCCESSOR WILL MEET AGAIN AND IT LOOKS TRUE.** **A review of this block reported `Fallowmont` for `Fallowmonth` forty-nine times in the ten files, and there is no `Fallowmont` in any of the seven hundred and ninety files, because the string the scan matched is the first ten letters of the correct spelling.** The repair pass made that mistake on the page, wrote Fallowmonthh into five files, and undid it, and the count of forty-nine is exactly the naive prefix count: 15, 11, 11, 2, 1, 2, 1, 1, 1 and 3 across the ten files. **A spelling finding has to be checked against a whole word and not a prefix, and a repair that is about to run over a corpus the size of this one has to run its own check once before it edits anything.**

739. **WHAT THE REPAIR PASS OVER BATCH 0004 DID NOT TOUCH, AND WHAT IT LEFT FOR SOME LATER PASS, AND THE ONE PROHIBITION IT WITHDREW.** **IT DID NOT TOUCH** the thirty earlier pages of Volume 16, which carry the stock closing clause about twelve free values and about fifty-two being the only free value under sixty, **and it left them recorded rather than repaired because they belong to Batches 0001 to 0003, because the clause is one shared stock paragraph rather than ten private ones, and because repairing it once across all forty pages at a time is a different job from repairing a block. `outline/volumes/volume-15-close.md` carries the same withdrawn set of twelve, and that file is a closed volume's record and was not edited.** **IT WITHDREW ONE PROHIBITION:** the clause forbidding a Volume 16 close prompt is out of `state/current.md`, out of item 735 above and out of `state/continuity.md` 1745, **because Batch 0005 is the fifth of five blocks, its own prompt section nine tells it to create exactly one `workspace/volume-16/close/PROMPT.md` for `outline/volumes/volume-16-close.md`, and `AGENTS.md` and `PHASE_SYSTEM.md` both put a close prompt where a close prompt goes, and a prohibition in the state file that contradicts the prompt the same phase is told to obey is the thing that was wrong and not the prompt.** **AND IT ADDED NO PROHIBITION OF ITS OWN, spent no name, opened no route, and moved no beat.** The prose repairs and the six classes of them are at `state/continuity.md` 1760, the money instrument at 1761, the moved and unmoved figures at 1762, and what the pass did not do at 1763.

740. **WHAT BATCH 0005 WAS FOR, IN ONE SENTENCE, AND IT IS THE VOLUME'S CLIMAX AND RESOLUTION IN THE SAME SEVENTY-TWO DAYS.** Nine people held nine things for eleven mornings by saying a number out loud twice at the fourth hour with the man of thirty-two not one of the two, a stair about nine hundred yards long fell to people with a Crown's warrant while he was four hours off standing at the ninth of nine stones and did not know for twelve days, a third copy of the roll arrived proposing a man by his trade and was signed at the foot under a name nobody read out loud, a man of fifty-one who keeps an inn was named, a man of thirty-two said forty-two words at a trestle table in front of about four hundred people about himself and about nine of them stopped, and the volume ended six days before Hearthmonth on a floor in the dark with a road being held by somebody who is not him. **THE FULL TEXT IS `state/continuity.md` 1764 TO 1778.**
